# ZyperOS design

This is the design system for everything ZyperOS puts on a screen: the shelf,
the menus, MP4, the keyboard, the volume bar over a game or a port, the
notices Diatom shows, the loading screen, the tutorial, and the sounds they
make. A screen that is not described here is described by the nearest thing
that is, and then it is added here.

Every value in this document has a name, and the code uses the name. The
tables marked *generated* are written by `tools/designdef.py`, which is the
one place a value is typed; `make design` writes them here, into
`src/design.h` for the launcher, Diatom's overlay and portkeys, and into
`docs/design/mock/tokens.js` for the mock kit. `make check` fails when any of
those disagree, and when a text color falls under the contrast it is owed.
To change a value, change `tools/designdef.py`, run `make design`, and say
why in the commit.

The choices here were made by me on 2026-11-04 in the design lab
(`docs/design/mock/`), from options drawn on live screens: two themes,
**Beard** (light, the default) and **Salt & Pepper** (dark); focus as an
accent fill; Lexend; soft corners; snappy motion; Zyper as a companion; soft
interface sounds; and a full reward in the tutorial. Where this document
settles something the lab did not ask about, it says so and gives the reason.

---

## 1. Principles

1. **The games are the content; the system is the frame.** Color, light and
   motion belong first to the shelf of covers. The chrome around them is
   quiet greys and one accent, so a cover never competes with a button.
2. **Every press is answered in the next frame.** A move moves, a press
   presses, a dead row says why it is dead. Nothing waits in silence, and a
   press nobody can act on still gets a sound and a reason.
3. **One meaning per color.** Lime is ZyperOS: the focus, the chrome that is
   the system's, success. A system's color is that system's. Warm is light,
   pink is love, red-orange is a warning. A color is never borrowed for
   decoration.
4. **Motion says where something came from and where it went.** It is
   short, it starts from wherever the thing is now, a new press redirects it
   rather than queuing behind it, and it never makes anyone wait for it.
5. **Built for a 640x480 panel at arm's length.** Everything is designed in
   the launcher's 1024x768 space and shown at 0.625. Text is never smaller
   than 37 units (23 panel pixels) and always meets its contrast; marks meet
   theirs.
6. **Say it plainly.** Short words, the button's own name, the state in the
   value column rather than a row that vanishes (src/sys_menu.c says why).
7. **Zyper is the warmth, and he is rationed.** He is at the moments that
   matter and in the corner of the menus, never in the way of a game.
8. **Nothing is cut off.** Truncation destroys information (src/ui.h); text
   that does not fit scrolls.

---

## 2. Canvas

| | |
|---|---|
| Design space | 1024 x 768 units, landscape (`ZYPEROS_SCREEN_W/H`) |
| GKD Pixel 2 panel | 640 x 480 shown, 0.625 px per unit; the panel is 480x640 portrait mounted turned, and the picture is turned onto it (`plat_save_screen`, plastron's SDL patch) |
| TrimUI Brick | 1024 x 768, 1 px per unit |
| Grid | 8 units, which is exactly 5 panel pixels; 4 units for fine steps |
| Screen inset | 24 units from every edge for text and marks |

Programs that do not draw in the design space - Diatom's overlay, portkeys -
draw at 0.625 directly, from the same tokens.

---

## 3. Color

### 3.1 Themes

**Beard** is the light theme and the default for a new device: the white of
Zyper's beard as the ground, charcoal text, lime as a fill with dark text on
it. **Salt & Pepper** is the dark theme: Zyper's coat as the whole UI, the
charcoal of his nose and eyes as the ground (the same 17,19,16 the power-off
screen has always cleared to, so the two grounds of ZyperOS become one), the
dark cap for surfaces, beard white for text.

MENU > Appearance chooses: **Light**, **Dark**, or **Auto**, which is Dark
in Night Mode's Night Hours by the handheld's clock (21:00 to 7:00 as they
ship) and Light the rest of the day (11.42). *(The setting's name is this
document's call; Auto's hours are the Night Hours since 1.1.0.)* A shelf shows its games as a row of covers, a grid of them
(11.47) or a list of names (11.39), each in one style.

### 3.2 Roles

Each role has one value per theme. Code asks for the role, never the value.

- **ground** clears the screen. **surface** is a panel, a card's slab, the
  bar's body. **raised** is a key, a chip, a pressed button. **edge** is the
  3-unit line around a surface. **track** is the unfilled part of a bar or
  rail.
- **text**, **text-soft**, **text-dim** are the three steps of text, as
  before: a list is read at all three at once, so the gaps rank the rows
  (src/ui.h). Unselected live rows are **text-soft**, not dimmed to
  **text-dim**: selection is said by the focus fill, not by dimming the rest.
- **accent** is lime, as a *fill*: the focused row, the selected key, the
  tutorial's check. Text on it is **on-accent**.
- **accent-ink** is lime as a *mark* on the ground: the bar's segments for
  volume, an earned achievement, the progress strip. In Beard it is a darker
  lime, because the fill's lime on a light ground is only 1.3:1.
- **light** is brightness, **favorite** the heart, **warning** low battery
  and errors.
- **store-games**, **store-saves** and **store-art** are Storage's bar and
  its legend's dots (11.32), and nothing else.

### 3.3 Accents

| Accent | Is | Never |
|---|---|---|
| lime (**accent**, **accent-ink**) | ZyperOS: focus, the system's chrome, volume, success, earned, MP4 | a system's color, decoration |
| a system's color (systems.cfg) | its shelf's marks and lift and missing-cover fallback | focus, any menu (its heading rule, its frame, its light), source sprite colors, unrelated screens |
| **light** | brightness | anything else |
| **favorite** | the heart | anything else |
| **warning** | low battery, an error a row reports | emphasis |

*This document's calls, from the audit (2026-11-04):* MP4 is no longer its
own green (0x9CD345 sat a few degrees from Zyper's lime and the two could not
be told apart); it uses lime like the rest of the system. Volume is lime in
both themes (Salt & Pepper is one-accent), and an earned achievement is lime
where it was cyan. Cyan leaves the system.

A system's color on the Beard ground is mixed a quarter of the way toward
**text** before it is used as a mark (a rail), so a yellow
like Game Boy Color's still reads on a light ground. Source console sprites
and game covers are never recolored for a theme. Their dark outlines remain
opaque. Focus uses Highlight's mark (3.5), size and text, not a tint of the art.

### 3.4 Scrims

| Scrim | Dark | Light | Under |
|---|---|---|---|
| **scrim.menu** | ink at 47% | ink at 28% | every menu, keyboard and one-line message over the shelf |
| **scrim.game** | ink at 73% | ink at 73% | the paused game under the in-game menu, save slots, achievements |

Two levels, not four. The game keeps the heavy one in both themes because a
game is content, not part of the theme. *(The audit found 120, 150 and 185
for these; the keyboard's 150 becomes **scrim.menu**, and the in-game menu,
which used the shelf's 120 over a paused game, gets **scrim.game** like the
slots beside it.)* "Could not start" and "Connect to Wi-Fi first." panels, which
drew with no scrim at all, get **scrim.menu**.

### 3.5 Your look

MENU > Display > Theme (11.42) changes six things, and nothing else: the
**accent**, one of ten, the same in both themes; a **background tone** for
each theme; the **folders' color**, each system's own or one of the ten
accents for every folder; **corners**, rounded or square; **Highlight**, how
the item in focus is marked on the home and the shelves; and **Zyper**, on
or off. Each starts as the themes ship: lime, Warm (Beard's ground) and
Black (Salt & Pepper's), System Colors, rounded, Bar, and Zyper on. *(My picks
for the first version, 2026-11-12; the folders' color in 1.0.6 rc.3:
"can we customize them in theme to be per system or a custom color for
all?")*

<!-- generated:look -->

| Accent | Fill | Tone | Beard (light) | Salt & Pepper (dark) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Lime** (as it ships) | `#bef23c` | **1** | Warm `#e6e4da` | Black `#111310` |
| **Green** | `#5ee08a` | **2** | Neutral `#e3e3e1` | Deep Blue `#0e1526` |
| **Teal** | `#3fd4c0` | **3** | Cool `#dde2e7` | Dark Grey `#1e2120` |
| **Blue** | `#74b0ff` | | | |
| **Purple** | `#b59bff` | | | |
| **Pink** | `#ff94c8` | | | |
| **Red** | `#ff7a70` | | | |
| **Orange** | `#ffa04d` | | | |
| **Yellow** | `#ffd84d` | | | |
| **Silver** | `#d3d5cf` | | | |
<!-- /generated:look -->

- The accent is the **accent** role, the focus fill, with its text, the
  **on-accent**, ink or the beard's white, whichever has more contrast on it.
  **accent-ink** is the accent walked darker in Beard (or lighter in Salt &
  Pepper) in OKLab until it is 4.5:1 on **ground** and **surface** and 3:1
  on **track**, as lime's own was chosen.
- A tone is the **ground**. The surfaces (**surface**, **raised**, **edge**,
  **track**) keep their lightness steps from the theme's own ground and take
  the tone's tint, **surface** at seven tenths of it; the three text roles
  keep their lightness, lean a third of the way to the tint, and are walked
  until each is 4.5:1 on **ground** and **surface** again; **light**,
  **favorite** and **warning** keep their own colors, walked only if a tone
  would take them under their contrast. src/look.c works the theme out once,
  when a setting changes, and every screen draws from it as from the tokens;
  the first accent and the first tones give back the tokens exactly.
  tools/look-check.c holds every combination, ten accents by three tones in
  each theme, to 3.2's contrasts.
- **Folder Color** is the color a folder is drawn in where a system's color
  was (look_folder_rgb): the shelf's folders of games, their cards and the
  list's small folders (11.39), and the consoles' folders' tiles on the File
  Transfer page. **System Colors** gives each its system's own, as before
  the row; an accent gives every folder that accent's fill, a system with no
  color too. A system's own marks, its lift, its light and a game's card keep
  the system's color whatever the row says. It is no theme of its own
  (`theme_at` is the tokens' while the colors are as they ship), but it is
  a look of its own: `look_id` counts it, so a card drawn in another folder
  color is drawn again.
- **Square** takes every radius under a pill's to 0: panels, the focus fill,
  keys, chips, Highlight's ring. A pill stays a pill (the bar, a button
  glyph, the bar under a tab, Highlight's bar), a circle a circle, and covers
  keep their own corners.
- **Highlight** (src/ds/focus.h; my picks in the focus lab, 2026-11-19: "can
  we experiment with custom active bar in the theme settings? so users can
  pick none, bar, border, and maybe other options?", then "the focuses on
  grid mode look bad. We need something more polished, more professional.
  attention to details") marks the item in focus on the home (Spotlight,
  Gallery, the Grid) and the shelves (the Covers row, the Grid, the List)
  one of four ways. Menus, option lists, the keyboard and the in-game menu
  keep their fill whatever it says.
  - **Bar**, as it ships: the item grows a little on a soft lift, and a
    pill of **accent-ink**, focus-bar-w x focus-bar-h, sits focus-bar-under
    under it (a system's under its name); on a row, the fill as always. On
    the Covers row it gives way to the video snap's wait bar while that
    fills, in the same place: both mark the cover. *(Until 1.1.0 the Covers
    row's sat 16 lower, under the wait bar's place, and a name long enough
    to reach the screen's middle ran into it.)*
  - **Border**: a ring of **accent-ink** round it, focus-ring wide past a
    gap of focus-gap of ground, its corners the item's own plus the gap and
    the ring, so the curves are parallel; on the home Grid round a
    **surface** card under the system and its name; on a row, inside it.
  - **Lift**: it grows more and rises on a deeper lift; a cover gets one
    light across it as it lands (the **sheen** token, once).
  - **None**: no mark; it grows, and on a row its name stands in a little.
  In every one the items out of focus step back, drawn a fifth of the way
  toward the ground (a system's still at 70%); names and their words keep
  their contrast. Every mark sits on whole panel pixels (src/focusgeo.h):
  the Pixel 2 shows the 1024 x 768 picture through linear filtering at
  0.625, so a mark is laid out in its pixels - the ring and the gap 2 each,
  3 and 3 on the Brick - and each pixel edge is drawn at the unit that lands
  cleanest, and the focused item grows to where its edges and its marks'
  land exact; check-focusgeo holds them to the filter. Every item has a 1-pixel
  hairline inside its edge (ink at 16%, white at 10% on Salt & Pepper), so
  very light art on Beard and very dark art on Salt & Pepper keep an edge.
  The grow, the lift and the name's color move with each item's weight in
  the focus's glide, and the mark glides from where it is, all in
  **ms.quick** on **out**: one motion, nothing drawn twice. It costs no
  frame: each mark is a few rects, the lift is the textures the lifts
  already have, tinted, and a system's soft shadow is made once as its still
  arrives.
- **Zyper off** takes him out of the menus' corner, the song pop-up (11.22),
  the achievement notice (11.7), the Flash Drive pages and the Scraper page (the heading then
  starts where the tiles do). He stays where he is the moment itself:
  the boot, the loading loop, the tutorial, Getting hot, the battery and the
  charging screens.
- The bar over a game and over a port follows the look too: the launcher
  writes its colors into the theme file beside the theme's word (themefile.h).
- Pink, red, orange and yellow sit near **favorite**, **warning** and
  **light**. Those keep their own colors, so the heart, a warning and the
  brightness bar are still themselves.

---

## 4. Type

Lexend, the whole UI, in two weights: **Regular** for everything, and
**SemiBold** for the name under the cursor (the **title** role). Lexend was
drawn for reading speed; at the same size as the Josefin Sans it replaces it
sets the launcher's words about 8% wider (4 to 19% over 54 of its strings,
measured 2026-11-04) and its x-height is a quarter taller, so it reads larger
at the panel's 23 pixels.

The two weights are two files in one folder, `menu.ttf` (Regular) and
`title.ttf` (SemiBold). Regular keeps the name it always had, so plastron
picks it up without a change; SemiBold is found beside it, and Wi-Fi File Transfer's
page loads both. `menu.ttf` serves the UI, the in-game menu, the titles the
launcher draws into the cards it generates (src/ui.c) and tools/gensingles.py;
`title.ttf` serves the title role, Wi-Fi File Transfer's bold and the names on
the home and games screens. A card without `title.ttf` draws the
title in Regular, and the launcher says so once on stderr. Both files are
under the SIL Open Font License (`res/fonts/OFL.txt`).

Lexend has no Chinese or Japanese. In **简体中文** and **日本語** (11.30)
every role is drawn in **Noto Sans SC** or **Noto Sans JP**, Regular for
all but the title and Bold for the title (their static cuts have no
SemiBold), `zh-menu.otf`, `zh-title.otf`, `ja-menu.otf` and `ja-title.otf`
beside `menu.ttf`: tools/cjkfont.py's cuts to the first level of GB 2312 or
of JIS X 0208's kanji, the kana, and every character ZyperOS writes, 700 to
830 KB a weight (`res/fonts/OFL-NotoSansSC.txt`, `OFL-NotoSansJP.txt`). Two
faces, since the two write some of the same characters differently. A set
opens the first time its language or text is drawn and stays open; nothing
else changes. A line with characters its face lacks is drawn in the first of
its role's faces that has them all (the set that is on, then Japanese's for
kana, Chinese's otherwise), placed by the capitals of the face it was asked
in (src/ui.c, `face_for`), so 简体中文 and 日本語 read in an English menu and
a game named in either is not boxes. The button letters
and the cards the launcher draws stay Lexend in every language: they are
marks. Measured on an M3 against Lexend (2026-11-07): 1 ms to open the eight
sizes, 0.5 MB more memory, and a new line of Chinese about six times
Lexend's time the first time it is drawn, then cached like any line.

<!-- generated:type -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Role | Size | Weight | For |
|---|---|---|---|
| **title** | 60 (38 px on the Pixel 2) | SemiBold | the name under the cursor; screen titles |
| **label** | 55 (34 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | menu headings, the keyboard's title, a notice's body |
| **menu** | 49 (31 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | menu rows, keys, a game's details |
| **meta** | 37 (23 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | menu captions and secondary text on detailed screens |
| **chrome** | 24 (15 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | home and games headers, status and footer legends |
| **support** | 28 (18 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | home counts, compact game metadata, and the action beside its button glyph (action 27 and support 29 until 1.0.6) |
| **game-title** | 37 (23 px on the Pixel 2) | SemiBold | the full game name below its cover row: meta's size, title's weight; its lines 40 apart (38 and 44 until 1.0.6) |
| **card** | 71 (44 px on the Pixel 2) | Regular | the title drawn into a generated card (512 px wide) |
| **clock** | 120 (75 px on the Pixel 2) | SemiBold | MP4's Classic look: the time played, large; digits only, so Lexend in every language |
<!-- /generated:type -->

- Text is placed by its capitals, not its em box: centered on them in a row,
  a key or a band (`ui_cap_y`), and a line given the top of its line has them
  two fifths of an em down (`ui_line_y`). A role's line is its size, an em
  (`ui_font_line`), not the face's line skip: Lexend's ascent is a whole em,
  a quarter of it room for accents, and a menu row of 1.5 skips would be 93
  where the mock's is 77.
- Digits that change while on screen are tabular (`ui_text_tabular`).
- **Title Case** for headings, menu rows and named choices ("Audio Output",
  "Light"), a switch's "On" and "Off" among them (Developer Mode, Wi-Fi
  and Bluetooth, Swap Screens, 2026-11-12: "Off/On in the dev mode row in
  settings should be capitalized"). **A capital first** for status words in
  the value column ("Not connected", "Needs Wi-Fi", "Never"), as every value
  there begins (2026-11-17: "make sure they are all capitalized"; a name keeps
  its own case, a number begins as it begins). **Sentence case** for lines and
  messages ("Press A to open the game you are on.").
- No italics, no letter spacing, no all-caps except button names, which are
  written as printed on the handheld: A, B, X, Y, L1, R1, L2, R2, START,
  SELECT, MENU, VOL+, VOL-, POWER.

---

## 5. Space and layout

- Positions and sizes are multiples of 8 units where a value is new; the
  values below that are not (the rail's 23) are kept for the reason in the
  code beside them.
- The menu panel is laid out from two tokens on the grid, **menu-row** 72 and
  **menu-pad** 56 (45 and 35 whole pixels on the Pixel 2; 1.5 menu lines and
  0.75 of that until 1.0.6), and the rest follows them (label-to-value gap a
  row, heading `line(label) + pad`). The keyboard's padding is the same
  token.
- The standard panel is 800 units wide, centered. Content is never wider than
  the screen less two insets and two paddings.
- The rail is the bar's 8 units, 23 from the bottom (or left) edge, inset 90
  at both ends; its marker is at least 18.

<!-- generated:space -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Token | Units | For |
|---|---|---|
| **grid** | 8 | the step for anything new; 5 panel pixels |
| **inset** | 24 | text and marks from every screen edge |
| **panel-w** | 800 | the standard menu panel's width |
| **bar** | 8 | the rail, a progress bar: on the grid (6 until 1.0.6) |
| **rail-inset** | 23 | the rail from the bottom edge |
| **rail-ends** | 90 | the rail's inset at both ends |
| **rail-min** | 18 | the rail marker's least length |
| **level-mark** | 42 | the volume and brightness bar's mark: its middle, in from the pill's left end |
| **boost-gap** | 12 | the bar's gap at the top, its thin mark in the middle, before the boost cells |
| **boost-charge-w** | 36 | the bar's charging cell before the boost opens, wide... |
| **boost-charge-h** | 30 | ...and tall: big, so its fill is easy to see |
<!-- /generated:space -->

### The system (round 3)

One way every screen is spaced, so every screen feels like one launcher, and
one way it is laid out, so a longer language or a second row of keys moves
the layout instead of landing on a word.

- **The grid.** 8 units, exactly 5 pixels on the Pixel 2, for every position
  and every distance between parts; 4 and 12 only inside a component (a
  glyph to its word). On the Brick and the Brick Pro every unit is a whole
  pixel anyway. Strokes (a rule, a frame, a ring) are hairlines and keep
  their widths.
- **The scale.** 8, 16, 24, 32, 40, 48, 64.
- **The roles** a screen keeps everywhere, in `src/ds/layout.h`: **margin**
  64 (content from the sides), **header** 72 (the top bar's band; content
  starts a **section**, 24, under it), **related** 8 (things that go
  together), **foot-inset** 32 (the keys' foot from the screen's: one row's
  middle at 720), **foot-gap** 24 (content over the keys keeps at least
  this), **caption-inset** 40, **list-row** 72, and each type's line as a
  box (**line-title** 72 down to **line-chrome** 32).
- **Corners nest.** A shape inside another keeps their corners concentric:
  inner radius = outer radius - inset, as a row's fill in a panel does.

**The layout engine** (`src/ds/flex.h`). A screen builds a small tree of
boxes each frame - columns and rows with padding, gaps, alignment, grow and
shrink, a row that wraps, a kept aspect (a leaf's: a picture, a cover),
sizes in whole steps (a list's rows) - its leaves measured with the real
font and line breaks of the language that is on, solves it, and draws each
part in its box. Integer units, no allocation. When something needs more
room, the rules give it: a text wraps, the part that grows gives room back,
a single line ellipsizes, and nothing is cut or overlaps (the keys check,
13). `check-flex` holds its rules, a thousand random trees that stay in
their parents the same way every time, and its time (a screen's 14 boxes in
a third of a microsecond on a Mac).

Screens move onto the engine and these roles one at a time, each first with
its pixels unchanged and then with its spacing on a sheet (section 14).
`check-layout-ratchet` (`tools/layout-ratchet.py`) holds the way to one
direction: each source's typed layout numbers (a layout `#define` set to a
number, words at a typed height, a rectangle typed in place) are held to
`tools/layout-ratchet.txt`, which only goes down. A file over its line
fails: its new places come from the engine or a token, or, where a number is
the screen's own with a reason beside it, its line is raised in the same
change, where the review sees it. A file under its line fails until the line
is lowered, so a number taken out cannot come back unseen.

### Layout

Where the launcher's own parts sit: a page's frame (Flash Drive, File
Transfer's page, the Scraper), a panel of tiles or a list, the marks and the
button hints. `tools/designdef.py` writes them into `src/ds/layout.h` as
`DS_PAGE_*`, `DS_TILE_*`, `DS_LIST_*`, `DS_BADGE*`, `DS_MARK_*` and
`DS_HINT*`, beside `src/design.h` (they are the launcher's alone; Diatom and
portkeys carry `design.h`). A screen takes its places from here; a number of
its own says why beside it.

| **page-zyper-y** | 168 | his middle's height |
| **page-zyper-h** | 124 | his height there |
| **page-head-x** | 208 | the heading's left beside him (with him off: the inset) |
| **page-head-y** | 168 | a heading on one line: its middle |
| **page-head-step** | 34 | a heading on two lines: each line this far above and under head-y |
| **page-title-y** | 150 | a heading with a smaller line under it: the heading's middle |
| **page-sub-y** | 208 | that smaller line's middle |

## 6. Shape

**Soft**: rounded enough to be friendly, not so round that a panel reads as a
pill. A pill is only ever a pill: the bar, button glyphs, chips.

<!-- generated:shape -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Token | Units | For |
|---|---|---|
| **radius-card** | 22 | a cover, a generated card |
| **radius-panel** | 28 | a menu panel, the keyboard, a notice, the tutorial stage |
| **radius-row** | 14 | the focus fill on a row |
| **radius-key** | 12 | a keyboard key, the text field |
| **radius-chip** | 8 | a chip, a hint, a save slot's dot |
| **frame** | 3 | the edge around a surface |
| **rule** | 2 | a heading's rule, the keyboard field's edge |
| **caret** | 3 | the keyboard's caret |
| **ring** | 4 | a button glyph's ring |
| **radius-bar** | 4 | MP4's bars (Player Look); a bar's cap is a pill |
<!-- /generated:shape -->

- A panel's frame is **edge**, 3 units, in both themes. *(The 12-unit cyan
  frame goes: with lime as the focus fill, a lime frame around it doubled the
  accent, and a grey frame lets the focused row be the brightest thing on
  the panel. A system's menu says which system it is in its heading; its
  heading rule is **edge** and its light in Salt & Pepper the neutral one,
  as every menu's. Tarik's for 1.0.6 rc.2: "why the line below the header
  in the menu varies with the system? can we not do that?", and the light
  the same for all systems too, "yeah".)*
- Covers keep their own corners (square for album covers, as now).

---

## 7. Elevation

Salt & Pepper lifts with light: the additive glow under a focused card, in
the system's color, as the launcher has always done (on the home and the
shelves Highlight's lift is a light in the accent and an ink-black shadow
under it, and in Beard a shadow leaning a third of the way to the accent's
ink: `ui_lift_focus`, 3.5); a menu panel's light
is the neutral one on every menu, a system's too (1.0.6 rc.2). Beard lifts with
shadow: light on a light ground is invisible, so the same places get a soft
shadow of ink instead, neutral, never tinted. In Beard the system's color is
the wash along the bottom of the shelf (a normal blend, not additive), the
card's band and the rail.

<!-- generated:elevation -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Token | Salt & Pepper glow (alpha / spread) | Beard shadow (y / blur / opacity) | For |
|---|---|---|---|
| **focus** | 105 / 2.3 | 18 / 48 / 22% | a card on the shelf |
| **focus-small** | 85 / 1.5 | 8 / 20 / 18% | a thumbnail, MP4's cover |
| **panel** | 60 / 1.5 | 24 / 64 / 18% | a menu panel, the keyboard |
| **wash** | 34 / 1.7 | the system's color at 16%, normal blend | the system's light along the bottom of the shelf |
| **bar** | shadow 4 / 8 / 40% | 4 / 8 / 18% | the volume and brightness bar |
<!-- /generated:elevation -->

- *(The audit found four focus glows, 110/2.4, 100/2.3, 90/1.7 and 85/1.35,
  with no reason given for the differences. They become two: **focus** for a
  card on the shelf, **focus.small** for a thumbnail or a cover in MP4.)*
- Reflections under covers stay, at 90/255, fading to nothing; in Beard they
  fade toward the ground. A reflection ends 8 units above the line of text
  under its row, the shelf's count, so the words are never read through it:
  a card whose foot reaches that floor has no reflection.

---

## 8. Motion

**Snappy.** Fast and sharp, like hardware: things arrive on a steep ease-out
and are there before the thumb is off the button. Nothing overshoots in the
interface. Zyper alone moves like a character (8.3).

### 8.1 Curves

| Curve | Formula | For |
|---|---|---|
| **out** | `1 - (1-t)^5` (quintic ease-out) | everything that arrives or moves: a card step, a panel opening, the focus fill, the bar coming in, a check appearing |
| **in** | `t^3` (cubic ease-in) | things leaving: a panel closing, the launch zoom |
| **turn** | `t^2 (3 - 2t)` (smoothstep) | the tutorial details panel |
| **fade** | linear | opacity of something going away; marquees |
| **follow** | `1 - e^(-dt/tau)` | tracking a moving target every frame: the background tint, the scrolling window |

*(The audit found cubic ease-out written four times and a quadratic ease-in
beside a cubic one. One function each, in `src/design.h`.)*

### 8.2 Durations

<!-- generated:motion -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Token | ms | Curve | For |
|---|---|---|---|
| **press** | 80 | out | a button glyph pressing in, a key |
| **quick** | 120 | out | the focus fill between rows, the bar coming in, a check |
| **move** | 160 | out | a card step, a panel opening |
| **exit** | 140 | in | a panel closing |
| **turn** | 300 | turn | the tutorial details panel |
| **spin** | 900 | turn | the 3D handheld turning from its front to its back or back again, where the keys lit are on both |
| **hold-spin** | 1800 | - | the 3D handheld held on its front or its back before it turns to the other |
| **launch** | 190 | in | a card zooming into its game |
| **fade-out** | 200 | fade | the bar, a notice going away |
| **hold-bar** | 1500 | - | the bar after the last press |
| **hold-short** | 1600 | - | a one-line message |
| **hold-long** | 2200 | - | a two-line message |
| **zyper-hop** | 280 | out | Zyper's hop |
| **zyper-tilt** | 160 | out | Zyper's head cocking |
| **zyper-tongue** | 160 | out | Zyper's tongue |
| **zyper-breathe** | 2400 | - | Zyper's idle bob, one loop |
| **zyper-pant** | 1000 | - | Zyper's pant, the tongue in and out three times |
| **zyper-blink** | 134 | turn | Zyper's blink: shut in 3/8 of it, held to half, open by the end |
| **zyper-blink-gap** | 3000 | - | the least time between Zyper's blinks at rest; the most is twice it |
| **hold-zyper-open** | 80 | - | a menu's first frame, before Zyper's opening hop |
| **hold-zyper-fail** | 860 | - | Zyper's cocked head after a failure, before he settles |
| **zyper-pop** | 360 | - | Zyper popping in by scale for the tutorial's hello, with his overshoot |
| **zyper-ears** | 420 | - | Zyper's ears swinging out and back as he says hello |
| **tour-reward** | 1100 | - | a tutorial step's reward, before the next |
| **tour-pulse** | 440 | - | the right glyph's two pulses after a press of the wrong button |
| **hold-unlock** | 2000 | - | MP4's lock: SELECT and START held together this long unlock it |
| **hold-power** | 1000 | - | POWER held this long turns the handheld off with Power Button on Hold, and starts the charging screen's handheld |
| **hold-boost** | 1000 | - | a level key held this long on the top opens the boost past it; the charging cell fills over it, linearly, and drains back the same way when let go early |
| **tap-gap** | 1000 | - | the most time between two presses on the top that count toward the three that open the boost; the charge drains after it |

| **bar-fall** | 140 | follow | MP4's bars falling to the sound: follow's tau, where a bar rises at once |
| **spin-up** | 360 | out | MP4's reels (Player Look) coming up to speed as the music plays |
| **spin-down** | 520 | out | ...and coming to a stop as it pauses; the sound stops at once |
| **rewind** | 700 | turn | MP4's cassette winding back to the start for the next song |
| **wind** | 180 | follow | MP4's cassette winding to a seek's place: follow's tau |
| **arm-glide** | 180 | follow | MP4's tonearm gliding to the song's place on the record: follow's tau |
| **hold-snap** | 1000 | - | a game focused on the shelf before its cover turns into its video snap |
| **hold-band** | 2000 | - | the Video view's band of covers and keys, after the last press on a game whose snap plays, before it goes down to its strip |
| **sheen** | 480 | turn | one light crossing a game's card with no art, once, as the cover row stops on it |
| **sharpen** | 200 | fade | a played game's card with no art turning from its blurred frame of play to the sharp one, as the cover row stops on it |
| **hold-low-battery** | 6000 | - | the low-battery start screen before the handheld powers off |
| **hold-heat** | 4000 | - | "Getting hot", on the home and over a game |
| **hold-charge-lit** | 10000 | - | the charging screen lit after the last button, before it goes dark |
<!-- /generated:motion -->

- **Every motion is interruptible.** A new press retargets from wherever
  the thing is now; nothing queues.
- **Every motion tells the redraw clock when it ends**, the way
  `plat_osd_until` does, so a screen that redraws only on change still draws
  the last frame.
- **A still screen draws nothing.** The shelf, MENU's panels and USB File
  Transfer draw a frame when what they show changes, when the redraw clock
  comes due, or once a second (src/pace.h); a press, a held button, a panel
  opening or closing, the volume bar and files on their way draw every pass,
  so saving power never costs a frame of motion. Zyper's breath and blinks
  draw every frame they change (2026-11-10: File Transfer left open drew at
  60 Hz and ran the Pixel 2's chip at 51 to 55 C; paced, see the PR's
  tables).
- **USB File Transfer left alone on the cable** goes off after Auto Off's
  time when nothing is moving, and only dims (the backlight's floor, or
  the player's rung if it is under the floor already) while files are on
  their way, so a transfer can still be
  watched. A press, a browser arriving or files starting light it fully;
  a press that lights it does nothing else, POWER's too. *(Tarik,
  2026-11-10.)*
- **Menus now open and close.** A panel opens in **move** (out): from 96% to
  full size and from clear to opaque, with its scrim fading in over the same
  time; it closes in **exit** (in), back the same way. The focus fill glides
  between rows in **quick** (out). *(New; menus snapped open before.)*
- **One panel at a time.** Going deeper (MENU > Wi-Fi > a network > the
  keyboard) the parent leaves in **exit** while the child opens in **move**,
  starting together, and the scrim stays as it is; going back is the same
  the other way. Only the first panel fades the scrim in, and only the last
  fades it out. *(This document's call: the launcher's menus are modal
  loops, each drawing the shelf, a scrim and one panel, and a stack of live
  panels would make every level wait on the ones under it.)*
- **One-line messages** ("Saved", "Could not start") hold for **hold.short**;
  two-line ones for **hold.long**. *(The audit found five hold times, 900 to
  2200 ms.)*

### 8.3 Zyper's motion

Zyper is drawn from triangles (src/zypermark.h) and has six moves, from the
boot animation and the loading loop; their times are the **zyper-** tokens of
8.2:

| Move | What | Time |
|---|---|---|
| hop | up 24 units and back, squashing 6% at the bottom | 280 ms |
| tilt | the head cocks 10 degrees, the high brow lifts | 160 ms |
| tongue | out or in | 160 ms |
| pant | the tongue 18% in and out, three times a second | 1 s |
| blink | the eyes shut and open again | 134 ms |
| breathe | at rest, a 2-unit bob | 2.4 s loop |

He may overshoot and settle; the interface around him does not. A move he
is already making is not started over: a hop under way runs on, and a pant
goes on to the end of its beat, so a held key that changes a setting every
90 ms does not drop him mid-air.

---

## 9. Sound

**Soft UI.** Short synthesized sounds, quiet, from the launcher only: never
over a game or a port. One exception: the sound of the press that starts or
resumes a game, A's accept or Continue's back, is let finish, at most about
100 ms over the game's first frames; cut, every launch's tick would be
clipped. They play through the system volume, so the volume keys and the
mute switch set them too.

They play on the speaker, the headphone jack and a USB-C DAC, through the
same shared output MP4 and Diatom use, in every screen the launcher draws,
the in-game menu over a paused game included. With a Bluetooth headset as
the output they are off: its A2DP stream is held by one player at a time,
and its fifth of a second of delay would put every tick after the press it
answers. *(This document's call.)* They follow Audio Channels: mono on one
side plays them on that side only.

There are three **voices** of the same six sounds, each sound a WAV that
`tools/gensounds.py` synthesizes from the tones below (`make sounds`): Wood,
soft mallets on wooden bars; Glass, pure high ticks; Bubble, drops that pop.
**MENU > UI Sounds**, under Audio Channels, picks Wood, Glass, Bubble or Off,
and a new device has **Wood**. The row plays each voice as it is chosen.
*(2026-11-04, in the sound lab, docs/design/mock/sound-lab.html:
Wood, with the voice a setting rather than one fixed set; the row with the
audio rows.)*

<!-- generated:sound -->
*Generated by tools/designdef.py.*

| Sound | For | Wood (default) | Glass | Bubble |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **move** | a step on a shelf or a list | sine 1098.83-1046.5 Hz 30 ms 0.055 at 0; sine 4186 Hz 6 ms 0.012 at 0 | sine 1900 Hz 25 ms 0.05 at 0 | sine 700-1150 Hz 28 ms 0.05 at 0 |
| **edge** | a step blocked at the end of a list | sine 823.2-784 Hz 38 ms 0.055 at 0; sine 3136 Hz 6 ms 0.01 at 0 | sine 1200 Hz 30 ms 0.05 at 0 | sine 600-480 Hz 34 ms 0.05 at 0 |
| **accept** | A, a menu opening | sine 1233.43-1174.7 Hz 55 ms 0.08 at 0; sine 2349.4 Hz 24 ms 0.02 at 0; sine 4698.8 Hz 6 ms 0.012 at 0 | triangle 1250 Hz 40 ms 0.09 at 0 | sine 800-1600 Hz 45 ms 0.08 at 0 |
| **back** | B, a menu closing | sine 924-880 Hz 60 ms 0.075 at 0; sine 1760 Hz 24 ms 0.018 at 0 | triangle 820 Hz 50 ms 0.08 at 0 | sine 1300-650 Hz 50 ms 0.07 at 0 |
| **deny** | a press nothing can act on | triangle 466.2 Hz 50 ms 0.08 at 0; triangle 440 Hz 60 ms 0.08 at 85 | square 330 Hz 40 ms 0.04 at 0; square 330 Hz 40 ms 0.04 at 70 | triangle 520-400 Hz 45 ms 0.07 at 0; triangle 520-400 Hz 45 ms 0.07 at 80 |
| **reward** | something good | sine 1098.83-1046.5 Hz 220 ms 0.08 at 0; sine 4186 Hz 8 ms 0.015 at 0; sine 1646.4-1568 Hz 380 ms 0.07 at 100; sine 6272 Hz 8 ms 0.012 at 100 | triangle 1318.5 Hz 160 ms 0.08 at 0; triangle 1975.5 Hz 260 ms 0.07 at 90 | sine 900-1318.5 Hz 160 ms 0.075 at 0; sine 1300-1975.5 Hz 280 ms 0.065 at 90 |
<!-- /generated:sound -->

- A step that moves plays **move**. A step that cannot, at the end of a list,
  plays **edge**, once a press: held against the end, the repeats are
  silent.
- A menu list does not wrap (11.3), so a step down from the last row or up
  from the first plays **edge**. A shelf or the home that wraps from the last
  item to the first plays **move**. A held wrap uses the normal held level. A
  blocked edge still plays **edge**.
- A held direction repeats every 90 ms after 300 ms. Its first step plays at
  full level and every repeat at half (**held**, 6 dB down), so the shelf is
  heard running and a long hold does not fill the room. The edge a hold comes
  to is news, and plays at full level. *(2026-11-04, in the sound lab,
  the held level.)*
- Left and right on a menu row step its value. A value that steps round
  (Appearance, Sort By, the in-game menu's Display) plays **move** on the
  press, before the change is made; one that stops at its ends (Auto Off)
  plays **move** when it changed and **edge** when it did not.
- A plays **accept**, and so does a menu opening (MENU, X's game page, the
  in-game menu); A on a row that does nothing with it (Auto Off, Sort By, a
  system's Display) plays **deny**. B plays **back**, and so does MENU
  closing a menu.
  A menu the launcher opens itself, or a game it starts with nobody
  pressing (a resume at boot), plays nothing.
- A press on a dead row, or of a button the screen has nothing for where it
  usually acts (X or Y on MP4's albums), plays **deny**, and the row says
  why.
- **reward** is for something good: a tutorial step, a favorite added.
  Taking a favorite away plays **back**.
- On the keyboard A types and plays **accept**, B deletes and plays
  **back**; the grid wraps at every edge, and its wrap plays **move**.

---

## 10. Zyper

**Companion.** Zyper is at the moments that matter, and in the corner of the
menus:

- **Boot**: the boot animation (tools/genboot.py).
- **Loading** a port: the bounce loop (tools/genloading.py).
- **The tutorial** (11.10): he says the welcome and the finish in his own
  bubble, comes up from the bottom edge for each step's reward and for a
  press of the wrong button, and in the power step plays the power-off on
  the stage, the corner left empty until its reward.
- **Power off**: the shutdown animation.
- **The File Transfer page's PIN** (11.15): over the heading, with the
  menus' breath, blink, tilt and hop, and his eyes shut while a PIN is typed.
- **The menus**: a small Zyper at the right of the ZyperOS menu's heading,
  64 units tall: the top panel MENU opens on the shelf, not the panels
  under it or a system's menu. He stands at the right end of the heading
  band, as high as he can and still hop inside the panel: his top 26 units
  (a hop at the top of a breath) under the frame, a few units under the
  middle of the heading's capitals. He hops as the menu opens; at rest he
  breathes and blinks every 3 to 6 s; when something in the menu succeeds
  (a setting changed, a sign-in, box art done) he hops and pants; when
  something fails (a sign-in refused, no network, a box art run that ends
  on a problem) he cocks his head to 18 degrees with his tongue in, holds it
  860 ms and settles. A move does nothing to him, and any press drops a
  return he had booked and starts him back from where he is. He never
  covers a row. *(My pick in the Zyper lab, 2026-11-04.)*
- **The menu music's new song** (11.22): on the home and the games shelf,
  in a slab under the header with the song's name; he hops and pants as it
  comes, and breathes and blinks while it stays. *(2026-11-06.)* The
  same slab says MP4's track when L2/R2 move MP4 from the shelf.
- **Getting hot** (11.23): on the home and the shelves when the chip passes
  85 C, up from under the bottom edge at the left with his own bubble, as in
  the tutorial; he hops and pants as he comes.
- **A battery too low to start** (11.24): the first screen, large, his head
  cocked with his tongue in; the power-off from where he stands, or a hop
  and a pant when a charger goes in.
- **Charging while off** (11.25): asleep, his eyes and mouth shut, breathing
  slowly, his z's going up.

- **An achievement won** (11.7): over a game, at the right end of the unlock's
  notice, a still picture of him as he rests in the menus. *(2026-11-07.)*

Apart from that pop-up he is never on the shelf; he is over a game only in
that notice, never more than one at a time, and his lime is the system's
lime: he does not get a color of his own.

In Beard his beard is the ground's own white, and drawn flat he loses his
outline; there he casts the **focus-small** shadow (8 down, 20 blur, ink at
18%), the way everything in Beard is lifted. In Salt & Pepper he needs
nothing.

---

## 11. Components

Each component lists its tokens. Sizes are units.

### 11.1 Home and game shelves

The home uses pixel console sprites on **ground**, without slabs,
perspective cards, reflections, decorative frames, or overlays. The sprites
are ZyperOS's own system art (`res/system-art/original`, 2026-11-12): every
console and handheld drawn for ZyperOS, 256 units square, with a motion of
its own. *(2026-11-13: "We should leave no trace of the old art behind".)*

MENU > Home Screen offers **Spotlight** (default), **Gallery** and **Grid**
(below); Right steps on, Left back, and A opens the three as a list (11.3's option rows). Old `cards` and `cards_dir` settings have no effect. Fancy Pants,
the old system posters and their generators are removed, not hidden.
**START** on the home steps it on too, Spotlight to Gallery to Grid and round,
with no menu, as START steps a shelf's View: the same setting, saved the same
way, the home on the same system, with the move sound; not with MENU or
SELECT held (MENU + START is the way out). Every layout's foot names where it
goes, START's hint 32 right of MENU's (**START Gallery** on Spotlight,
**START Grid** on Gallery, **START Spotlight** on Grid), in the Home Screen
row's words. *(2026-11-17: "also implement start in the home menu with the
systems".)* The place stays in the middle of the foot; where a long word
would come nearer than 32 to it (Italian's "In evidenza" by the Grid's
"12 / 21"), it stands 32 right of START's word instead.

**Spotlight:** system name and count on the left, the console art on the
right, five nearby sprites in an unframed dock below. The selected sprite
has Highlight's mark (3.5): Bar's bar where the underline always was, 16
under it, Border's ring round its 128-unit place, holding still as the dock
slides under it, Lift's rise on its own shadow, or None's others a step
back. Names use concise display labels such as
GBA and N64 and remain on one line. These labels never change file identity.

**Gallery:** large focused sprite with smaller neighbors, the full system
name and count below. The full name stays on one line at a readable size.
The focused sprite is one and a half times its 256-unit cell (384, at y 120 to 504);
its mark (Highlight's, 3.5; Bar's bar where its underline was) sits a grid
step under the whole cell, never on the art, and
the name a little lower again, so tall art such as the Game Boy keeps its
lower body clear.
**Grid** *(2026-11-15, the dense layout, with the shelves' Grid, 11.47)*: the
systems in a grid, more of them at once, each its still with its concise name
under it, **text-soft**; the focus as Highlight marks it (3.5): the focused
still grows (6% with Bar, 12% with Lift, 8% with None, 3% with Border) on its
own soft shadow - its shape blurred, made once as it arrives, leaning to the
accent's ink in Beard and a light in the accent in Salt & Pepper - its name
following the still's foot and turning to **text**; Bar's bar 8 under the
name's box, Border's **surface** card 8 round the cell, grown 3%, on a soft
lift, with the ring round it; the others a step back. The marks glide from
cell to cell in **ms.quick**, the window of rows in **ms.move**.
*(2026-11-15: a name and its underline alone were hard to find at arm's
length among 21 systems; 2026-11-19, the focus lab: the step back of the
others and the still's own rise answer that now, with a mark of the player's
choosing.)* The focus is drawn in one place (src/home.c, `hg_focus`). Only the
focused system's sprite moves, as on the others.

**The grid sizes itself** to how many systems the home shows *(2026-11-19:
Man in Boots on the Discord, "a little too much white space" on a card of few
systems, asked for a grid size to set; I wanted no setting, the grid fitting
the systems instead, and picked from the home grid lab)*: the still in four
steps, the biggest whose rows hold every system, so fewer systems get bigger
tiles and a full card keeps the 1.0.6 grid. The name grows with its still. A
step's cell is its still and 12 either side (129 for 104), the cells
DS_GRID_GAP apart, as many across as fit between the 64 margins, the columns
as few as the rows allow so the rows are even (5 is 3 + 2, 7 is 4 + 3), the
grid in the middle of its room (from the top bar's band and a section, 96, to
568). The rule is `gh_layout` (src/gridrule.h); `check-gridrule` holds this
table. On the 1024 x 768 canvas:

| Systems | Still | Across, rows | Name | On the Pixel 2 | On the Brick |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 to 3 | 256 | up to 3, one row | **meta** (37, line 40) | 160 px | 256 px |
| 4 to 8 | 176 | up to 4, two rows (4 is one) | **support** (28, line 36) | 110 px | 176 px |
| 9, 10 | 128 | 5, two rows | **chrome** (24, line 28) | 80 px | 128 px |
| 11, 12 | 104 | 6, two rows | **chrome** | 65 px | 104 px |
| 13 and more | 104 | 6, three rows in sight, the rail | **chrome** | 65 px | 104 px |

Thirteen and more is the 1.0.6 grid: cells of at least 128 filling the width
(129 x 140 from 65, 98), three rows in sight, scrolling, the rail at the right.
A wider canvas (a screen of another shape in the 768-high design space) has
more across by the same rule. A short last row stands in the middle under
the full ones. The stills are the 128-pixel ones drawn larger, nearest, as
Gallery's are; nothing more is decoded. The band: the system's whole name in
**game-title** (Favorites in the language that is on), A's **Open games** at
its right, its count under it in **support**; the foot at 720: MENU and
START, the place, SELECT. **Up** and **Down** move a row, to the system
nearest the place where the column started, so Down then Up comes back to the
same system (Down from the last column over a short row lands on its last
system), stopping at the top and the foot with the edge sound; **Left** and
**Right** step, a press going round the ends and a held one stopping at the
first or the last system with the edge sound once, as a list's does;
**L1** and **R1** jump a screenful, or to the last (first) system where less is left, and from the last round to the first (as the shelf grid's, 11.47).
A system hidden or emptied leaves the grid as it leaves the row; Favorites is
a tile like any other. When the systems change (one turned off or on in
Home Systems, 11.48; Favorites or Recents made or emptied; a system's last
game deleted; System Order turned), the grid **glides** from the tiles it drew
last to its new shape: each system slides and grows to its new place on the
**turn** curve in **ms.turn** (300), one that left fades out as its own still
where it was in **ms.fade-out**, one that came fades in at its place. While a
menu is over the home the glide waits at its start, drawing no more frames
than the home would; it runs from the frame the menu starts to leave, so it
is seen; a press during it moves the focus with its system. It runs only from a grid drawn last: never on the first frame, nor
from Spotlight or Gallery. A frame of the glide is the same stills drawn at
places between: nothing is decoded for it.

MENU remains at the left footer with START after it, the position in the
middle, and A at the right. SELECT still opens the player without another footer caption.
**L1**/**R1** jump three systems, coming round past either end as a step
does, the row moving all three in the press's direction in **move** and
**out**; on a games shelf they jump a screenful. **Up**/**Down** on the home
jump the same way, by what MENU > System Order (11.29) says: with release
date, three systems, as L1/R1 do; with A to Z, **Down** goes to the first
system of the next letter and **Up** to the first of the cursor's own letter,
or of the one before when it is already there. They come round past the ends
as L1/R1 do, in the same motion and sounds. Favorites, which the order puts
first, and Ports, which it puts last, are a group each, so a letter never
swallows them and they keep the places the order gives them. The letter is the
first letter or digit of the system's name, worked out from the names in
memory when the press comes: nothing is read, and nothing runs per frame.
*(2026-11-07: I wanted Up and Down to do on the home what they do on a shelf.)*
**L2**/**R2** are the menu
music's song before and the next on both, with the song pop-up (11.22);
while MP4 plays behind the shelf, MP4's track before and the next instead
(11.14). *(2026-11-06.)*

Only the focused sprite animates, in the motion drawn for it: a handheld its
startup, a console its cartridge going in or its lid or tray, a symbol its two
poses of 400 ms each (Favorites' heart beats, MP4's note moves beside its
video). Each frame keeps its timing, colors and transparency. The asset
compiler (tools/systemart.py) writes, for each design, its first pose whole
and the moving region of every pose packed under it, and a still 128 units
square; the renderer keeps the focused design's first pose as a texture and
writes the region of the pose due into it, and only the focus's atlas and its
two neighbors' are decoded, at most 2.1 MiB each. The row and every other
place draw the stills. Movement uses **ms.move** and **out**, including
interrupted reversals.

Nothing a step on the home does waits on a file *(2026-11-19: on the Pixel 2
the home's frames stalled to 50 ms and more as the cursor crossed systems)*:
the stills and atlases are decoded on the art workers, and an atlas arrives
in the texture's format and is kept as it comes, never copied on the frame;
the focus draws its still until its atlas is in. The focused system's shelf
- its covers' paths and files, MP4's album covers - is asked for ahead of A
only once the cursor has rested on it 400 ms, not on every step: a shelf
opened sooner asks for its own as it draws.

**Games:** the selected **Cover row** design has a straight horizontal row of
real box art: the focused cover large and flat in its 384 x 328 box, and two
covers either side of it, five in sight, turned as MP4's album row turned
its side cards (its coverflow, until MP4's shelf became a games shelf's in
11.67): 0.82 radians, the near edge outward, seen through the same weak
perspective, the far edge shorter. The near pair is at 72% and 290 out, at
side alpha 150, the far pair at 56% and 458 out, 48%, standing partly past
the screen's edge for the widest boxes;
a cover sliding in comes from 560 out, unseen, its picture decoded before it
shows (src/games.c's seats). A turned cover is narrower, which is what lets
five fit with the focused one at its size, and the near pair stays clear of
the focused cover for every shape of box. *(2026-11-18: "can we fit more
covers instead of three? and make the furthest ones a bit inclined like we
do in the mp4 player?"; three flat covers before, the two beside at 72%,
42% and 390 out.)* A turned cover is drawn as MP4's are, in upright strips
through the GPU's geometry (8 a cover), every texture coordinate inside 0 to
1, as the handhelds' SDL 2.32.10 draws nothing for one outside. Every cover
keeps its aspect ratio. There is no reflection or added card frame. The full
scraped title appears below, followed by play time and the achievement
count. Missing metadata is omitted rather than invented.
A is **Resume** only when the existing resume path is available, otherwise
**Play**. B returns to systems, START steps the shelf's View on, Covers to Grid
to List to Video and round (11.39, 11.47, 11.57), Y starts the game from the start, and X opens game details, whose
last row is Delete Game (11.31). The foot spreads their hints from B at the
left to X at the right (11.4).
Long titles wrap or fit within their region, clear of the action and footer.
**A game with no art** gets a card of its own (src/cardart.h, drawn by
src/ui.c's game_art_card on the art workers), the same in both themes, as a
box is:

- **Its ground** says the system without anyone's trade dress: a gradient
  in the system's color, the texture of its era (Bayer dither for the 8-bit
  systems, scanlines for 16-bit and arcade, low-poly shards for the 3D
  systems, LCD dots for handhelds), a shape (a band, a disc - a sunset on
  the scanline era - or two stripes) and a second color picked by a hash of
  the name, and the system's own art (res/system-icons, ours) faint in a
  corner. A grey system's hash mixes a hue in. The same name, the same card,
  always; a shelf of games with no art is varied.
- **Once played** the ground is the game's own frame of play, blurred,
  dimmed and turned a little toward the system's color: Diatom keeps one
  frame a minute into a game's first play, <rom>.card.bmp, and never writes
  it again; until then, the Auto slot's picture.
- **The name** is one treatment on every card, every system, played or not,
  so a shelf, the Grid and the list are read the same way: the title face
  (Lexend SemiBold), as the shelf writes the name; hard left at 44 of 512;
  its first line's capitals at 38% of the card; at 60 on every card, smaller
  (50, then 42) only for a word wider than the card or a name past four
  lines; what follows " - " under it at 36 in up to two; balanced lines,
  never a word of three letters or fewer alone at the end; near-white on a
  dark band across the card, feathered; the system's rule under it. The era
  is in the ground, never in the letters. At least 5:1 on the band over the
  brightest ground any system can make (tools/cardart-check.c).

- **As the cover row stops on it** (src/focusfx.h): one light crosses the
  card, once, over **sheen** on the turn curve - a soft band leaning across
  it, kept inside its round corners - and a played game's blurred frame
  turns sharp over **sharpen**, the sharp card asked of the art workers for
  the focused card alone. Nothing loops: a still shelf draws nothing, and a
  press stops both at once. Not on the Grid or the list. ZyperOS has no
  Reduce Motion setting yet; the sheen is the one to honor it when it comes.

An album with no cover keeps the theme's generated text card.

**Art badge** (src/artbadge.h): a small mark on a card's art that says one
thing about it, at the art's top left - the corner opposite the video snap's
mark (11.19), and clear of the middle, where a cover's subject and title are.
A glyph on a **surface** disc AB_D 40 across, inset AB_INSET 12 from both
edges, over a soft shadow of **ink** (down 2, softened over 6, 28% in Beard
and 47% in Salt & Pepper), so the glyph has the theme's ground under it on
any art; drawn by signed distance on the status marks' 24-unit grid, 3:1 or
better on its disc in both themes. Disc and inset scale with the card as the
row scales it (a near side cover's is 29), at the turned corner of a turned
cover and as large as the cover is there, the card's fade fades it, and under
20 there is none. It is the same component everywhere it is used:
- The favorite's heart is no badge (below): it leads the game's name.
- On MP4's **All** (11.14), what each card is, in **accent-ink**: an
  **album**, the top bar's two beamed notes; a **book**, an open book; a
  **video**, a screen with a play mark cut out of it. On one kind alone the
  shelf says it, and no card carries one.

*(2026-11-09: "show a small heart badge when the game is a favorite", and on
All "a small badge saying what it is ... in the same badge component and
style as the heart". On the card, not beside the title on the page: a
title's heart says it of the focused game only, while the card's goes with
every cover in sight as the row moves, and it is where the tutorial's Keep a
Favorite step already pops it.)*

**The favorite's heart** leads the focused game's name on the games page, in
**favorite** (draw_heart), centered on the name's capitals, and the name moves
over for it. Not on Favorites, where every game is one, and on no card.
*(2026-11-09: "The heart is very hard to see. Maybe put it to the left of the
title? No need to show heart when in the favorite page.")*

The original game and music identities, saves, launch paths and controls do
not change with either home layout or with scraped display names.


**Under a game's name** on a games shelf, in **chrome** and **text-dim**: on
**Favorites** the game's own system ("SNES", the full name as the Gallery
shows it) right under the name, at cap y 628 in the second line's place when
the name takes one line, ending by x 760, clear of A's hint; under a name of
two lines, or one too long for that place, it leads the line below. That line,
at cap y 664, has "Played ..." and the achievements, 32 apart. On a console's
own shelf, whose heading names it, there is no system. A part that would pass
x 960 (the right margin, as the left) is left out with the ones after it.
*(2026-11-07: "In the favorites screen, let's have the system written
below the game name", then, seeing it at y 664: "have the system right below
the name. now there is a big margin".)*
### 11.2 Rail
**bar** high, **track**, the marker a window onto every item's color.

### 11.3 Menu panel
**surface**, **radius.panel**, a 3-unit **edge** frame, **elevation.panel**,
over **scrim.menu**. Centered, 24 from the screen's edges, but never above
y 96, clear of the header row (11.12), which stands over
every screen a panel opens on; a list too tall for that scrolls in its
window. Heading in **label**, **text-soft**, over a 2-unit rule
in **edge**, a system's menu and the in-game menu's too. Rows in **menu**:
live rows **text-soft**, values **text-dim**, dead rows **text-dim** at both.
The focused row is an **accent** fill, **radius.row**, its label and value in
**on-accent**. More rows above or below: a small triangle in **text-dim**,
24 by 12 (`ds_more`), as on every list. An option's list (below) that
ends in a footer (a rule and only notes under it) and does not fit keeps the
footer still under its rows (`menu_style.pin_foot`): the rows above the rule
scroll in what room is left, as a card's body scrolls over its pinned
footer. Elsewhere a long list's footer goes round with its rows.
Opens and closes as 8.2 says.

**Option rows, one rule (1.0.6).** A row whose value is one of several
choices answers the same way on every page - MENU, a system's menu, the
MP4 shelf, the in-game menu's Game and Display tabs, Theme, Night Mode's page,
Video Snaps, Menu Music:

- **More than two choices: A opens their list**, a panel of its own under
  the row's name, the choice that is on ticked and the cursor on it; A
  chooses one, B leaves it as it was. Its words begin with a capital, as a
  list's rows do, where the row's value may be "never" or "stereo". Where a
  choice needs saying what it does, the list says it, for the choice under
  the cursor: two lines under a rule, always two, so the panel holds its
  size as the cursor moves - the row itself says only its value.
- **A choice with a picture** (Display's modes, 1.0.6; `src/ds/preview.h`,
  section 15): the list has a column at its left, as the Controls tab's
  handheld has, **preview-gap** (32) from its rows, and in it the
  handheld's screen in small (4:3, **ink** where the picture is not, framed
  in **edge**'s `DS_FRAME` line, its corners the focus fill's,
  `DS_RADIUS_ROW`) with what the choice under the cursor does to the game,
  and its sentence under that in **type.meta**, **text-soft**, left, lines
  40 apart, up to six (four in the widest column), in a room as tall as the
  set's tallest, so nothing moves as the cursor does. The column is at the
  list's left, where the Controls tab's handheld is, so the two pictures
  beside a list sit in one place (the sheet approved for 1.0.6); the rows
  keep their right edge and their values. In a game, the paused frame at the size and shape Diatom
  said, placed as Diatom would (src/dmodefit.h, held to Diatom's own
  arithmetic by make check-dmodefit); on a shelf, the game under the
  cursor's last frame, or a drawn scene for one never played. The column
  is **preview-col-w** (336) units, narrower where the choices run long, to
  **preview-col-min** (200), so every choice keeps its words; the list is as wide as the column and its
  longest choice need (`menu_pick_preview`, src/menu_pick.h).
- **A row with a scale** (Screen Colors, 1.0.6; `src/ds/scale.h`, section
  15): a value from -range to +range, zero in the middle, is a graded bar
  where its value would be, its row's words at the left as any row's. The
  track, 16 high in an **edge** ring, says in its colors what the scale
  does: cool blue to warm orange, green to magenta, one grey parting to
  black and white, a color's grey to the color at its fullest, darker to
  lighter mid-tones. Marks under it at the ends and halves and zero's
  through it, the tallest; the value a knob 32 across, a ring in **text**
  round the panel's **raised**; the number after it, signed, its digits
  tabular ("−3" with a minus sign, "0", "+5"). The bars of a page start
  together, past the widest of their rows' words, and are never under 192
  units, so a row's words stay at most about 313 units in every language.
  On the focus fill its marks, ring and number are **on-accent**. A row
  carries it as its value (`ds_scale_value`); the panel draws it.
- **A color chart** (Screen Colors; `src/ds/colorref.h`): what a color
  setting is judged on, the 24-patch chart photographers use (skin, sky,
  foliage, the primaries and their mixes, six greys), a sixteen-step grey
  ramp and the hues in a band; four across in a column beside the rows,
  six across in a list's picture. The setting changes the whole screen, so
  the chart shows it as the panel makes it.
- **What the cursor shows** (1.0.6 rc.2; `src/ds/hover.h`, section 15):
  a list whose choice changes how the whole screen looks shows the one
  under the cursor from the frame the cursor reaches it, once a move and
  never each frame; the tick stays on the kept choice, and B, MENU or
  POWER put back what is kept before anything else runs - nothing is saved
  until A. While a list shows a choice that is not kept, nothing that keeps
  the screen's state writes it down (the boot screen's words, the theme
  file: `ds_hover_showing`). Theme's four lists (11.42); Folder Color's has
  a picture beside it too, since no folder is on the list. `check-hover-sweep`
  shows every choice of them and fails on a database row or a file written,
  or a page that reads otherwise after.
- **Two: A turns it over**, unless the two need a picture to tell apart:
  **Timing** (Smooth or Exact, a GB, GBC, GBA, DS or Genesis shelf's)
  opens its list on A all the same (`menu_choice.list`), Left and Right
  still turning it over. Its picture is the two ways slowed down, in the
  dark theme's colors as a game's screen: a ball crossing a small screen a
  step a game frame, a refresh every 160 ms, and where it was at the five
  refreshes before behind it. Smooth's steps are even; with Exact a refresh
  now and then shows the frame before again, the ball stopping for one,
  its two places ringed in the accent - one refresh in four here, one in
  about 170 on the Pixel 2, so it can be seen at all. Under it, what each
  does in a sentence. *(Tarik's for 1.0.6 rc.2: "improve the timing screen
  to explain/demonstrate that".)* **Super Game Boy**'s two open their list
  the same way, a picture of each (11.58).
- **Left and Right step every row**, as before, and show the change at
  once, so a look before choosing is one press away. A scale (minutes,
  percent, volume) stops at its ends; a set of names goes round.
- Not option rows: a row that opens a page (Theme, Side Lights, Player 2),
  Date & Time's year, month, day, hour and minute (digits of one value), and
  rows with a list of their own (Language, Time Zone, a Controls button).

It is one runner, not code on each row: a page's build gives each option
row a table of its choices (`menu_choice`, src/menu.h) beside its rows, as it
gives their sound classes, in the order its Left and Right go; the runner
opens the list (src/menu_pick.c) and applies the choice by sending the row's
own Left or Right as many times as it is away (`menu_choose`), so a screen
needs no code to apply one. `check-menu-rows` fails on a row that steps with
no table, unless it is a switch or a pair, and on a table that says
something other than its row.

**Lists go round.** Down on the last row that can be selected goes to the
first, and Up on the first to the last, in every menu list - MENU, a
system's menu, the in-game menu, a screen's rows, MP4's list, the
achievement list, Storage, the yes/cancel panel. A **held** direction stops
at the end and stays there, playing **edge** once (9); the next press goes
round, heard as any step. It is one rule in one place, `menu_step_round`
for a press and `menu_step_sel` for a hold (src/menu.c), and
`menu_step_index` for the loops that keep a plain index. *(2026-11-12: I
want lists to go round, and a held direction to stop at the end. From 1.0.1
to then they stopped at both.)*

A list that fits its panel sends the focus fill back to the other end in
**quick**, as any step. A list longer than its panel **goes on**: after its
last row comes a seam, a row's height with the heading's rule across it,
and then its first row again, without end either way, so going round is
one step and the window scrolls through the seam as it scrolls through rows
(`menu_loop_*`: slots, where slot s is row s mod (n + 1) and the seam is the
slot that comes to n). Both triangles show on such a list, since there is
always more.

**MENU's tabs.** MENU is in six tabs, **Games**, **Display**, **Sound**,
**Connections**, **System** and **About**, and **L1** and **R1** go round
them (src/sys_menu.h, `sys_menu_tab_of`). The heading band is the tab row:
the tab that is on in **label** **text**, centered where the heading was,
and the home's underline, an **accent-ink** bar 6 high, on the heading's rule
under its name. The row has the whole band: the L1 glyph (11.9's pill at 40,
as the keyboard's action keys) at the content's left inset and R1 at its
right end, which is a grid step and a half before Zyper, or the content's
edge with Zyper off (3.5). Between them, the tabs either side in **meta**
**text-dim**, two each way, going on round from the tab that is on as far
as they reach; whatever reaches an end fades into it over 64 units, so a
long name shows as much of itself as fits and is never cut hard
(`ui_text_faded`). *(2026-11-12, from my test of 1.0.5: "use the full width
up to the zyper icon (if present) and kinda fade away on the extremes
before the shoulder buttons". Until then a neighbour that did not fit whole
was left out.)* A press slides the rows out the way it went and the next
tab's in from the other side, 64 units and faded up, and the tab row the
same, 96, in **move** on **out**, the glyphs holding still; the bar takes
the new name's width. The
panel is as tall as the screen allows on every tab and as wide as the widest
tab's rows, so it holds still as the tabs go round. MENU opens on Games after
a start, and after that on the tab and row it was closed on, until the
handheld powers off; on Connections' USB Device while a plug waits for its
answer (11.36). A system's menu is one list.

**The in-game menu has tabs too**, four of them, **Game**, **Display**,
**Controls** and **Achievements** (11.46), in the same tab row from the same code: the tab set (names,
count, the tab on, the move) is passed to `menu_tabs_draw`, `menu_tab_row` and
`menu_tabs_bar` in `menu_style.tabs`, so MENU's six and the in-game menu's
three are one component, and the neighbours shown either side are at most
(count - 1) / 2, one each way for three tabs (11.28). *(2026-11-13: I wanted
the in-game menu to look like MENU, and the Controls tab needed the room.)*
*(My picks in the menu tabs mockups, 2026-11-12: the tab row as drawn, the
groups, "Connections" and "Display" for the tabs' names, and MENU opening
where it was.)*

| Tab | Rows |
|---|---|
| **Games** | Search Games, Play Time, Random Game, Downloads (11.53) |
| **Display** | Appearance, Theme, Home Screen, System Order, Home Systems, Battery Icon, Screensaver, Shader and Strength (ZyperOS's own screens', 11.50; not on the Brick), Screen Colors, Night Mode (its page: Night Mode, Night Warmth, Night From, Until) |
| **Sound** | Audio Output, Audio Channels, Equalizer (11.60), UI Sounds, Menu Music, Rumble (a handheld with a motor ZyperOS runs, 11.54) |
| **Connections** | USB Device (while a plug waits), Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, File Transfer, USB Accessories, Developer Mode |
| **System** | Date & Time, Clock, Language, Hotkeys, Power Button, Auto Off, Charge Limit, Side Lights, Storage, System Update |
| **About** | About ZyperOS, Controls, Show Tutorial |

A row added to MENU goes into `pm_row` where its tab's rows are and into
`sys_menu_tab_of`, whose switch the compiler holds to every row.

**A panel of pages** (Left/Right turns them: About ZyperOS, MENU > Controls,
a game's Controls) says which page it shows at the heading's right, in
**meta** **text-dim**, "2 / 5", with the same room kept free on the
heading's left so the centered title never meets it; and has no footer
saying so ("Left/Right: more" is gone). 2026-11-06, for every panel of
pages (docs/design/mock/controls-fit-lab.html). Without the footer six rows
fit under the heading; a heading too wide beside the count gives way to a
shorter name ("Controls: GBA"). A page with no cursor that does not fit
scrolls itself, so every Controls page is measured with the font
(`--controls-fit`, in check-controls-shots).

A key or a token is shown as four bullets and its last four characters,
"••••1a2b", and nowhere in full: a value in a row (MENU > Downloads > Settings), a
link on the File Transfer page. Notes under a rule fit the panel on one line
each, about 30 characters at **menu**; a longer one is said in two.

### 11.4 Key legend
**Button hints** are the form now (2026-11-10, src/keyhints.h): what each
button does as a row of the button's glyph (11.9, 32 units) and its word in
**chrome** **text-soft**, 12 between them, 32 between hints (at least 16
in a spread foot), as the
shelves' feet have always drawn them. One component lays them out: a list
of {button, word} in order, as many in a row as fit, rows 44 apart, never
a hint cut or split, centered under a panel's tiles or spread across a
screen's foot (Now Playing's grows upward from its line). The d-pad's
Left/Right ("< >", `KEYHINT_LEFT_RIGHT`) is the d-pad itself, the tutorial's
cross a quarter larger than a face's glyph, its left and right arms a step
darker with their arrows (since 1.0.6; a pill with the signs before). Words are res/strings' keyhint.*
or a screen's own, a hint's capitals (Title Case in English). Under every
question in tiles (11.18, 11.23, 11.36) and Now Playing's foot. *(I asked
for one component to use across the UI.)*

The game shelves' feet are one too since 2026-11-13, on every screen's foot
(720, `DS_FOOT_Y`, every screen's), spread from x 64 to
960: B, START (List or Covers), Y when the game resumes, X. They were at
fixed places (B 64, Y 432, X 832), with no room kept for a fourth.

Since 2026-11-11 every legend is one: a panel's foot (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth,
Play Time, Date & Time, Developer Mode, Album Art, File Transfer, every "B
close" and "B stop"), the hotspot's page and the tutorial's "(START) skip".
A screen hands a panel its list packed as a row's label
(`keyhints_legend`), and the panel draws it centered on that row; the
checks read it back as text (tools/legend.h, `legend_text`). The words are
keyhint.*, each language's as it wrote the old legend's. The older text
form, "A: go   Y: by game", is gone.

### 11.5 Keyboard
The whole keyboard of src/keyboard.c, every decision in it kept because each
saves presses on a D-pad: four rows of ten keys with the digits on every
layer, three layers (letters, capitals, symbols), the grid wrapping at every
edge; the face buttons are verbs - A types, B deletes, X shifts, Y goes to
the symbols - L1/R1 move the cursor, START is done, MENU cancels; the text is
never hidden.

Under the grid, an **action row** of keys that do the same as the buttons:
shift, symbols, space, delete, done. Each shows its button's glyph beside its
label (X shift, Y #+=, B delete, START done; space is drawn as the space
mark), and X's and Y's labels follow the layer ("unshift", "abc"). One line
under the row, in **meta** **text-dim**, names the two with no key: L1/R1
cursor, MENU cancel. A has no caption: it is OK everywhere. *(
2026-11-04, in the keyboard lab: the action row, with one quiet line instead
of the two rows of seven captions it had.)*

The panel is a menu's around the keys: a menu's padding at each side (about
940 wide in all), its heading band and **edge** rule, its top at a tall menu's,
and the field as wide as the keys, so their edges line up. *(2026-11-11: it
had a tighter band, no rule and a wider field than the keys, and sat higher
than a menu.)* A button and its label fit in each action key at
**meta** size. In Lexend the longest, "unshift", has 3 to 4 units to spare at
each side of its key; a face that sets it wider gives up half the gap between
button and label and then the end of the label, never the key's edge. Keys are
**raised**, **radius.key**; the selected key is the **accent** fill with
**on-accent** text, its glyph outlined in **on-accent**; the field is
**ground** inside an **edge** line, with a 3-unit **accent-ink** caret (lime
ink, since the fill's lime is 1.3:1 on Beard's ground). A press pops the key
in **press** and plays **accept**; a delete plays **back**.

### 11.6 Volume and brightness bar
A pill (src/osdbar.c) 452 x 64 at the top center, 22 below the top edge:
**surface** at 93%, **edge**, **elevation.bar**. The mark - a speaker whose
three waves grow arc by arc, each over its third of the scale, with a cross
at silence, or a sun whose disc and rays grow - in a 52-unit box 42 units in
from the pill's left end, 13 units clear of the first segment at its
largest; it follows the level in **quick** (out), up and down. It is in
**accent-ink** for volume and **light** for brightness, and one segment per
step in the same color over **track**. In **quick** (out) sliding down 14,
held **hold.bar** after the last press, out in **fade.out**. The same pixels
on the shelf, over a Diatom game and over a port. Diatom reads the theme
from `/tmp/zyperos-theme`, which the launcher writes; portkeys, over a port
the launcher has exited for, reads Appearance from the launcher's database
and resolves Auto by the launcher's rule in the zone Date & Time saved, and
reads the file only when the database cannot be read, so a hand edit of the
file changes the bar over a port only then.

On the Pixel 2 brightness has twelve rungs, equal ratios from 9 to 255
(src/device/pixel2_levels.h). The backlight itself goes no lower than 17, so
the two bottom rungs keep it there and the display's gamma table takes the
light to 13/17 and 9/17 of it (src/panelgamma.h): the launcher, Diatom and
portkeys each set the table from the value in sysfs, which is the rung's
name. Moving between those two rungs blinks one frame at the floor: the
PX30 loads its table with the table off. A rung kept before 1.0.6 moved once
to the new rung nearest its light. The player's Screen Colors (11.42) go in
the same table, on every rung. *(Discord, 1.0.6: "one or two dimmer
steps"; Tarik, 2026-11-15, after a 20 kHz backlight went black under 17.)*

**The boost past the top** (src/boost.h, src/levels.c). Some films and
games are quiet even at volume 20, and the top rung of brightness is already
the backlight's own maximum. At the top, the up key held **hold-boost** (1 s)
more, or pressed three more times with no more than **tap-gap** (1 s)
between two presses, opens cells past it. While it charges, the pill's end
pushes out by one big charging cell (36 x 30 units, in the first 30% of the
charge, on out) and the cell fills in the theme's **warning**: linearly with
a hold, a third with each press; let go early, or the presses stop for
longer than tap-gap, and it drains back at the speed and on the curve it
filled. Full, it settles to a cell's size in **boost-open** (240 ms, out)
while the pill grows both ways, staying centered, and the other cells come
out of its end; a thin mark in **text-dim** at 70% stands at the top between
the steps and the cells; the rim flashes in **warning** and fades back to
**edge** over **boost-flash** (360 ms, fade); and a 40 ms rumble at the
player's Rumble level is felt, with no sound - a volume press never makes a
UI sound, the media's own is the feedback. The opening fills the first cell
and a key still held stops there: each further cell is a press. Each cell is
