a little taller than the one before, 16 to 28 units, so the boost reads by
its shape too, with any accent (a Red, Orange or Pink accent's ink is near
**warning**) and to a color-blind player. A cell fills from its left in
**quick** and empties in **exit**; a press on a full boost pushes the end 6
units out and back (**press**, then **exit**). The boost's parts of the mark
are in **warning** too, so the mark keeps its color: a fourth wave, and
eight short rays between the sun's, growing with the cells filled.

Going down empties the cells one by one; at the top they wait, empty; the
next press goes under the top and folds them back into the end in **exit**.
An empty boost closes with the bar; a filled one stays, kept on the card
beside the level (`volume_boost`, `brightness_boost`), so it is there at the
next start. A plug going in or out closes the volume boost: the new output
starts at the top without it.

- **Volume:** six cells of 2 dB, up to +12 dB, near a step under the top
  (1.8 dB on the Pixel 2's speaker, 2.3 on the Brick). The gain is software,
  in src/eqdsp.h after the equalizer's bands, moving over 20 ms, with a
  limiter that keeps every peak under -1 dB of full scale (down at once, back
  over 100 ms, both channels together): a quiet film gets louder, a loud one
  only flatter, and the speaker never gets more than a loud game gives it at
  20. It opens in MP4 (MP4, told with a GAIN line), in a game (Diatom) and
  over a port (the zyperos-eq plugin, now in every port's chain, the gain in
  the shared file that portkeys writes during the port). MP4's lock screen
  fills and empties an open one but never opens one: a held key in a pocket
  must not. The menus show the bar as it was, the menu music is never
  boosted, and a press under the top there folds a kept boost.
- **Brightness:** three cells, each a lift of the mid-tones on the display's
  gamma table after the player's Screen Colors, like Gamma +3: x^0.88,
  x^0.76, x^0.64 (mid grey 128 to 139, 151 and 164), black and white kept.
  No more light, no frame and no battery: a bright scene looks flatter. It
  opens anywhere. On the Pixel 2 each step blinks one frame as any table
  load; the Brick loads it at vblank. Screenshots never show it.

My picks in the volume boost lab, 2026-11-19: the hold of 1 s and three
presses each within 1 s, the big charging cell, marks that grow with the
level, rising warning cells, the pill growing both ways, six cells, the end
pushing out, a held key stopping at the first cell, a short rumble, the
boost kept like the level, games, MP4 and ports, headphones the same (a plug
closes it), a mid-tone lift for brightness. A host shot: `--osd volume 20
+4`.

**POWER held** (MENU > Power Button on **Hold**, 11.47) is the same pill with
the power mark - a ring open at the top and the line down into it, as every
power button has it - and 20 segments in **accent-ink** over **track**, one
lit for each twentieth of **hold-power** while POWER is held. Full, the
handheld turns off as a press does, and the pill holds and fades as after a
level step; let go early, it goes in **fade.out** at once and nothing else
happens. On the shelf and every screen, over a Diatom game (the launcher
tells Diatom, SETPOWERHOLD, and Diatom fills it on its own clock) and over a
port (portkeys reads POWER there). A host shot: `--osd power LEVEL`.

### 11.7 Notice (over a game)
A slab of **surface** at 85%, **radius.panel**, at the bottom center; heading
in **meta** **text-dim**, body in **label** **text**.

An achievement's notice carries its badge when the card has it: the badge as
tall as the two lines, corners **radius.row**, at the left, and the lines
left-aligned beside it. A progress notice is the achievement's name in
**meta** **text-dim**, a strip **bar** high in **accent-ink** on **track**,
and the count ("74 / 100") in **meta** **text** under it. Notices take turns:
each has its time and a 300 ms gap before the next; unlocks go ahead of
progress. The achievements list (a game's Achievements row) has the badge at the start
of every row, greyed and faded until earned, and the count where the points
are while it moves. *(My picks in the achievements lab,
docs/design/mock/cheevos-lab.html, 2026-11-05: badge at the left, strip, on
every row.)*

**The plain badge.** Where the card has no badge for an achievement, none yet
or never (a handheld with no network has none until a computer puts them
there, `tools/ra-sets.py`), the place is not left empty: a trophy on a tile,
the badge's size and corners, in the theme's own colors (src/noticeart.h).
Earned, the trophy is **accent-ink** on **raised**; locked, as every row of
the list not yet won and a progress notice, **text-dim** on **track**, faded
as a badge is. So a list and a notice are the one shape whether the art is on
the card or not, and nothing moves when a fetch lands it. Badges are fetched
on the shelf, never in a game, on a thread of their own in one curl for what
is missing (src/rabadge.h); no frame waits on the network.

**The achievement's card.** A on a row of the list (or of the Achievements
tab, 11.46) opens the achievement's card: its title as the heading, then its
badge under the heading's rule, the list's own picture of it (greyed and faded
until earned, the plain badge while the card has no art), 96 on the grid
(RetroAchievements' 64-pixel art at about its own size on the Pixel 2),
centered over the description as the description is centered, with the
rule's gap under it (the panel's header picture). The description is wrapped
whole, never cut; where it is taller than the panel leaves it, it scrolls
itself, as a card with no cursor does, and only it: the rule, **Points** and
**Status** under it stay. *(My ask, testing 1.1.0, 2026-11-19: the
card shows the achievement's picture, and a long description scrolls.)* Host shots: `--cheevo-card 0` to `3`, with
`--cheevos-badge SET NAME` for art and `--phase MS` for the scroll.

**Zyper on an unlock.** The notice an unlock makes, and the one for mastering
a set, has Zyper at its right end, as tall as the badge, cocked and panting as
he rests in the menus (10), in his own colors; on Beard with the
**focus-small** shadow under him, since his beard is the slab's own white
there, on Salt & Pepper with none. A count moving, the set's arrival and
fast forward's word do not have him: he is for the achievement won. He is
filled into the notice's pixels from the table the menus draw him from
(src/zyperplace.c, src/noticeart.c), so he is the same dog; the slab may run
a little wider with him (7/8 of the screen, not 5/6) so a long name keeps its
room.

### 11.8 One-line message
A menu panel over **scrim.menu**. One row holds for **hold.short**;
two rows hold for **hold.long**. The hold starts after the panel opens.
The panel fades in and out with the menu motion. B dismisses it early.
Progress before a synchronous operation is one presented frame, without a hold.
A result wider than the panel goes onto balanced lines at the menus' shared
width, up to five, and is never cut: three cut Rename's refusal of a name in
English, at the very characters it names (1.0.6).

### 11.9 Button glyphs
For the tutorial and anywhere a button is drawn rather than named
(`ui_button`, `ui_dpad`, `ui_rocker`, `ui_power` in src/ui.c). Face buttons
are circles, 96 units, **raised**, a 4-unit **edge** ring, the letter in
**title**. A key printed with a word (POWER; the Brick's MENU) is a pill 64
high, the word Regular at half the title, 30 units at each end. The Pixel
2's SELECT and START are faces: round keys nearly as big as A B X Y with
SE and ST engraved on them (tools/build-pixel2-model.py), their two letters
at 5/8 of a face's letter, as the model engraves them (1.75 mm to 2.8); they
were pills with the words until 1.0.6 (2026-11-16: "the pixel select button
is actually a circle"). MENU is a face, as the key is on the Pixel 2: a
round key with three dimples down the device's height
(tools/build-pixel2-model.py), so its glyph is the face circle with three
**text** dots in a vertical line, each 0.15 of the face letter's cap height
across in radius and half of it apart, in place of a letter *(
2026-11-05: "don't write menu... do the circle with the three dots")*.
Wherever running text says MENU (a hint, a Controls row, a message, "MENU >
Controls"), `ui_text` draws the same key inline: a ring 1.3 capitals across
in the text's own color, the three dots inside, centered on the capitals,
and `ui_text_width` measures it so fitting and breaking agree. "Menu" in
lower case stays a word. The D-pad is a cross 120 across: arms of the ring
40 wide with a corner of 10, the face inset 4 with a corner of 7, an **edge**
dot in the middle and an arrow per arm in **text**. VOL+ and VOL- are one
rocker pill 64 high with a half each, VOL- on the left, 24 units at each end
of each half's word, and a 2-unit **edge** line between them. POWER is a
96-unit circle with the power mark in **text**: a ring of 20 open at the top,
a stroke of 6 with round ends, and a stroke down into it.

**By device** (1.0.6, `src/ds/button.h`). A screen names a button by its
id's name (A, SELECT, L1, F1, the d-pad's "< >" and "^ v"), never by what one
handheld prints, and the glyph is the one this handheld prints, from its
profile's table (`dev_button`, src/device/profile.h). A round key with one
or two letters is a face with them (A; the Pixel 2's SE and ST). A round key with a mark is a face with the mark: the Pixel
2's MENU, its three dots; the Brick's SELECT, a square, and START, a play
triangle, as its model draws them (the square 0.72 of a capital across, as a
solid square carries more ink than a letter's strokes; the triangle about a
capital tall and a little right of its box's middle, so it looks centred),
in the letter's color. A round key with a word (the Brick's
MENU, whose TrimUI mark is no glyph, and its POWER) and every other cap (a
pill, a shoulder, the rocker) is a pill with its word. A stick's cap (L3,
R3) is a face with its top's ring inside, 0.7 across, under its word.
Running text that names a key whose cap carries a mark and no word draws
the key inline, as MENU's three dots always were: on the Brick, "Press
SELECT" shows its square key and "START" its play key (TrimUI's manual calls
them SELECT and START; the caps show only the marks), so the words and the
glyph tell the same story. A key printed with letters (the Pixel 2's SE and
ST, the Brick's MENU word) stays a word in running text. The host's desktop
has the Brick's keys drawn as the Pixel 2's; a name no table has is a pill
with that name. A chord is named by what it does (`dev_role_keys`:
brightness, home, battery), so a page says each handheld's own: MENU+VOL on
the Pixel 2, F1/F2 on the Brick.

**A chord as glyphs** (1.0.6). A hint that names two buttons or more ("L1/R1"
in Now Playing's and the keyboard's, "SELECT+START") draws each button's own
glyph with the sign between them, "/" for either and "+" for together, in
**meta** **text-dim**, 4 units either side at the hints' sizes and 8 at the
tutorial's 64, so the chord reads as one hint. It was one pill with the
whole name in it. A sign with nothing on one side is part of a name (VOL+).
**The Controls pages in glyphs** *(2026-11-18: my review of the Controls
ideas, "draw the Controls page with the buttons render component")*. The
button column of MENU > Controls and of a game's Controls pages (11.28) is
drawn as this handheld's glyphs, not words: the Pixel 2's SE and ST faces
and MENU's dots, the Brick's square and play keys and its MENU word, "+" for
held together, "," for one press after the other and "/" for either, at 44
(the keyboard's line), where the label goes, the value at the right as
ever. The d-pad's words are the d-pad's glyph ("Left/Right" its left and
right arms lit, "Up/Down" its up and down, "D-pad" the whole cross with all
four, "SELECT+D-pad" SELECT with it) and the volume keys' the rocker. The
rows stay words in src/controls.c, which `make check-controls` reads; the
screens turn each row whose label is buttons into a key label as they draw
it (`ctl_glyph_rows`, `MENU_KEYS_MARK` in src/menu.h), and words that are no
button (a port README's own) stay words. A glyph row is a row's height, so
six rows a page stays, and `--controls-fit` measures every page with the
glyphs. The comma sits close to the press before it, twice the chord's air
after it.

**In a menu row**, a glyph says a button does the row's job from anywhere on
the page: the row's value is `MENU_KEY_X` (src/menu.h), and the glyph is
drawn at 40, the keyboard's action keys' size, where the value's right edge
is, plain off the fill and UI_BUTTON_ON_ACCENT on it. The in-game menu's Quit
(11.28) and the Quit of MP4's MENU (11.14) are the two. "MENU, X" names the
press on a Controls page: MENU's glyph, a comma and X's, for one press after
the other, as a plus is for held together.

A pressed glyph is 92% size, its ring **accent**, with a soft **accent** halo
(16 units of blur at three quarters), in **press**. A pressed arm of the
D-pad or half of the rocker fills with **accent** and its arrow or word turns
**on-accent**, so the direction shows. A glyph may also be drawn larger about
its middle (the tutorial's pulse after a wrong press) and faded (with the
line it belongs to). At rest it is drawn by the same rule as before, pixel for
pixel.

### 11.10 Tutorial
It runs once, the first time the launcher starts on a handheld whose device
database has `tour.done` at 0 (seeded so), before the shelf is first drawn,
and again from **MENU > Show Tutorial** (between Controls and About), which
closes the menu so it ends on the shelf. A handheld that ran a ZyperOS from
before the tutorial shows it once after the upgrade too *(My call,
2026-11-04)*; `tour_wanted` is the one rule. Finishing or skipping it sets
`tour.done`, and so does the power step's press, so the second POWER in that
reward, which turns the handheld off, leaves it seen; a handheld turned off
earlier runs it again at the next start. It does not run when the launcher
starts straight back into a game, and runs at the next start that comes to
the shelf. src/tour.c is its steps, its key table and Zyper's moves,
src/tourdraw.c its screens; `make check-tour` holds the steps and
`check-tour-shots` the drawn screens and the first start, and `--tour STEP
[MS [DONE]]` draws any frame of it for a shot. *(My picks in the
tutorial lab, 2026-11-04: the welcome and finish said by Zyper, Zyper only
for the reward, and a wrong press tilted, nudged and answered.)*

| Step | Glyph | The demonstration | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|
| Language (11.30) | A on the focused row | Zyper says hello in the language under the cursor, which steps a language a second until a press | A on a language; START keeps the one moved to |
| Welcome | A | Zyper pops in and says "Hi! I'm Zyper." in his bubble | A |
| Move Around | D-pad, and L1/R1 lit on the handheld | the little shelf steps one card right and back; "L1/R1 jump 3 systems." under the line | two moves, any direction, L1/R1 counting as jumps; the little shelf follows them, a jump as far as it has cards |
| Open a Game | A | the focused card grows into a playing game | A |
| Go Back | B | the game shrinks back to its card | B |
| See the Details | X | the card turns into the details, the info screen in small: the game's name over a rule, Synopsis in focus, Year, and Favorite Off with Y's glyph | X |
| Keep a Favorite | Y | in those details Favorite turns from Off to On, the row's own Y pressing with the glyph; the line says Y does it in the details | Y |
| Settings | MENU | a menu panel opens over the shelf | MENU, which opens nothing here |
| Music | SELECT, and L2/R2 lit on the handheld | MP4's Now Playing fades in; "L2/R2 play the previous or next song." under the line | SELECT, which opens nothing here |
| Volume | the VOL rocker | a stand-in bar slides onto the stage and steps up | VOL+ or VOL-, which change the real volume and show the real bar |
| Brightness | MENU+VOL: the MENU pill, a plus and the rocker (F1 and F2 on the Brick) | the stand-in bar's sun steps up | the chord, which changes the real brightness |
| Power | POWER | the shelf fades to the ground and the power-off's own frames play small, "Saved. See you soon." | POWER, which in this step only does not turn the handheld off; the line under it says so, and after the press it says to hold POWER for 10 s only if the screen stops answering |
| Theme | none: the choices side by side | the home screen in Light and in Dark | A, on the one focused |
| Home Screen | none | the home screen in Spotlight, in Gallery and in Grid | A |
| Sounds | none | four tiles, Wood, Glass, Bubble and Off, heard as they are focused | A |
| Menu Music | none, and no step on a card without tracks | On and Off, the music playing while On is focused | A |
| Finish | A | Zyper hops among falling confetti, "You're all set!" in his bubble, "Every button is in MENU > Controls." | A |

*(2026-11-13: Keep a Favorite comes after See the Details, and its line says
"In the details, press Y to add a favorite." Since 2026-11-08 Y on the
shelf starts a game without its resume point and Favorite is a row of the
details page X opens, and the step still taught Y on the shelf as a
favorite.)*

*(2026-11-13, after I tried 1.0.5: the step still drew a heart popping onto
the little shelf's card, where a favorite has had no heart since 2026-11-09,
and the details X opened were a panel the info screen stopped being long
ago, a cover beside the name, system, year and play time. Both are the info
screen now, in its own words, and Y does there what it does on the
handheld.)*

The demonstrations are drawn from shapes and tokens on the stage - a little
shelf of five system-colored cards (Game Boy, NES, SNES, Game Boy Color, Neo
Geo Pocket Color, by systems.cfg's accents), a stand-in game in shades of the
SNES's color, a menu and MP4 whose words are bars, the details in the info
screen's own words - never the player's
library, so the tutorial is the same on a card with no games. Each plays as a
loop half a second after its step comes on, pressing its own glyph; a press
of the player's own goes on from wherever the loop was.

- **Zyper's moves** are the menu's rig (src/zyperrig.c, 8.3, section 10), one
  copy: his breath, his blinks on the rig's own times, his hop and pant for
  something good, and for a wrong press the rig's failure - the tongue in and
  the head cocked to 18 degrees, held **hold-zyper-fail**, then settled. The
  tutorial adds only what the menu has not: popping in by scale on his
  overshoot in **zyper-pop**, and his ears swinging out and back in
  **zyper-ears**.
- **The welcome and the finish**: Zyper 300 high at (268, 372), popping in a
  **quick** after the screen - at the finish an **exit** after it, once the
  last reward's corner Zyper has gone down, so one hands over to the other -
  his tongue out in **zyper-tongue** once he is in, his ears swinging; his
  words in a bubble at x 452, 120
  high, **text** at **radius.panel** with a tail toward him, in **title**
  **ground**; two **meta** **text-soft** lines on y 392 and 440; A at (500, 536)
  and "Let's go" or "Start playing" in **menu** **text-soft**. The finish has
  the progress strip full, Zyper hopping and panting every **tour-reward**, and about
  40 bits of confetti falling and turning, in **accent** and **accent-ink**
  only: lime is success (3.3). Its A is heard from 600 ms, so the A that ends
  the last reward does not skip it.
- **Progress**: a 40 x 6 segment for each lesson and each choice, at the top center, y 32: done
  **accent-ink**, current **text**, to come **track**. In the volume and
  brightness steps the real bar covers it while it shows.
- **Stage**: 680 x 340 at (172, 84), **ground** mixed a third toward
  **surface**, **radius.panel**, a 3-unit **edge** drawn over what plays in it.
- The button glyph sits on the stage's bottom edge, centered at (512, 424).
- Title in **title** **text** on y 516, line in **meta** **text-soft** on y 578,
  an optional second line in **meta** **text-dim** on y 624. A step's stage,
  glyph and lines fade in over **move**.
- **The handheld** (a device whose profile has a drawing of its own, the
  GKD Pixel 2 and the TrimUI Brick; src/device/profile.h,
  `dev_tutorial_model`; the host's desktop borrows the Pixel 2's and shows
  it only with the Pixel 2's chords): each lesson shows
  the handheld the tutorial is running on, in a column at the left, with the lesson's
  key lit, so a player who has never seen MENU, the volume keys or POWER -
  all on its sides - finds them. *(My pick in the tutorial-buttons lab,
  2026-11-05: "Turns to the key", over a figure that faces front with the
  side keys as tabs on its edges; `docs/design/mock/tutorial-buttons-lab.html`.)*
  For it the lesson block - progress, stage, check, glyph, title and lines -
  moves right 104 units, so every x above is 104 more; Zyper's corner, his
  bubble and "START: skip" stay. A title, line or note wider than that
  leaves keeps the **inset** from the right edge instead, ending where
  "START: skip" ends (Music's line, wider than the stage, sits a little
  left of the stage's middle). On a device with no drawing (the host's
  own, beside F1 and F2) nothing moves and there is no handheld.
  src/tourdevice.c draws it in code, no image files,
  from the device's model (src/device/td_model.h: where each key is and on
  which face, the views it turns to and its kept textures' boxes; the Pixel
  2's in src/device/pixel2_model.c, the Brick's in src/device/
  brick_model.c). A lesson's view follows from the faces
  of the keys it lights: a key on a back ledge turns it round to its back,
  and with keys off the ledge too (the Brick's d-pad with L1 and R1, SELECT
  with L2 and R2) it shows their view first and then turns round to the
  back and back again, held **hold-spin** on each and turned in **spin** on
  the turn curve, both sets lit all the while (td_swing), as no one view
  shows both; a key on the top edge tilts it back, keys on both
  sides face it front with their tabs out of the edges, one side's turn it
  to that side, and the rest face front. It draws into a texture of its
  model's size (the Pixel 2's 304 x 382, the Brick's 256 x 334)
  kept while it does not change (it changes only as it
  turns, a key lights or presses, and in the first lesson's fade-in): drawn
  every frame it cost the Pixel 2 a third of the lesson's frame rate, kept
  it costs nothing measurable. `check-tour-shots` holds each lesson's lit key on its side of the
  handheld, the shoulders lit in Move Around and Music and no others, the
  figure inside its texture in every frame of every turn
  (`--handheld-box tour`), its words clear of the inset, and the block
  moved and otherwise the Brick's, in both themes.
  - **Shared with the Controls tab** (11.28, 11.45): the tutorial's handheld
    and the in-game menu's Controls tab draw the same device's model
    through `td_draw`, from a pose (the model, centre, scale, turn, tilt,
    each key's light and press) and the theme's colors, into the one kept
    texture, sized for its caller (the model's box for each). The tutorial's look is the Controls tab's: a change to the model
    changes both. Both tilt it back the same 25 degrees for the shoulders
    (the Controls tab's shoulder rows, the tutorial's Move Around and
    Music), the top face showing with the shoulders standing on it; every
    other pose is level, and a level pose draws as it did before the tilt,
    pixel for pixel.
  - **Drawn** in code from `tools/build-pixel2-model.py`'s millimetres, the
    landing page's model, in the theme's roles and nothing else: the body
    **surface** with a 3-unit **edge**, its turned side **raised** (Beard)
    or **surface** 60% toward **raised** (Salt & Pepper); the glass **ink**
    and the screen in it **ground** (Beard) or **ink** half toward
    **surface**; the keys **raised** ringed in **edge**, on Beard a step
    darker so the face reads on its light body (**raised** 45% and **edge**
    30% toward **text-dim**); SELECT and START in their pocket (**surface**
    55% toward **raised**, or 45% toward **ink**), the shoulders over the top
    edge. On the left side the volume rocker on its recess and the card slot,
    on the right MENU (round, three dots in a column: **on-accent** when
    lit, in both themes, and at rest **text-soft** on Beard and **text-dim**
    on Salt & Pepper, 4:1 or more on the key; drawn 0.5 mm and 1.45 mm
    apart where the model's are 0.3 and 1.05, so they are three dots and
    not a line on the panel - I asked for the dots to show in the dark
    theme too), POWER (the diagonal
    capsule) and the four light lenses as **edge** marks (20% toward
    **text-dim** on Beard). Pressed, a key is 92% of its size. No words
    on it (principle 5): the glyph under the stage names the key. But the
    volume keys carry a + and a -, 2.4 mm long and 0.65 thick, in the
    colors MENU's dots have, so which one turns it up shows in both
    lessons that light them *(my ask after trying 1.0.5, 2026-11-13: the
    brightness step's keys had no + and -; the model's chevrons are not
    drawn)*. The
    handheld's own green is not drawn: lime is the lit key's alone (3.3).
  - **Turned to the key**: orthographic, turned about its upright axis, its
    middle at (138, 262). The D-pad, A, B, Y, X and SELECT: 10 degrees to
    show its right side, 3.55 units a millimetre. MENU and POWER: 52 degrees
    to the right, VOL 52 degrees to the left, both at 3.95, a little nearer.
    Brightness needs MENU and VOL, on opposite sides, so it faces front at
    3.45 and those keys stand out of its edges as tabs, 2.8 mm when lit,
    the volume keys 4.6 so their + and - fit on them pressed too. Move
    Around (L1, R1) and Music (L2, R2) light shoulders on both sides, so
    they keep the front pose's 10 degrees and tilt it back 25, as seen from a
    little above, so the top shows with the lit shoulders standing up on it
    *(my ask, 2026-11-14: "can we use that same pixel 3d component on the
    tutorial page?")*. A step turns it, and tilts it, from the last lesson's
    pose in **turn**, as the step comes on; the first lesson fades it in
    with the stage.
  - **The lit key**: **accent**, ringed in **accent-ink** (Beard) or
    **accent**, a soft **accent** halo under it (0.35, 0.75 while pressed),
    and a 3-unit ring 9 units out in the same ink so a 5 mm key is found
    at a glance; VOL's ring goes round the whole rocker. It presses with the
    glyph - the loop's and the player's own, `tour_part_press` - to 92% in
    **press**, a tab back toward the edge. The new step's key lights in
    **quick** and the last one's goes out in **quick**.
  - **The shoulders** have no step of their own: each pair is a note on
    the lesson it belongs with and is lit beside that lesson's key, L1/R1
    with the D-pad in Move Around and L2/R2 with SELECT in Music *(the
    follow-up to #90, which gave them the jump and the songs)*. Facing
    front, the shoulders show only their tops over the top edge, so a lit
    one stands up out of it 2.8 mm, as MENU and VOL stand out of theirs as
    tabs for Brightness, its top corners rounded as a tab's; its halo is
    drawn before the body, so it glows from behind the edge, and its ring
    goes round what stands up. The player's own press shows on it (the
    shoulders have no glyph), back toward the edge in **press**.
  - **The TrimUI Brick** (src/device/brick_model.c): original art
    from its published outline, 73.2 x 109.9 mm, in the same roles and lit
    look. Two blocks: the thin part, 11.8 deep and the whole height (front
    corners 0.5 at the top, 4.1 at the bottom), and the thick part behind
    it from the back ledge (y 42.9 from the top) down, 19.9 deep, each
    drawn from whichever way it is turned, the farther first, its top a
    tone between its sides and its ends. The front: the glass and the
    screen in it, the d-pad with a plain middle, the F1 and F2 pills, A B X
    Y, MENU (plain: no maker's mark), SELECT (a small square) and START (a
    play triangle) in MENU's dots' colors, and two speaker grilles of dots;
    the left side the volume keys with their + and -; the right the FN
    slider (drawn, never lit: no key of the launcher's) and POWER; the top
    edge a light bar at the back, a mark as the Pixel 2's lenses are, and
    six screws in the back so it reads as one. Its shoulders, L1 L2 R2 R1
    with the USB-C housing between L2 and R2, are blocks on the ledge, which
    the thin part hides from any front view, so a lesson lighting them
    (Move Around, Music) turns it round 160 degrees, the right side's way,
    and tilts it back 20, where they stand up 1.6 mm off the ledge, L1 and
    L2 at the right; the d-pad Move Around also lights is not seen there,
    and A, B, X and Y show it in the lessons after. F1 and F2 lit together
    take one ring, as VOL's does. As tall as the Pixel 2: 2.6 units a
    millimetre facing front, 2.9 turned to a side, 2.45 at the back.
- **The choices**: after the power step's reward and before the finish,
  four steps make the handheld the player's - **Theme** (Appearance, Light
  or Dark), **Home Screen** (Spotlight, Gallery or Grid), **Sounds** (UI Sounds:
  Wood, Glass, Bubble or Off) and **Menu Music** (On or Off), the last only
  on a card with menu music to play. *(I asked for them on
  2026-11-06, "our onboarding should ask the user for which theme, display
  mode, and sounds they want", and picked in the onboarding lab: after the
  lessons, side by side, the Home Screen as the display mode, Menu Music a
  step of its own; and "have the menu music play the music when it's on in
  onboarding". `docs/design/mock/onboarding-lab.html`.)*
  - **Choosing**: a step starts on the setting as it is (at the first start
    the defaults: Light, Spotlight, Wood, On). LEFT and RIGHT move the focus
    round its choices, **move**, or **edge** on the wrap, and the focused
    choice is applied at once, to be seen and heard: the whole screen turns
    to the theme, the sounds play in the voice landed on (Off is silent),
    and the menu music plays while On is focused and fades out on Off. A
    keeps it, saved to the key MENU's row writes (`appearance`, `home`,
    `uisounds`, `bgm`), with the lessons' reward ("Saved!" in Zyper's
    bubble) and the next step after **tour-reward**. Any other button is a
    wrong press, "That's B. Try LEFT, RIGHT or A." START skips: a choice
    looked at and not kept goes back to what was saved, and a step not
    reached keeps its setting.
  - **Side by side**, no stage and no glyph: the choices themselves, each
    with its name under it, the title, the line "Press LEFT or RIGHT to look,
    A to keep it." and the note "Change it later in MENU > Appearance." (the
    row's own name) on 11.10's lines. Two choices are 380 x 285 previews at
    y 96, 56 apart (x 104 and 540); three (Home Screen) 272 x 204, 32 apart,
    on the two's middle line; the four sounds are 180 x 180 tiles at y
    130, 32 apart. A name is **meta** 48 under its choice's bottom edge,
    **text-dim**, the focused one on an **accent** pill in **on-accent**.
    The focus is a 6-unit **accent** ring 10 out from the choice, sliding to
    the next in **move** on **out**, the name's pill going with it. The
    reward's check sits on the kept choice's top right corner.
  - **The previews**: Theme shows the home screen in Spotlight in Light and
    in Dark, both, with the screen around them in the focused one; Home
    Screen shows Spotlight, Gallery and Grid in the theme that is on. They
    are the launcher's own frames of a card holding the little shelf's five
    systems (Grid's twelve, so it reads as a grid) - stand-ins, as on the
    stage, never the player's library -
    shipped as `res/tour/home-LAYOUT-THEME.png` (380 x 285) and made by
    `make tour-previews`; a 3-unit **edge** frame at **radius.row**. Sounds
    is a speaker and Menu Music a note, each drawn in **text-soft** on a
    **surface** tile with a 3-unit **edge** (Off crossed through), the
    speaker's waves pinging as its voice plays.
  - **Progress**: the strip has a segment for each choice after the
    lessons' ten, 14 (13 without tracks), filled as theirs are. On the
    Pixel 2 the choices are centered, as on the Brick: the handheld is for
    buttons, and these steps teach none.
  - Each step is a few texture copies and the words, at the panel's 60 fps:
    the sounds' and the music's tiles are drawn once a theme into textures
    (drawn every frame, the four speakers held the Pixel 2 to 41.5 fps).
- **Reward**: a 76-unit **accent** disc with an **on-accent** check at the
  stage's top right, (852, 84), appearing in **quick** (out); Zyper, 120 high,
  comes up from under the bottom edge in **move** to (96, 684), hops and pants
  once he is up, and a bubble beside him at x 168 says a cheer ("Nice!", "Got
  it!", "Woof!", "Perfect!", "Good one!") in **meta**, **ground** on
  **text**; the **reward** sound. The next step comes on by itself after
  **tour-reward** (1100 ms), and Zyper goes down in **exit** as it does.
  Between rewards the corner is empty. Up or down, he goes from wherever he is,
  so a reward that follows a wrong press does not snap him.
- **One Zyper at a time** (section 10): in the power step the stage's is the
  one until the press, and from the press the stage plays on without him and
  the corner's comes up.
- **A press of the wrong button**: **deny**, Zyper up from the bottom edge with
  the rig's failure, the right glyph pulsing twice (6%, in **tour-pulse**),
  and his bubble saying "That's X. Try Y." for **hold-short**, after which he
  goes down. In the power step the bubble says it alone, his place taken by
  the stage's Zyper. A bubble too wide to keep 16 units clear of "START: skip"
  takes its second sentence to a second line above. In the brightness step a
  volume key alone says "That's volume. Hold MENU too." ("Try F1 or F2." on
  the Brick), and MENU alone "Hold MENU, then press VOL+." START, POWER and
  the volume and brightness keys are not wrong presses, and a press in the
  first 250 ms of a step, of any key, is the last step's thumb and is not
  heard. L1/R1 are wrong presses everywhere but Move Around, where they are
  moves. L2 and R2 are wrong presses in every lesson, Music's too, whose note
  names them: no step waits for them, so Zyper answers "That's L2. Try
  SELECT." like any other misclick. The Pixel 2's device layer reads L2+R2
  together itself, outside a game, to flash the battery level on its side
  lights, in the tutorial as anywhere; that chord sends neither button, so it
  is no misclick.
- **The volume and brightness keys** work in every step, as on every screen,
  and answer their own.
- **POWER** is the tutorial's in its own step until it is pressed; anywhere
  else, and a second press during that reward, it turns the handheld off.
- **START** skips at any point, "START: skip" in **meta** **text-dim** at the
  bottom right, its right edge at 1000. After a skip at the first start, a
  one-line message (11.8), "Show Tutorial is in MENU.", holds **hold-short**
  over the shelf; any press puts it away.
- The sounds: **accept** for the welcome's and the finish's A, **move** (or
  **edge**) for the first move, **reward**, **deny**, **back** for START.
- On the GKD Pixel 2 MENU is the FUNCTION key, reported on its release, so
  the Settings step answers there; its glyph lights while it is held
  (`plat_menu_held`). Its brightness chord is MENU+VOL in the tutorial and in
  MENU > Controls alike, the names its glyphs carry. Go Back's loop shrinks a game back to its card, as this
  table always said, though in a game B is the game's and MENU leaves it; a
  truer loop would be the games shelf stepping back to the systems row, which
  is a new pick.

### 11.11 Loading screen
While a port starts. The game's cover (a game with no art, its generated
card) at equal area 368x512, centered at 272, 376, with the **focus** lift.
Beside it a column at x 536, 456 wide, its block centered on y 312: a 48x6
band in the system's mark color, the system's name in **meta** **text-dim**,
the game's name in **title** in up to three balanced lines 64 apart, and
"Starting. This can take a minute." in **meta** **text-soft**, broken after
"Starting." when it does not fit. Behind it all, the cover blurred to fill
the screen under the theme's **ground**, 70% at the top to 90% at the foot,
raised as far as the cover needs for every word on it to keep 4.5:1. The
band does not raise it: where the band is under a mark's 3:1 against what is
behind it, its color moves toward **text**, in twentieths and no further than
it must, which also gives a pale system color (Nintendo DS's silver on
Beard) its 3:1. Zyper's bounce loop stands under the column, his left edge
on its left edge (`loading.txt` x 315 y 330). No word and no part of the
cover is in his frame: only the backdrop, and the soft edge of the cover's
lift, which reaches it by a few levels at the most (7 measured). So the
splash must copy the still back under his frame before it draws each one,
not fill it with the ground.

**The port's output** (1.0.6, after a player's first start installed for five
hours behind this screen with no sign that anything ran): the latest line the
port's script and PortMaster print, at the foot, as it comes, once the screen
has been on the panel 5 s in a row, and from then on whenever it is. A quick
start shows nothing new: PortMaster's own start prints "chmod: /dev/tty0: No
such file or directory", harmless, which read as an error for the three
seconds before its window; and the screen back for a moment between its
splash and its window counts from zero again. One line in
**support** **text-dim**, from the cover's left edge to the column's right
(992), its capitals centered on the foot's 720 (`DS_FOOT_Y`), or just under
Zyper's frame when they would reach it (724 in Lexend). Its box, the font's
whole height, is held to 4.5:1 for **text-dim** as the words are, the scrim
raised for it where a cover needs it. The line is what a terminal would show:
what follows the last carriage return, escapes and other control characters
dropped, a run of spaces one space, a letter the face lacks left out; a line
too wide ends at the last whole letter that leaves room for an ellipsis. The
lines of ZyperOS's own programs (`portkeys:`, `zyhome:`, `ssh:` and `mdns:`,
which portkeys runs over a port, also where one lands in the middle of a
progress line the port has not ended yet; `port:` and `kernel:` at a line's
start) and all the log gets while the in-game menu is over the screen are
passed over. The launcher writes the letters at the panel's size (18 px), the box and
the ink (src/portload.c, `port_out_write`), the letters drawn once and kept on
the card, so a start does not wait for them; plastron's splash draws them into
its own buffer only, a few times a second while that buffer is on the panel
and not at all while anything else is (splash/src/portout.h), so a port's or
PortMaster's own window is never drawn over. A splash older than 1.0.6 shows
the screen without it.

A name too wide for its box breaks balanced, not greedy: one line when it
fits, else the fewest lines that fit and, of those, the break whose widest
line is narrowest (src/linebreak.c). A sentence set left in a list's place
(the in-game menu's Achievements tab, 11.46) is filled instead, each line as
far as it goes, so it reaches the panel's right margin. Either way, the ">"
of a way through the menus ("MENU > Downloads") stays with the word before
it: no line starts with one.

### 11.12 Header and status corner

A quiet header sits on **ground**, without a container or divider. The clock
stands at its left end, from x64, in **action** **text** with tabular digits,
where a ZyperOS wordmark was until I asked for the time there instead
(2026-11-05). The home and games pages carry no wordmark.

The status row is 24 units high, centered on y64, and right-aligned to x960.
Battery, Wi-Fi, a connected Bluetooth headset and headphones in the jack
retain the marks below. It
is one component in one layout, the top bar (src/topbar.c), and the same
header, clock at the left and marks at the right, is on every screen over a
paused game (PR #40) and every MP4 screen (2026-11-09): its shelf, an album's
tracks, Now Playing, a video with its controls and the lock screen over them,
each in its own theme. The second layout, the clock beside the marks at the
right, which Now Playing, a video's controls and MP4's shelf drew, is gone. MP4's shelf puts nothing in the
row between the clock and the marks since it is a games shelf's (11.67), and
a video with its controls puts its title there in **action**
**text**, centered, or sliding when it is longer, as a long name slides
everywhere (2026-11-18: it was cut). A background track
occupies only the space between the logo and the measured status corner. Its
music mark, two eighth notes on one beam (heads of radius 3.5, stems and
beam 2.5-unit strokes) in a 24-unit cell, uses **accent-ink**; paused
playback uses a pause's two bars, 4.5 wide. Both are drawn as the status
marks are, by signed distance (src/status.c), at the row's height and in
each theme (2026-11-09: the note of rectangles and discs before it "looks
bad"). The track uses
**chrome**, **text-soft**. A name wider than its room (at most 256) slides
back and forth inside it, the launcher's one marquee (ui_text_marquee: the
same hold, pace and faded ends as a long game title), starting over with each
new song and drawn only while it moves, the holds slept through
(ui_pingpong_wait); a name that fits stands still, centered. It never
scrolls over the clock or changes the status marks' positions. No track means
no music mark or empty capsule. Cached player state drives updates, not an
extra socket query during rendering. A track change, pause, resume or stop
requests a frame even when the rest of the home is still.

Panels stay clear of this header. Their minimum top edge is y96 when drawn
over the home or game shelf. The level bar may temporarily cover the header;
it has priority for direct volume or brightness feedback.

- **Clock.** "10:08", 12-hour with no AM or PM (the words would carry the
  corner into the systems card), or "22:08" and "09:05" when MENU > Clock is
  24-hour. None while the clock reads before 2026: a Pixel 2 never set keeps
  2018.
- **Wi-Fi**, 36 wide: a wedge of radius 8 and arcs at 14 and 22, 45 degrees
  each side of straight up, 4-unit strokes as the bar's speaker waves. The
  lit parts **text-soft** and the rest **track**: all three at -60 dBm or
  better, two to -70, one under, from `wpa_cli signal_poll` every 2 s
  (My call: a second fork beside the state's, never in the same frame,
  since neither kernel fills `/proc/net/wireless`); all three when the
  driver cannot say. On but not connected (no network, or one joined with no
  address yet), the whole fan in **text-dim**, crossed by a 4-unit slash cut
  3 units out of it. Off, or no radio (a Pixel 2 with no adapter): no mark.
- **Bluetooth**, 16 wide: the rune, a 3-unit stroke in **text-soft**, on the
  Brick while a headset is connected.
- **Headphones**, 24 wide, between the rune and the fan, while a cable is in
  the jack (the codec's SW_HEADPHONE_INSERT): a headband of radius 9, a
  3-unit stroke as the rune's, its ends inside two cups 6 by 10, in
  **text-soft** (2026-11-09). Audio Channels' Auto plays stereo there.
- **File Transfer**, 24 wide, left of the others while it runs behind the
  shelf (11.23): an arrow up and one down, 2.5-unit strokes, in **text-dim**
  with nothing moving and **text-soft** while files move; then a 2-unit ring
  of radius 11 round them in **track**, filled clockwise from the top in
  **accent-ink** as far as the upload on its way. Not while its own screen is
  up. *(My pick in the File Transfer 2 lab, 2026-11-06: "A mark in the
  corner".)*
- **A download**, 24 wide, right of the USB plug, while System Update
  downloads or installs behind the screen (11.17), or a Scraper or
  Achievements run goes on behind it (11.19): an arrow down into a tray in
  **text-soft**, in File Transfer's ring, swept as far as the download, or
  as far as the runs have got together. Not on the page that shows it.
- **USB plug**, 24 wide, leftmost, on the Pixel 2 while something on the
  USB port waits for an answer to What did you plug in? (11.36): a plug
  pointing up, its metal end with two slots over a body of radius 2.5 and a
  1.25-unit cable under it, in **accent-ink**: something waits for you.
  MENU > USB Device asks again. *(2026-11-10.)*
- **Battery**, 44 wide: a 40 x 24 body in a **frame** outline with
  **radius-chip** corners, a 3 x 8 tip, and a fill up to 28 x 12, at least 4
  wide while there is any charge. **text-soft**; **warning**, outline and
  fill, at 10% and under off the charger, the fill blinking a second on and a
  second off at 5%, the same as Diatom's disc over a game; charging, the fill
  in **accent-ink** and a bolt over the body, cut out of it by its own
  half-stroke; on the cable and held at the charge limit (11.26), a plug in
  **text** in the bolt's place, cut out the same way: two prongs, its body
  and its cord. It replaces the low-battery dot.
- **Battery Icon** (MENU, under Home Screen): **Bars**, the default, is the
  mark alone; **Percentage** writes the charge, "82%", 8 units left of the
  mark in the clock's font and tabular digits, in the mark's own color
  (**text-soft**, or **warning** when it is low), so the clock and the marks
  before it move left by its width *(2026-11-05: "show percentage or
  just bars")*; **Time Left** writes the time in the same place the same
  way, "3 h 25 min" *(2026-11-07)*. Left and Right go round the three, A
  forward. Saved as `battery_icon` ("bars", "percentage" or "time") in the
  device's database.
  Off the cable the time is `src/battery_eta.h`'s: the gauge reads whole
  percent, so it is read from how long a percent takes to fall, as an
  exponential average over 10 minutes weighted by each fall's length, with
  the part of a percent already used since the last fall counted (it counts
  down with the clock) and a fall that is late slowing the rate as it waits.
  It is the time to BATT_EMPTY_PCT, where the launcher saves and powers off,
  not to 0%, and it is shown to the minute under 10, to 5 minutes under 10
  hours and to the hour after, a number changing when it means something. It
  starts after two falls (the first only marks where a percent began): a few
  minutes after a start, longer at a light load, so the first number is the
  gauge's own *(2026-11-08, from a first try on the Pixel 2: "after a while
  the remaining time appeared")*: `charge_now` over `current_avg`, less the
  charge under BATT_EMPTY_PCT, read through `plat_charge_read` every 2 s
  from the first read. The falls' number is blended in by a weight that
  rises in a line over 20 minutes from the first rate, so the shown time
  does not jump and the better long-run number takes over; a gauge with no
  current file (the Brick, a simulator) gives the falls alone. The cable, a
  gauge that rises with no cable and more than a day clear the falls. On the
  cable the gauge's straight line (the charge to the Charge Limit, or to
  full, over the current going in; 50 mA or more) is blended over 30 s into
  the charging screen's time to full (`src/charging.h`, CC then CV, after its
  30 s settle). `batt_corner_kind` says what the corner writes: the time;
  three dots filling in turn (STATUS_CALC_W wide, a step every 400 ms,
  asking for a frame only for the first minute of them) while there is no
  time yet and one is coming, in **text-soft** and **track** as the fan's lit
  and unlit parts; "On hold" held at the Charge Limit, and "Charged" when
  full, in the time's place beside the plug or the bolt *(2026-11-08)*; and
  the percentage only where none can come (a charger whose gauge cannot say
  its current), so the corner is never without a number or a word. Up to
  `STATUS_PCT_MAX` (160) units are kept for the text; the corner asks for a
  frame each minute while it shows a time, as the clock does.

The games title lives below the cover row (11.1), separate from the header.
Status marks are cached in a small texture when their values change; the
clock requests a frame at the next minute. Cable changes use the shared
200 ms charger cache while capacity remains on a five-second interval.

The whole header stands in the same place on every MP4 screen and on every
screen over a paused game - its menu, the save slots, the achievements (
2026-11-04), where the dot was: on MP4's screens in the theme, over a paused
game in Salt & Pepper, as scrim.game is dark in both.

MENU > Clock, under Date & Time: **12-hour** (the default) or **24-hour**, for
the corner and for Date & Time's own row and Hour.

---

### 11.13 About ZyperOS
Four read-only pages, Left/Right to change them, round from the last to the
first. The first keeps Version, Address, Battery and Awake for. The second
shows Storage used, Storage free, Storage total, Battery health, Full charge
and Design charge. The third, headed **The name**, is the README's story of
it: Zyper, my dog, who passed away, and zippy and hyper, signed Tarik;
laid out as a synopsis is, but it holds still: it opens at the top, **Up**/**Down**
scroll it a line at a time (held, they repeat) and it stops at the top and at
the bottom, with the same soft edge a scrolling card has wherever more text
is hidden, so the top has none and the bottom has one until the end (11's
synopsis rule is for a synopsis, which still walks by itself). **Left**/**Right**
still turn the page. The last, headed **Thanks**, is one page walked by hand
as The name is (**Up**/**Down** a line at a time, held they repeat, stopping
at the top and the bottom, the same soft edges): the README's Credits as
rows, who on the left and what of theirs on the right, short enough that no
row scrolls sideways, Eric Reinsmidt first; then, under a rule and the
heading **Help with other handhelds**, the people who help with the other
handhelds ZyperOS runs on, a name and the handheld each (src/about_screen.c's
`ABOUT_HANDHELD_THANKS`, shown only while it has a row); and held at the
foot, under the footer's rule, the note "All licenses are in Licenses/".
Every page uses the standard menu panel and one measured width. The **meta**
**text-dim** page count, "1 / 4" to "4 / 4",
sits at the right of the panel's heading, with space reserved around the
centered title. No page text floats over the shelf. No row changes a
setting. Host shots: `--about 0` to `3`, and `--about-scroll F` (0 to 1) for the name's page or the thanks scrolled F of the way down. *(I picked Details, then asked
to put its page information inside the panel, 2026-11-04. On 2026-11-06 he
asked for the page on the name, a tribute to his dog, and a thank-you page to
the open-source work ZyperOS is built on. On 2026-11-07 I asked for it to stop scrolling by itself, so I can scroll it. On 2026-11-16 I asked for the thanks to be one page that scrolls, like the name's, so I can thank those who help me with other handhelds there.)*

Storage is the mounted card's filesystem in decimal GB. Free is the available
space; used excludes reserved blocks. Capacity comes from the battery
controller's charge_full and charge_full_design in microamp-hours, displayed
as whole mAh with grouped thousands. Health is their ratio, rounded to a whole
percent and capped at 100, explicitly "100% estimate" rather than a measured
wear claim. Missing, zero or invalid capacity readings show "unknown". On the
GKD Pixel 2 the full charge was only ever the design's 1730 mAh, so health was
100% on any battery. Its kernel now measures it (plastron's
9-px2-rk817-wear.patch): a full charge marks the gauge's counter, and the next
start after 30 minutes or more off, at 50% or under at rest, compares what went
out with that level and moves the full charge half way to the measure, kept in
the PMIC over updates, slots and cards. It says how many times it measured
(charge_full_learned); until the first, Battery health and Full charge both
read "Not measured yet", not a 100% that says nothing. The Brick's gauge does
not say, and shows the estimate as before. *(On 2026-11-19 I asked whether
updates lose the battery's health; they did not: it had never been measured.)* Storage
and capacity refresh every two seconds while About is open. The charge row
uses the shared battery cache and follows the charging corner's timing.

### 11.14 MP4
The media player, Muse until it played video (2026-11-05): "MP4" on
its card, its Controls page, the tutorial and every line that names it, and
in the code from 1.1.0: `mp4` in its names, the daemon `mp4d` on
/tmp/mp4d.sock, its settings under `mp4.*`. A handheld's `muse.*` settings move
there once, at its first start (src/mp4keys.h). A book's and a video's places
(`book.*`, `video.*`) and the shelf's tag, `MUSE` (a card's `.last`, its sort
and view settings, the system art), keep their names, so a card's saved places
and modes still read.

- **Shelf.** One card, three kinds: MENU > Show steps All, Music,
  Audiobooks and Videos, only the kinds the card has, and is there with two
  or more. All, where it starts (2026-11-09, "Tudo"), is every album, book and
  video on one shelf, mixed: by Folder, the folders' names (a loose film's
  own), or by Title; its count row is Total, and each card carries the art
  badge (11.1) of its kind: an album, a book or a video. A film straight in
  `Videos/` is a card of its own and A plays it; a folder of videos lists its
  files like an album. A video's cover is a picture beside it with its name, else one of its
  own frames (src/mp4d/cover.c). On videos, Sort By is Folder or Title and
  there is no Album Art; Shuffle All and Album Art are on music, and on All
  when the card has songs. MENU > View and START show the shelf in Covers,
  Grid or List (11.67).
- **Video.** On Now Playing, one view, as a phone's or a TV's player
  (1.0.6; before, two views Y turned between, the smaller one giving up
  nothing to the keys but its frame): the picture as wide as the screen at
  16:9 under the header's band (the clock, the title in its row, the marks)
  on **ground**, the same size in every language; over its foot, on
  **surface** at 92%, the bar with the time played and the time left at its
  two ends on one row, and the keys under it on every screen's foot. The band
  is as tall as the keys need: a language whose words take a second row
  makes it taller, never the picture smaller. The controls come up on any
  key, which also does what it does (the first press too, as a remote's
  would), stay for **hold-controls** after the last press and for as long
  as it is paused, in over **quick** and out over **fade-out**. Y turns
  **Repeat** (MP4's MENU > Repeat on Videos and All: Off, One, All and
  round), kept, and brings the controls up as any key does, Repeat's mark
  beside the time when it is not Off; the keys say "Y Repeat" between X and
  L1/R1 (2026-11-19: "Make the y button change it"; it showed or hid the
  controls before). The header goes and comes with them, at the
  same pace, and the ground around the picture eases with them from
  **ground** to pure black (#000): hidden, there is the picture and black,
  as a TV's player has it (2026-11-18: "when the footer times out and
  disappears while video is playing, the top bar should too, and the
  background should go pitch black"). Then, the surround gone, the picture
  grows to the whole screen over **turn**, its shape kept and black only
  where its shape is not the screen's, so a 4:3 film fills the Pixel 2
  (2026-11-19: "if we could make the video go full size when the surround
  collapses that's better"); on a key it goes back to its box over
  **quick** before the surround comes back over **quick**, so the header
  never stands over the picture. A 16:9 film is the same size either way
  (its box is the screen's middle) and waits for neither. The title in the
  header's row slides when it is wider than its room, from its start each
  time the header comes back, and is drawn as it moves, paused too. Paused
  or stopped, a play disc (`ds_play_disc`) stands in the picture's middle,
  its triangle centered by its centroid (UI_GLYPH_PLAY is drawn so; it sat
  a sixteenth of the disc right before, 2026-11-19). Under the lock the
  controls stay hidden, and with them the header, the picture whole on
  black: no key they name works there.
  **Too big, or no picture.** The daemon judges as it plays whether this
  CPU carries the video: more than a quarter of its pictures dropped in two
  stretches of 2 s running and it says the video is too big, two stretches
  under a tenth and it takes that back; the first stretch after an open, a
  seek, a pause or the screen coming back is not judged, as the decoder
  catches up then on any file (src/mp4d/vidtry.h). A format nothing here
  decodes, or a decoder that gives not one picture, it says there is no
  picture. MP4 says so in words rather than
  show a slide show or a black box (2026-11-19: "If a size cannot keep up,
  MP4 must say so plainly"): the notice's slab (11.7) centered over the
  picture's foot, **inset** over the band, what the video is ("AV1 ·
  1920x1080 · 60 fps", **meta** **text-dim**) over what that means in
  **label** **text** ("Too big to play smoothly here.", "This video's
  picture cannot play here."), on one line or two balanced (Chinese and
  Japanese set their own break), no wider than a menu panel. Too big, the
  controls come up as on a key when it is first said, and the notice comes
  and goes with them; no picture, it stands in the black whatever the
  controls do. With dav1d on the Pixel 2 (2026-11-19), AV1 plays whole up to
  1440x1080 at 24 fps, 1280x720 at 60 and 1920x1080 at 30; 1920x1080 at 60
  is too big.
  The keys are hints as the shelves draw them (`page_hint`: a glyph and a word,
  **chrome** type, **text-soft**), from x 64 to 960 with the room shared out, and
  on a second row above where a language's words need it, never cut: A, B,
  X, L1/R1 (a folder of more than one), the d-pad with its sides lit
  (11.4). Now Playing's music view keeps Y (its mode or a book's speed).
  The picture is drawn under the screen at the panel's own resolution
  (plat_video_under), so nothing scales it twice. A video keeps its place
  as a book does, per file; none is kept in its first or last minute.
- **Lock screen.** X on any MP4 screen while something plays, **SELECT** and
  **START** pressed together there (2026-11-09: one gesture locks and
  unlocks), or MENU > Lock Screen. SELECT alone still closes MP4, as it is
  let go rather than pressed, since it may be the first of the pair. Every
  button is blocked, POWER too, but the volume keys; **SELECT** and **START**
  held together for **hold-unlock** unlock. The lock screen - Now Playing
  under the menu scrim, and a panel 720 wide, wider only for a line no break
  fits: the padlock in **accent-ink**, "Locked" in **title**, "Hold (SELECT)
  + (START) to unlock" in **meta** **text-dim** with the glyphs of 11.9, on
  one line where it fits the panel less 48 each side and else on two, broken
  where they come out most even ("Segure (SELECT) + (START)" over "para
  desbloquear"), and the bar that fills while the pair is held - shows for
  **hold-lock-lit** as it locks, again for as long at every press of a
  blocked button (2026-11-09), and while the pair is held. Music turns the
  backlight off between. The volume keys step
  the volume and show the bar of 11.6 as anywhere; with music they light the
  lock screen with the bar over it for the bar's life (**hold-bar** after
  the last press, then **fade-out**), a key held keeping it lit, and then it
  is dark again. A video stays in sight, and the bar comes over the picture
  with no panel.
- **Shuffle All.** MENU on the music shelf: every song on the card, the mode
  set to shuffle and kept.
- **Player Look.** MENU on the music shelf, under Shuffle All: how Now
  Playing shows a song. Cover, the cover large as it always was, is the
  default; the others are 11.64's.
- **Repeat.** MP4's MENU on the shelves with videos (Videos, and All):
  videos' own play mode, **Off** (each once, in order, as before), **One**
  (the video playing, again and again) or **All** (its folder, round
  again), never shuffled; left and right step it round, A opens the three,
  it applies to a video playing now and is kept (`video.mode`), and music's
  mode, Y's on Now Playing, is left as it is. The video's controls carry
  music's mark for it before the time left, at music's size (half again the
  times' line, so repeat one's 1 reads), and none for Off. A video looping
  holds Auto Off as music does, so the screen stays on while it plays
  (2026-11-19: loop playback, to leave a film playing as a decoration). Y in
  the video player stays the controls' key.
- **Quit.** The last row of MP4's MENU on every shelf (music, books, videos,
  All), from the shelf, an album's tracks or Now Playing: MP4 closes, as
  SELECT closes it, to the screen it was opened from (the home, a menu, a
  paused game's menu). **X** does it from any row of that MENU, heard as A
  is, and the row carries X's glyph at its right (`MENU_KEY_X`, 11.9), as the
  in-game menu's Quit and its X (11.28). Like SELECT it leaves what plays
  playing, heard behind the home as above; A pauses it first for silence.
  *(2026-11-18: "add an option in the mp4 menu to quit it, like we have in
  games (with the shortcut too)".)*
- **From the shelf.** While MP4 is heard behind the home or a games shelf
  (music, a book or a video's sound, playing; the menu music is quiet for
  it), **L2**/**R2** do what **L1**/**R1** do on Now Playing: the track
  before and the next, or a book's chapter where its file has them, and
  the song pop-up (11.22) says what plays now. Paused or stopped, they are
  the menu music's again. *(The follow-up to #90.)*

*(My picks, 2026-11-05: Now Playing keeps its large cover (A over a
wider text column), video opens full screen (C over the view with
controls), the lock stays dark until unlocked, and SELECT + START unlocks
it, over L1 + R1. 2026-11-06: the volume keys work while locked, lighting
the screen only as long as the bar shows.)*

### 11.15 File Transfer page
The web page Wi-Fi File Transfer and USB File Transfer serve
(`res/web/`), in a browser on a phone or a computer. It wears Beard, or Salt
& Pepper where the browser asks for dark, and every value is a token: the
colors are 3's roles, the radii 6's at the Pixel 2's 0.625 (panel 18, card
14, row 9, key 8), the type 4's roles with **menu** at 16 px (**title** 20,
**label** 18, **meta** 13.5, held there so a phone reads it), the motion 8's
curves and times; `tools/designdef.py` writes them into the page's token
block. *(My picks in the File Transfer lab, 2026-11-05:
`docs/design/mock/transfer-lab.html`.)*

- **The PIN**: a **panel** over the ground, Zyper over the heading, four
  boxes of one digit (fields: **ground** in an **edge** line, **accent-ink**
  when focused). The page goes to the device as the fourth digit lands; a
  paste fills all four. Zyper breathes and blinks at rest, shuts his eyes
  while the PIN is typed *(My note)*, cocks his head 18 degrees at a
  wrong one for **hold-zyper-fail** while the digits stand in **warning**,
  and hops at the right one.
- **Wide** (880 px and more): a **surface** sidebar with the ZyperOS lockup,
  the card folders as rows, each with its pixel art at 30 (the open one is the
  **accent** fill, as the focused row on the handheld), the card's room, the
  uploads in a card, SSH's command over the cable, and **Settings** at the
  foot with a line mark. The listing is one **surface** panel of rows a thumb
  tall, in a column no wider than 960.
- **Narrow**: the folders are pill chips over the list, Upload is an
  **accent** button at the bottom right with **focus-small**'s shadow, the
  uploads fold to one line along the bottom that opens into a sheet, and
  Settings is behind a **⋯** at the top.
- **Rows**: a game's cover, an album's, an artist's four; a folder's pixel
  art; or a file's line mark; the name; the size in **meta** **text-dim**; a
  **⋯**. Under the pointer a row is **raised** (its size steps up to
  **text-soft**, since **text-dim** misses 4.5:1 on Salt & Pepper's raised);
  keyboard focus is the **accent** fill with **on-accent**.
- **Folder art** *(My pick, File Transfer 2 lab, 2026-11-06: "Pixel art
  on a tile")*: every folder wears the shelf's pixel art (`res/system-icons`,
  the 128-unit still, drawn at 40), on a 46 x 46 tile with **radius.row**
  corners: the system's color (or Folder Color's, 3.5) at 14% over
  **surface**, ringed at 30%; a folder of no system on the same tile in
  **text-dim**. A system's folder in ROMs and
  Save States has its own picture - every system the card knows, not only
  the shelves with games; the card's folders theirs (ROMs a cartridge, Music a
  vinyl album, Audiobooks a book with headphones, Videos a video tape, Menu
  Music a cassette, BIOS a chip, Saves a memory card, Save States a floppy
  disk); any other folder the small folder drawn for rows.
  All are ZyperOS's own system art (`res/system-art/original`). A picture
  that does not come leaves the line mark on the tile.
- **Hidden folders**: a name that starts with a dot (`.media`, where covers
  go) is not listed, unless Settings' **Show hidden folders** is on.
- **The uploads** *(pick: "One line and one bar")*: the heading, "Sending 3
  of 12", over one **meta** **text-dim** line, what is left in size and time,
  "4.2 GB left · about 6 min", and an 8-high bar for the whole queue in
  **accent-ink** on **track**; each file keeps its own bar under it. The time
  is the bytes left over the speed of the last 20 seconds, its words changed
  at most once a second; none until there are three seconds to go by; under
  a minute, seconds in fives. On a phone the bottom line says "3 of 12 ·
  about 6 min". When the handheld stops after the file on its way (11.23),
  the line says so in **text-soft**, the files that stay say "not sent", and
  the panel stays until the next batch.
- **The room** *(pick: "Send what fits")*: under the folders, "88.4 GB free
  of 119 GB", the size in **text-soft** SemiBold, and a 6-high meter of how
  full the card is in **text-dim** on **track**. The free is what files may
  have: the handheld keeps 100 MB for saves, states and the databases
  (`HARE_RESERVE`). A batch that does not fit is asked about before a byte
  goes: "Not enough room on the card" and what it needs against what is free,
  with "Send the 9 that fit" (the files that fit, in their order) or Cancel;
  with none that fits, a note and OK.
- **Settings** *(pick: "A page")*: a page in the main area, opened from the
  sidebar's foot (a phone's **⋯**) as a folder is, at `#settings`. A
  **surface** panel per thing, 18 x 20 inside, **label** SemiBold headings:
  Update ZyperOS (over the cable only), Box art and album covers (over the
  cable only), SteamGridDB, The card (its room, and Show hidden folders, a
  switch: **track**, **accent** when on, the knob **surface**), and Help (the
  logs). Each shows only where it works here.
- **Update ZyperOS** *(pick: "Download, then drop")*: "ZyperOS 0.4.1 is out.
  You have 0.4.0." in **text-soft**, then two numbered steps (30 circles in
  **raised**, the done one **accent**): download the update's files from
  GitHub, a button; drop them on a dashed **edge** zone, or choose them, the
  names it waits for ticked in **accent-ink** as they come. Then one bar
  with what is left and the time, the handheld's own count while it checks
  and installs, and "Restart the handheld". An image with no release key
  says updates through the computer are off, and why, in a **ground** box.
  docs/updates.md says how it works.
- **Menus**: one per row's **⋯**, a right-click, and the narrow **⋯**: a
  **surface** pop-up with the **panel** lift beside what opened it on a wide
  screen, a sheet from the bottom over **scrim.menu** on a narrow one. They
  open in **move** and close in **exit**. Delete is in **warning**, ringed
  rather than filled under the pointer, and always asks first.
- **Dialogs** (rename, new folder, a delete's question): a **panel** over
  **scrim.menu**, centered on a wide screen and a sheet on a narrow one; the
  yes is the **accent** button, or a **warning** one for a delete.
- **Toasts**: **raised**, in **quick**, held **hold-short** or **hold-long**
  by length, a failure in **warning** on **surface**.
- **The SteamGridDB key**: in Settings, one line saying what is known about
  it ("Key ••••1a2b. It works.", not checked yet, refused, checking) over
  "Add a key" or "Change key" and Remove. Add opens the dialog with a field
  to paste into; Remove asks first, as a delete does.
- Lime is a fill only for the open folder, a focused row or item, and the
  one button that matters (Upload, the yes); as a mark it is **accent-ink**:
  a progress bar, a field's focus, the frame while a file is dragged over.

- **Moving** (2026-11-13: "we should be able to drag files around to
  organize them"): a row dragged onto a folder's row, or onto a crumb of a
  folder above, moves there on the card (`/api/move`). A mouse lifts it once
  it has gone 6 px; a finger once held still 450 ms, so a swipe still
  scrolls. What is lifted fades to 45% and a chip in **accent** with
  **on-accent** words follows the pointer: its name, or "3 items"; a row or
  crumb that takes it wears the drop's **raised** fill and **accent-ink**
  ring, as a file dropped from the computer does. **Select** in a row's ⋯,
  or Shift, Ctrl or Cmd with a press, picks rows (a **raised** fill and an
  **accent-ink** bar at the left), and one of them dragged takes the others.
  A loose `.cue`, `.gdi`, `.toc` or `.m3u` takes the files it names beside
  it, and the files kept by its stem (src/sheet.h); a folder goes whole. A
  move stays in its card folder (ROMs, Music...), and a folder moves only
  where a delete would take it whole (xfer_delete_rule): a console's own
  folder, or one in BIOS or Saves, stays where the shelf and the cores look.
  A name already in the folder refuses the whole move, and the toast says
  which ("Contra.nes is already in Hacks").
- **A console's own folder** (1.0.6 rc.3, "Can we not allow moving console
  folders in the file transfer portal?"): one at the top of ROMs or Save
  States named as a system is (systems.cfg, xfer_console; the listing says
  `"console":true`) is never moved, renamed or deleted from the page,
  empty or not. Its ⋯ holds Open and, under a rule, one line in **meta**
  **text-dim**: "Console folders stay where they are, so ZyperOS can find
  their games." (web.console.kept). A drag lifts nothing and a pick, Shift,
  Ctrl or Cmd with a press, or a finger held still picks nothing: the toast
  says the same line. Select all and Ctrl or Cmd+A leave it out. The device
  refuses the three itself (a delete's rule is **no** for it, and a rename
  asks that rule too), so an old page or a script cannot. Everything in it
  still moves, renames and deletes, a person's own folders of games too;
  a folder at the top of ROMs that is no system's keeps its old rules.
- **Picking several** (1.0.6, Discord: "tick several ROMs and move them to
  another folder"): once a row is picked - **Select**, Ctrl or Cmd with a
  press, or a finger held still for the lift's 450 ms and let go where it is -
  the list is picking. A press on any row picks or unpicks it (Shift with a
  press, the rows from the last one picked); a folder does not open. Each
  row's ⋯ and › give way to a tick: a ring of **edge**, filled **accent** with
  an **on-accent** check when picked. A bar on the **surface** with the small
  lift is held to the foot of the window under the list (on a phone, fixed
  where Upload is, which goes, in two lines): ✕, "3 selected · 328 KB" (the
  size when only files are picked), Select all (gone once all are), **Move
  to folder** in **accent** and a bin. Escape or ✕ puts them all down, as
  another folder does; Ctrl or Cmd+A picks every row and Delete asks about
  the picks. **Delete** asks once, with what the folders among them hold
  (each counted by `/api/count`, the one-row rules: a folder that may not go
  whole stops it, with why).
- **Move to folder** (the bar, and a row's ⋯ for one): a dialog of one height
  (600 px, 82% of a phone's height as a sheet) whatever it holds, so it does
  not jump as folders are walked: the heading ("Move 3 items to"), crumbs,
  the folders of the place shown as rows with their pixel art and ›, a note,
  and New folder, Cancel and the yes, which names where ("Move to Game
  Boy"). It opens at the folder above theirs, scrolled to their own, which
  says "current folder"; ROMs itself, the folder they are in and any folder
  inside one being moved are no place to go: the yes is off and the note
  says why. A folder being moved is dimmed and cannot be walked into. New
  folder asks for a name, makes it there and opens it. The moves are
  `/api/move` one by one, as a drag's. After a move (a drag's too) the picks
  go down and a toast says what went where ("Moved 3 items to Game Boy",
  "Moved Contra.nes to Hacks") with **Undo** at its end (**text**, bold
  and underlined: **accent-ink** misses 4.5:1 on **raised** in Beard),
  held three times **hold-long** and not while the pointer is on it; Undo
  moves each back to the folder it came from (a disc set's folder and a
  `.cue` with its tracks go back as they went) and says "Moved back.". A
  move that was refused says why instead, with no Undo. No copy: a copy is a second full copy
  on the same card, and the page's server runs in the launcher's loop, so a
  disc image would hold File Transfer for minutes.

### 11.16 Hotspot screen
Wi-Fi File Transfer when the handheld is its own network (src/hotspot.h): a
screen of its own on the **ground**, not a panel over the shelf. It opens and
closes as a panel does (8.2), the whole screen being the panel: from 96% and
clear to full over the dimmed shelf in **move**, back in **exit**. Everything
starts 48 from the left (6 grid steps). The title, **title** **text**, on the
line at 56; under it at 136 the one instruction, "Scan to join. The page opens
on your phone.", **meta** **text-dim**. From 208 down the left, the QR code
that joins the network; 64 to its right a column of three entries 120 apart
from 216 - Network, Password and PIN - each a **meta** **text-dim** name over
its value in **menu** **text** (the PIN in **title**), digits tabular. Under
the column at 584 and 632, two **meta** lines, the name **text-soft** and the
value right-aligned **text-dim** at 976: Phones (`waiting`, `1 joined`) and
Now, the transfer's news, which scrolls when it is too wide (principle 8).
The key legend "B: close" is centered at the foot (11.4), at a page's foot
height (720, DS_FOOT_Y, every screen's foot; 718 until 2026-11-14, 704
until one foot, 1.0.6), "B: keep going or stop" while files move (11.23). No status corner, no Zyper. src/hotpanel.c draws it; tools/qr-check.c reads its code back.

- **The code.** One module is 12 units, half again the grid step, so the
  code is 444 units with its quiet zone of four modules: 5.5 cm on the Pixel
  2, read from arm's length. On the panel that is 7.5 pixels a module, so
  every other module's edge falls between two pixels; zbarimg reads a shot
  scaled to 640x480. Ink (**ink**) on a tile of Beard's **surface** in both
  themes, since a phone's camera reads dark on light, with **radius.row**
  corners and the 3-unit **edge** frame. The text is the camera's Wi-Fi form,
  `WIFI:T:WPA;S:<name>;P:<password>;;`; at level M it is version 3 (29
  modules).
- **The network** is `ZyperOS-` and the last two bytes of the adapter's
  address in capitals: one handheld keeps one name. **The password** is new
  each time the screen opens: two groups of four lowercase letters and digits
  without look-alikes (0 o 1 l i), `q7mx-3kpa`.
- **The phone's sheet.** In a phone's sign-in sheet only, a note sits at the
  top of the transfer page, **surface** over an **edge** line: what the sheet
  cannot do in **text** SemiBold, and the two steps to the real browser in
  **text-soft** with the address in **accent-ink** (res/web/captive.js).

*(My picks in the hotspot lab, docs/design/mock/hotspot-lab.html,
2026-11-05: "A whole screen", the adapter-tagged name, letters and digits, a
note at the top. The Now line is this document's: the screen today says what
the transfer is doing, and the lab's whole screen had room for it.)*

### 11.17 System Update
MENU > System Update, just above About ZyperOS, on a card that has the
update program (docs/updates.md). Its value is the installed version, or the
news: "0.2.0 available", "downloading", "restart to update". The screen is a
full page on **ground** with the wash, as the tutorial's: "System Update" in
**label** **text-soft** centered at y 66; Zyper at rest, 150 tall, centered
on (158, 232); the versions in **title** **text** from x 270 on y 200,
"0.1.0" and "0.2.0" with a drawn arrow between them in **text-dim** (the
faces have no U+2192), or the installed version alone; under them at y 270
one line in **meta** **text-dim** (what downloads and the whole folder, "Up
to date · checked 9:41", "Looking for updates…"), **warning** when it says
something went wrong. Then What's New: the release notes' points whole, in
**meta**, filled a word at a time onto lines of 804 from x 130 (a point's
lead, its words before the first ": ", in **text**, the rest in
**text-soft**), a **text-dim** dot at each point's first line, from y 348
(362 under a two-line line) every 42, 14 more between points, down to y 548
over a bar or a line, 640 when there is none; up and down scroll it a line
at a time, and a small **text-dim** triangle at the right says there is
more above or below. *(2026-11-11: the first release's points were cut at
two lines, and at 110 characters by the release tool.)* While it works, one bar from
x 90 to 934 at y 572, 18 tall, round, **track** with **accent-ink** over it,
and its two words under it at y 626 in **meta** **text-dim** ("Downloading"
and "6.1 of 14 MB"); when there is no bar, one centered **meta** line at
y 600 in its place ("Or it goes in at the next start.", "Use the ZyperOS
Installer."). A problem (the download did not finish, the battery is low, the
card is full, a version did not start) is said in full where What's New goes,
which steps aside: **menu** **text**, up to three balanced lines from y 348
every 52, with an 8-wide **warning** bar at their left; the line under the
versions stays short ("Back on 0.1.0"). *(2026-11-05: the error was
hard to read as a cut **meta** line in **warning**.)* The buttons at the foot, centered on y 714: 11.9's glyph at 48
and its word in **meta** **text-soft**, 40 between pairs. A is the page's
action (Download, Continue, Restart Now, Look Again, Try Again), B is Back
("Continue in background" while it works; "Later" when it is ready), Y is "Roll
Back to 0.0.9" when the image allows one. Opened with a network and no look
in the last ten minutes, it looks at once. It also looks each time Wi-Fi
connects and once a day, and a release it finds is said once, by Zyper's
bubble on the shelf (11.23's, "ZyperOS 1.0.4 is ready to download" over
"Open MENU > System Update.", the lines balanced onto two each, no
