thermometer) or as a notice over a game. A download goes on behind every
screen and game: the corner shows it (11.12, an arrow into a tray in File
Transfer's ring, swept as far as the download), Auto Off waits for it, and
when it is in, Zyper says "ready to install" the same way. One cut short (Wi-Fi
gone, the handheld off) goes on by itself from where it stopped once
Wi-Fi is back, and the page says so: "It goes on from where it stopped
when Wi-Fi is back." Every state's words are
in tools/sysupdate-check.c. *(My picks in the update lab, 2026-11-05:
its own row, the full page, once a day on Wi-Fi.)*

### 11.18 Computer Connected
The question the shelf asks when a computer is plugged into the Pixel 2,
whose USB port only charges until then (src/usblink.h). Plugged in with a
menu open, the menus close back to the shelf, which asks at once
(usblink_wants_ask, 2026-11-12: the question had waited, unseen, for the
menu to be closed by hand); over a game's or a port's menu nothing closes,
and the shelf asks once the game is left. Three tiles side by
side on a panel (11.3's slab, **elevation.panel**, over **scrim.menu**),
heading **Computer Connected** in **label** **text-soft** over an **edge**
rule. Each tile is **raised**, **radius.card**, 272 x 232, 24 apart: its
mark (a bolt, a folder with an arrow up out of it, a terminal's prompt),
72 across, and its name in **menu** on two lines, in **text-soft**; the
focused tile is the **accent** fill with both in **on-accent**. The key
button hints (11.4) "< > Move   A Choose   B Charge Only" are under the
tiles in **meta** **text-dim**. The tiles are **Charge Only**, **File
Transfer** and, on a development image, **Developer (SSH)**; it starts on
Charge Only, so a quick A keeps the port charging. B, MENU, or the cable
coming out mean Charge Only. Asked once per plug, over the shelf when it
is lit, never over a game; a wall charger is never asked. Developer (SSH)
answers with a one-line message (11.8): "Developer link on. Unplug to end
it." Opens and closes as every panel does (8.2). A host shot:
`--shot out.png --usb-ask [N]`, tile N focused. *(My picks in the USB
lab, `docs/design/mock/usb-lab.html`, 2026-11-05: Three tiles, starting on
Charge Only.)*

**Flash Drive** (2026-11-11; each language's everyday word for a USB
stick: "Pen drive", "Clé USB", "USB-Stick", "USBメモリ"), UI_GLYPH_USB.
With it the tiles go two to a row, a grid of TILE_W2 (408) by 164: Charge
Only and File Transfer, then Flash Drive and, on a development image,
Developer (SSH); a last row of one as wide as the grid (a release image's Flash Drive under Charge Only and File Transfer, 2026-11-12). A grid's tile has its mark at
54 and its name on one line at 110 (its two lines joined, with no space
after a hyphen), in **meta** where **menu** does not fit. Up and down move
between the rows. It starts on Charge Only, the safe answer. Chosen, ZyperOS
draws three full pages for plastron's usbdrive, which has no words or theme
of its own, saves them in /tmp and leaves. Each page is the empty home's
top (11.19): Zyper (124) at (123, 168) beside the heading in **title**
**text** at x 208 (two balanced lines when one is too long). "Connected as
a flash drive" has two **raised** tiles side by side under it
(**radius-card**, 472 wide from **inset**, y 256, 280 tall), one for each
way (2026-11-11, my pick): **Download**, to the card (the computer writes), an arrow down
into a tray (UI_GLYPH_TO_CARD, System Update's corner mark's family), and
**Upload**, from the card (it reads), an arrow up out of a tray
(UI_GLYPH_FROM_CARD). Each language's everyday words for them ("Réception" and
"Envoi" in French, "Descarga" and "Subida" in Spanish).
In each, the mark (56) and its name in **menu** **text** at the top, the
speed big in **title** **text**, and the total since the start under it in
**meta** **text-soft**. A way that moved in the last 2 s has its mark in
**accent-ink**; an idle one's is **text-dim** and its speed reads 0 MB/s.
No status row: the two tiles say it. Under them "Eject it on the computer
to finish." in **meta** **text-soft**, and at the foot two hints, the
shelves' foot's size (11.9), centered as a pair: B "Eject" and POWER "Stop
and power off". B ejects from the handheld (the medium taken away, so the
computer lets go of it, then back as after the computer's eject); if the
computer wrote in the last 5 s, or more than 1 MB of its writes is not on
the card yet, or usbdrive cannot read the gadget's counters at all, it asks
first, on a page of the same make:
"Eject anyway?" over "The computer may still be copying to the card." in
the wide tile, A "Eject" and B "Keep" at the foot. Ejecting on the computer
stays the way the eject line names. FUNCTION and volume step the
brightness on its usual rungs, and the brightness bar (11.6) shows at the
top as everywhere, pre-drawn at every rung by the launcher on the ground
it sits on (bright.raw), up for 1.5 s; the rung is kept as the launcher
keeps its own. "One moment / The
card is getting ready." (both ways) and "The card is busy / It stays on the
handheld. Try again in a moment." (fail closed: never exported while
mounted or held) have their words in one tile the width of both, in
**menu** **text-soft**. The tiles are live: ZyperOS also leaves an atlas,
each token of a number in the speeds' face and in the totals' (a digit in a
cell as wide as the widest digit, so a number does not shift as it counts),
the language's decimal mark and units ("1,24 Go"), "/s", and each way's
mark moving and idle, on the tiles' color, with where they and the six
slots are; usbdrive reads, once a second, the bytes the gadget itself
served (the f_mass_storage thread's /proc io counters: the card's /sys stat
counted a write only when the page cache reached the card, up to 30 s
late, 2026-11-12) and draws only a tile that changed, so an idle drive costs nothing (plastron's
package/usbdrive/src/atlas.h and drivestat.h; the atlas is read as
untrusted). Speeds 1000-based, one decimal in MB/s (999.9 MB/s at most),
totals with two in GB (999.99 GB); never a time left, as the handheld sees
blocks, not files. Every slot edge is a multiple of 8 so it lands on a
pixel of the saved 640 x 480. File Transfer's news (11.35) is this page too, the
tiles drawn by the same code, with what File Transfer knows that Flash Drive
does not: the file's name and the upload's bar. Without the atlas the page shows as drawn,
both ways idle. Host shots: `--usb-ask N [plain] drive`, `--usb-drive N
[in|out|both|wide]` (a copy each way, both, the widest numbers), and
`--usb-drive 3` for the question, `--usb-drive 0 bright` with the bar,
and `--usb-drive 4` for the atlas, which prints where everything is.

MENU > **Developer Mode** (on or off, between Show Tutorial and System
Update) is a development image's only: on, the link is always up and nothing
is asked.

On a release image, the row opens **Developer (SSH)**, a menu panel (11.3):
two switches, **SSH over Wi-Fi** (Off as it ships) and **Developer Mode**
(On), each turned over by A, Left or Right; a rule; then up to three
full-width **text-dim** lines, left-aligned: what to type (`ssh root@<address>`,
the address first as Wi-Fi File Transfer has it, then over Wi-Fi
`ssh root@zyperos.local`, or on the cable "Password: x7k4mq2d"), the keys
SSH takes, the Developer folder's and ssh-copy-id's alike ("1 key on the
card"; with none, where one goes: "No key in the Developer folder"), and,
only while nothing listens, why ("SSH not with Wi-Fi", "SSH needs a key",
"SSH locked"); then "A change  B close". The cursor rests on SSH over Wi-Fi
and Developer Mode is under it, since turning it off is not one press to
undo (the hold on About); off, the page closes back to MENU. SSH over Wi-Fi
turned on says once, as a message with "B close", that over Wi-Fi only a key
signs in, never the password, and how the first key gets on. Turning
Developer Mode off turns it off too. *(My pick, 2026-11-15: the switch on
the page, not in MENU.)*

The same tiles ask File Transfer's questions while files move (11.23), with
a **meta** **text-dim** line under the rule saying what moves and a **meta**
**text-soft** line under the tiles saying what the focused one does.

### 11.19 The empty home
The home when the card has no games (MP4 alone included), under the home's
own header (11.12) and with its MENU and SELECT hints in the foot
(src/emptyhome.h). Zyper, 124 tall, at the left of **No games yet** in
**title**, and under it "Add them from a computer or a phone." in **support**
**text-soft**: where a page has them (the page frame, section 15; Zyper at
168, the heading at 150 and its line at 208, the cards from 256, since
2026-11-14). Then the two ways as cards side by side, **raised**,
**radius.card**, 476 x 368, from the screen's **inset** (24) with 24 between:
each has its mark (the USB trident, or three arcs and a dot for Wi-Fi), 56
across in **accent-ink**, and its button at the right (A for **USB File
Transfer**, Y for **Wi-Fi File Transfer**, 48, on-accent style); the way's
name in **game-title**; and its three steps in **support**, numbered in
**text-dim**. "BIOS files go in Bios/ on the card." is under the cards in
**support** **text-dim**. One press of the button opens that way's transfer
screen (11.15).

It follows the port, live: on the Pixel 2 the cable and the USB Wi-Fi adapter
share it, so the way that cannot start now has its name, mark and steps in
**text-dim**, its button plain, and one line at the card's foot saying why in
**text-soft** ("Unplug the Wi-Fi adapter." / "Plug in the Wi-Fi adapter
first."); its button plays **deny**. The Brick's words are its own (a cable
that links when a computer is on it, Wi-Fi built in). Nothing on it moves by
itself.

When games arrive (the transfer screen closes and rescans, or Roms/ changes
by any other way, which it looks at every two seconds), the home becomes
Spotlight, Gallery or Grid, and Zyper cheers once over it: the home fades toward
the **ground** (65%) on **move**, Zyper hops and pants (8.3) at the middle,
the tutorial's lime check pops on **zyper-pop**, his bubble says "12
games!", and with the **reward** sound it holds **tour-reward** and leaves
on **fade-out**. Any button ends it early. Host shots: `--ways
usb|wifi|both|none[,brick]` on a card with no games, and `--cheer MS` over a
home. *(My picks in the empty home lab,
`docs/design/mock/empty-home-lab.html`, 2026-11-06: Two cards, with the USB
trident; Follow the port; Zyper cheers first.)*

---

### 11.19 Scraper and video snaps
MENU > Downloads' Covers, Videos and Names and Details rows (11.53; MENU >
**Scraper** until the Downloads row took its place) open a page in the Flash
Drive pages' look (11.18):
Zyper and the heading at the top, the keys at the foot. A console's own
**Box Art** opens the same page for that console alone. MENU's Scraper row
still says what is missing (the count, "complete", "needs Wi-Fi"), counted
as the page closes. The page says four things before anything runs: what a
run will get, for which systems, which games get videos, and their room.

Its frame is Flash Drive's page's, the page frame of `src/ds/page.h`
(section 15): the sides at **DS_INSET** (24 and 1000), the heading at 168,
or at 150 with a second line in **meta** **text-soft** at 208
(DS_HEAD_SUB), what the page holds from 256 (DS_PAGE_BODY_Y), one line over
the keys at 648 (its box DS_PAGE_LINE_OVER over theirs), and the keys on every
screen's foot, 720. Every state keeps to it, the
Systems grid's focus ring and the run's lit cover inside the sides
(2026-11-14, on the Pixel 2: "Systems is not respecting the window
padding ... Review the whole interface margin and spacing").

A size is either still to fetch, "+525 MB" on a tile and "525 MB to fetch"
in words, or on the card, "525 MB on the card"; the plan adds up its two
tiles' figures in its line, Systems' line is the focus's own share, and
Videos for's choices add up the systems with videos on, which the line
under its heading says ("For 3 systems with videos on"). A video's size and
a cover's are the card's own average once it has five (24 covers a system
sampled), else 2.5 MB and 0.25 MB. (2026-11-14: "in the videos for popup
it's saying 1.9gb for all games, when collapsed it says 883mb in the video
part": the popup added up every system with videos on and Systems' line
was the focus's alone, with its videos on the card added in.)

- **The plan**, the page as it opens: each system's icon (nine at most, or
  eight and "+N") over two marks, a cover and a video, lit when the system
  gets them; then two **raised** tiles, **Covers** (the covers a run will
  look for, "on 7 systems") and **Videos** (the videos it will get, "+525
  MB" on the number's row, "on 3 systems", "all off" or "needs
  ScreenScraper"), each tile with what it adds to the card on its number's
  row; the heading's second line is about how long the run takes beside a
  clock ("about 35 min"), or "needs Wi-Fi"; the line over the keys the room,
  "656 MB to fetch · 11.2 GB free", in **warning** when the run would leave
  less than 200 MB free, and then A does not start it and says "Not
  enough room on the card". A fetches, Y opens Systems, START Settings, B
  closes. From a console's Box Art there is no Y.
- **Systems**: a tile a system, three by three, the rows following the
  focus, each with the system's icon and its two marks. A switches the
  focus's covers, X its videos, Y asks Videos for, B is back on the plan.
  The focus is named under the heading and again over the keys, as marks:
  the covers it misses ("41 missing") and its videos to fetch ("883 MB to
  fetch", "complete", "needs ScreenScraper"), or "off". Three tiles a row
  fill the page between its sides with the focus ring inside them.
  Covers start on and videos off, as a video is ~2.5 MB and one
  ScreenScraper lookup (snaps_covers, snaps_get); a system with no switch of
  its own follows 1.0.4's Fetch with Box Art and console switch, so a card
  that was getting videos still does.
- **Videos for**: tiles over the page, as Computer Connected's (11.18):
  **All Games**, **Favorites**, **Played** (a game with play time), each
  with "210 · 525 MB to fetch" (no-break spaces keep a number with its
  unit), and the systems they add up under the heading.
- **Settings**: a list, as What did you plug in?'s (11.36), of
  **ScreenScraper**, **SteamGridDB** and **Videos**, each with its value
  under it: tiles left "ScreenScraper" no room, 264 wide in **meta** where a
  tile has 248 inside, with "linked as" and the account under it. Up and
  Down move. A source opens its page: what it is linked as and a dead Status row,
  **Unlink** (which asks first, 11.8's question), and two notes under a
  rule on where an account or a key comes from; SteamGridDB's tile says
  "key refused" when it is. libretro needs nothing and has no tile.
  **Names and Details** (1.0.6) is the fourth row, the open book its mark,
  `on` or `off` under it: a choice of two, so A switches it where it is and
  the list stays open. Off, a run asks no names for covered games with none
  (artscrape.c, art_names) and fetches only what is missing; on, the plan's
  line over the keys starts with the names a run will look up ("199 names"),
  the book its mark, before the room.
- **The run**: the game it is asking about is the heading, cut to the
  screen; under it its system's icon, the mark of the source being asked
  (a cloud for ScreenScraper, a book for libretro, a grid for SteamGridDB),
  a bar and "31 / 142": games LOOKED AT, which moves with every game. Each
  cover found lands big at the left over 520 ms while the six before it
  move one place along, three by two, each with its source's mark and a
  play mark when a video came with it; the pictures are decoded off the
  frame (mediatex). The covers and videos found, and the time left beside
  a clock, over the keys, as marks. B is **Continue in background**
  (System Update's words, 11.17): the page closes and the run goes on
  behind the screens (below); Y stops. The time left is the part it
  is in at its own pace once it has one (six games and 15 s), else 2 s a
  ScreenScraper lookup, 1 s a libretro try and 2 s a video's download, and
  in the covers part the details part's games after it, counted as
  details_count picks them. The newest lands in a 360 square at the page's
  left side, the six before it in 168 squares to its right side. The card
  is looked at every 2 s: under 100 MB free, the run stops and done says
  "Not enough room on the card" (the downloads' queue's rule, DLQ_FLOOR,
  for every run).
- **Done**: "All done", the covers found as a wall (fourteen at most), the
  counts as marks (covers, videos, missing) and the room said plainly
  ("Videos: 3 systems, 525 MB on the card", or "Videos: needs
  ScreenScraper"); A runs again, B closes. A problem that ended the run is
  the heading's second line, in at most 25 characters.

**Behind the screens** (src/dlq.h; my pick in the downloads lab,
2026-11-19: continue in the background, wait in a game). A run is a lane of
the downloads' queue, not its page's: it has a network slot of its own, so
it asks its servers beside the achievements download's run and beside every
screen's request (a game's Cover and Video page, an account's answer), and
it is stepped from behind every screen - as one draws (tick_theme, not while
a button is held, so a held direction scrolls without paying for a step)
and while one rests (the input wait, cut to 16 ms while a run goes). The
status corner shows it (11.12's download mark, swept as far as the runs
have got), MENU closes over it as over anything, and Auto Off waits for it
(11.38). While a game runs it waits, nothing of the game's frame spent on
it, and goes on when the game is over. POWER stops it, as everything; the
next run starts from what is missing. Opening the page again shows the run
where it is, or, when it ended while the page was closed, Done as it ended;
a run that ended behind the screens leaves its names, covers and snaps to
the shelf's next pass (scraper_after_run), as the page's close does.

A run gets covers for every system first, then the details (names, years,
synopses) of games that already have a cover, with a count of their own.
The details went first on each console once and held the count at 0 for
minutes on a card of covers from elsewhere (a player's "hangs" on 1.0.4,
and its logs, 2026-11-13). libretro is asked right after each ScreenScraper
miss, ten misses in a row on a console leave the rest of it to libretro,
and a game ScreenScraper says it does not know is left alone for 30 days
(the ss_miss table). A system with covers off gets no cover pass and no
names, only its videos.

Video Snaps opens a page from Settings: **Play on the Shelf**, **Sound**
(muted, quiet, full), a rule, every console with "on · 212 MB", and
**Delete All**; every switch steps round with Left and Right (9's ring).
Its console switches are the shelf's, which play; the Systems grid's get.
Host shots: `--scraper-page STATE`; tools/scraper-live.py plays the real
loop on the Mac, network and all (ZYPEROS_SCRAPER_TEST).

On the games shelf, after **hold-snap** on a game - the shelf still, lit,
nothing over it - the focused cover turns into its video snap: over
**turn** the cover fades out while the snap shows through a frame that
grows from the cover's rect to the snap's own (4:3, inside the cover's
384 x 328 box), never stretched; the cards either side stay where they
are. It loops. A move takes it away at once, with the card; a menu, the
dark screen or MP4 fade it back over **quick**. It is muted to start.
While it waits, a bar under the focused cover says the snap is coming: the
cover's own width, 12 below it, **UI_BAR_H** tall and round-ended, a
**track** that fills with **accent-ink** over **hold-snap**, held full
while the first picture comes, and fading with the cover as it turns.
Highlight's bar gives way to it meanwhile (3.5). Only a game that has a
snap shows it. Host shots: `--snap-wait F`; `games-long-title` and
`games-long-title-wait`, a name that reaches under the cover.
A snap's picture goes to the GPU in a texture never smaller than 640x480,
in its corner when it is smaller, and is drawn from its own rectangle:
the Pixel 2's GPU faulted, and the launcher with it, on narrower ones (a
Nintendo DS game's 320x480, a Game Boy game's, 534x480), 2026-11-09.
Halfway through **hold-snap** the snap is asked for held: MP4 opens it and
puts out its first picture with no clock and no sound, so the cover turns
the frame the bar is full, from the clip's first picture, and its sound
starts then. Not sooner: a shelf being scrolled opens no file.
*(2026-11-07, on the Pixel 2: "when the video bar finishes loading,
it still takes half a second to show the video. We should load the video as
the bar fills up, or like 500ms after it starts filling up, to avoid
affecting scrolling performance".)*
Every cover whose game has a snap carries a mark at its bottom right, inset
12: an **accent** disc 40 across with the play glyph in **on-accent** (its
triangle's centroid on the disc's middle), 30 and inset 9 on the cards
either side (at the turned corner of a turned cover), fading with the cover
as its snap takes over, and none when Play on the Shelf or the console's
switch is off. The
shelf knows from one listing of the folder's .media, taken when it is first
drawn and again when the folder changes (looked at every 2 s at most). A
Scraper run, from MENU or a console's Box Art, and a game's Cover and
Video page that got or deleted a snap say the card's media changed
(snapshelf_media_changed): the listing is taken again at once, and the
focused game's snap file, worked out when the focus moves, is worked out
again under the same focus, so a snap the run brought for it plays after
the wait as soon as the shelf is back (2026-11-14, on the Pixel 2: "after
i download a video from A4, and go back to the game list, the video doesnt
render automaticlaly").
*(My picks in the Scraper lab, `docs/design/mock/scraper-lab.html`,
2026-11-06: one list, a page per source, a Video Snaps page, the cover
turns wide, sound a setting muted to start, 3 seconds. Later that day, on
the Pixel 2: the bar under the cover, and 1 second.)*
*(2026-11-13, on the panel that came before the page: "the scraper is
stuck on 0/523 while the game on the header is changing ... it's very
confusing", then "too verbose, and the video part looks like an
afterthought". My picks over five rounds of shots: the images coming in,
more icons, and "careful not to be too crowded or text overflowing
depending on the language and themes and zyper on/off".)*

### 11.20 First start
The first start of a freshly written card copies the ZyperOS folder the
system image carries onto it, and the first start of a newer system image
copies that image's: 468 MB, about 54 s on a Pixel 2 (2026-11-06), where the
boot animation's loop played on with nothing said. The loop goes on (Zyper
pants), and once the intro has written the word, under the lockup: one line
in **meta** **text-soft**, centered on x 512 with its baseline at y 706,
"Setting up ZyperOS on this card", or "Updating ZyperOS on this card" after a
system update; under it a **bar** 420 wide at y 728, **track** filling with
**accent-ink** from the left as the copy lands on the card, as the progress
strip of 11.7: lime on Salt & Pepper, the dark green on Beard. In the theme
the boot animation is in (11.21). *(My pick of three,
2026-11-06: the line alone, the line and the bar, or a screen of its own in
the port loading screen's shape.)* tools/genfirststart.py draws the line and
the bar in panel pixels (res/boot/first-start/); plastron's splash lays them
over the loop and fills the bar.

The line is in the card's language (11.30; my pick in the languages
lab, 2026-11-07): res/strings' `boot.setup` and `boot.update`, drawn into a
folder a language beside English's pictures, Chinese in its face, no line
past 64 units inside either edge. plastron's splash takes the language from
the copy's line, which its init ends with the card's `.userdata/<device>/language`
(the launcher's copy of the setting), else from `zylang` on the kernel's line
(11.21), else English: a new card, which has no language yet, says it in
English, and the first start's first step asks.

### 11.21 Boot screen theme
The boot animation is in the theme MENU > Appearance chose: Salt & Pepper's
as it was, Zyper on his dark ground; Beard's on its ground, the word in the
cap's dark with the Z in accent-ink (the README's light lockup), and Zyper with the
focus-small shadow under him, as the light loading loop has it (10). Auto is
Salt & Pepper in the Night Hours by the clock and its zone, as in the
launcher: its word carries them (`zytheme=auto-1260-420`, 21:00 to 7:00), and
a 1.0.5 launcher's plain `auto` is 19:00 to 7:00 until the launcher writes
the file again. A
card with no choice yet starts in Beard, the launcher's own default. *(
2026-11-06: the boot follows the setting; the Beard version approved from
a mockup.)* The animation plays before the card is mounted, so the choice
travels another way (src/boottheme.h): the launcher keeps it in theme.txt on
the boot partition, boot.scr puts it on the kernel's line, and the splash
reads it there. tools/genboot.py --light draws Beard's frames. The language
travels with it (`zylang=`, 11.20), and a card whose boot.scr is older than
that, which System Update leaves as it was, keeps the language on the card
as well.

The boot screens are at the player's brightness, not the kernel's full light
*(Discord, 2026-11-15: "Update, boot and charging screen brightness. Can
those screens follow the set brightness? Currently they illuminate the whole
room.")*: the boot animation, its first start's and update's lines, and a
charger's start before the charging screen (11.25). The launcher keeps the
backlight's own value at the player's rung (the device's ladder) with the
theme (`zybright=`), and the splash sets it before it lights the panel. On a
card whose boot.scr is older than that, the launcher keeps it on the card as
well (`.userdata/<device>/boot-brightness`), and plastron's init sets it as
soon as the card is mounted, before an update's or a copy's line. Until a
brightness was ever kept (a new card), the panel lights at the kernel's
level, as before; a value of 0, or above the backlight's maximum, is never
taken.

### 11.22 Song pop-up
Zyper saying the menu music's new song on the home and the games shelf
*(2026-11-06: "whenever the bg song changes, can we have zyper popup
and say the new song name?")*: whenever a new song starts there - by itself
at the last one's end, by the shuffle, or by **L2**/**R2** - and never over a
menu, a game, a port or MP4, nor when the music is off or quiet, nor for a
song that changed while a menu was open. One track starting over by itself
is not new. **MENU > Menu Music > Song Pop-up** turns it off.

The notice's slab (11.7) with Zyper where an achievement's badge is:
**surface** at 85%, **radius.panel**, its padding the notice's (a third of
the **menu** line at the sides, a sixth above and below), lifted with
**elevation.panel** since it lies over the shelf and not over a game.
Centered, its top at y 96, where a panel's may be, clear of the header and
the volume bar above it, which comes first where the two meet. Zyper at its
left, 64 tall as in the menus, with his **focus-small** shadow on Beard;
beside him where the song is from in **meta** **text-dim** (the album, which
for a soundtrack rip is the game, else the artist, else "Now playing") over
the song in **label** **text** (its title, else the file's name tidied:
src/bgm_tags.h). As wide as its longer line and at most the standard panel's
800; a line wider than that scrolls as a marquee does.

In over **quick** on **out**, sliding down 14 as it fades up (the bar's
motion, 11.6); he hops and pants as each song comes, and breathes and blinks
while it stays. Held **hold-long** after the last change, or until a name
that scrolls has reached its end and rested there, whichever is longer; out
over **fade-out**. A new song while it is up changes the words where it is,
back to full at once if it was fading, with no second slide: songs do not
queue behind one another. No sound of its own; an **L2**/**R2** press plays
**move**, or **deny** with no music to move. It never takes a press.

**MP4's track.** While MP4 is heard behind the shelf, **L2**/**R2** move MP4
(11.14), and the same pop-up says MP4's track as the press lands: music and
video the artist (else the album) over the title; a book the book (its
album, else its author) over the file's title, or, in a file with chapters,
the book over the chapter as Now Playing names it ("Chapter 3" for one that
is only a number). It comes up with the file's name, all MP4 knows of a
track it has only just asked for, and while it is up its words follow
MP4's - the tags a moment later, the next chapter, the track after one
that ended - changed in place, with no second hop. R2 on the last track of
a queue that does not go round moves nothing and plays **edge**; the
pop-up still says what plays. It goes when MP4 stops being heard (a
headset's pause), and **Song Pop-up** Off turns it off too. MP4 moving by
itself is not said: the home header already names its song.

### 11.23 Getting hot
The chip's temperature said when the kernel slows it down hard *(
2026-11-06: "Yes, build both"; the picks in the battery and heat lab,
docs/design/mock/battery-heat-lab.html: over a game, the notice with a
thermometer)*. The Pixel 2's kernel slows the CPU
and GPU at 70 and 85 C and powers off at 115 C; at 85 C, the second step, a
game gets visibly slower. src/heat.h: read every 5 s from the two thermal
zones, the hotter counting; said after two readings in a row at 85 C or
more; said again only once the chip has been under 80 C since, and never
within 5 minutes of the last time. No sound: the slow-down is nothing the
player did.

On the home and the shelves Zyper says it himself *(My pick, "Zyper's
bubble", with his note: a thermometer in it too)*: 220 tall, his middle at
(200, 632), he comes up from under the bottom edge in **move**, hopping and
panting - a dog cools off that way - and as he lands his bubble comes in
beside him in **quick**, sliding up 6: the tutorial's bubble (11.10),
**text** with **radius.panel** corners and a tail toward him, its top left at
(334, 452), "I'm getting hot!" in **label** over "Slowing down to cool off."
in **meta**, both in **ground**, and at their left a thermometer as tall as
the two lines, its outline in **ground** and its mercury in **warning**.
Held **hold-heat**, then the bubble out over **fade-out** and Zyper down in
**exit**. It takes no press, and it goes with the shelf when another screen
takes the display. Due over a menu, MP4 or the tutorial, it waits for the
home, and goes unsaid if the chip cools under 85 C first. Over a game it is the notice (11.7) with a thermometer in
**warning** where an achievement's badge goes, the lines left-aligned
beside it, held **hold-heat**, ahead of every pop-up waiting (an alert,
src/noticeq.h). Host shots: `--heatpop MS` (MS ms after he starts up) and
`--notice "Slowing down to cool off." "Getting hot" --notice-heat`.

At 115 C the kernel powers the handheld off in order (/sbin/poweroff): the
system's shutdown ends the launcher and Diatom, Diatom saves the game and its
state as it does for POWER, and the game stays marked so the next start goes
back into it. The chip's own cut, at 120 C, is outside Linux.

### 11.24 A battery too low to start
A start at 2% or less off the charger (BATT_EMPTY_PCT, where a game already
saves and the handheld powers off) used to come up to the home and power off
ten seconds later with nothing said. Now it is the first screen, before a
resume, the tutorial or the home *(My picks in the battery and heat
lab, 2026-11-06: Zyper and two lines, a bar that empties, Charging then the
home)*: on the
ground, Zyper 230 tall, centered at y 226, his head cocked to 18 degrees with
his tongue in, breathing and blinking; the status corner's battery mark,
half again as large and empty in **warning**, before "Battery too low" in
**title** **text**, centered as one row at y 418; "Plug in a charger." in
**label** **text-soft** at y 508; and the first start's bar (11.20), 420 wide
at y 640, **warning** on **track**, emptying from the right over
**hold-low-battery**. Then the send-off from where he stands, the power-off's
last two parts: his head straight as the words fade, and dimmed to the
ground. POWER powers off at once. A charger plugged in meanwhile turns the
mark to charging and the line to "Charging.", Zyper settles, hops and pants,
the bar goes over **fade-out**, and after **hold-short** the start goes on as
it would have. The power-off is decided by a second, fresh reading at the
end of the hold; a battery that reads over the line then goes on to the
home, where the empty battery's own watch goes on. src/lowbatt.h has the
rules; a host shot: `--lowbatt MS [charging|off]`.

### 11.25 Charging while off
The screen of a handheld that is off and on a charger *(2026-11-06:
"Can we have a charging screen as well for when the pixel is off but
charging? With info like charging speed, eta"; his picks in the charging lab,
docs/design/mock/charging-lab.html: Zyper asleep over the numbers, the speed
too, lit 10 seconds, hold POWER to start - with his note, "tell to press and
hold like we do on the unlock screen" - and the power-off on a charger going
to it)*. Two ways in. A charger plugged into a Pixel 2 that is off wakes it:
plastron's bootloader starts it with `zycharge=1` on the kernel's line
instead of turning it straight off again, and this is the launcher's first
screen, before a resume, the tutorial or the home; plastron's splash shows
only the theme's plain ground then, no boot animation, and neither the
system's slots nor a new ZyperOS on trial count it as a start. And POWER, or
Auto Off,
on a charger: the game saved and the send-off played as ever, then this in
place of black, ZyperOS off (the music too).

On the ground: Zyper asleep, 211 tall, his middle at (512, 198), his eyes and
mouth shut, tongue in, rising and falling with a slow breath (4 s), and three
z's in **text-dim** going up from his head and fading, in **meta**,
**menu** and **title**; the clock in its corner (11.12); under him the
status corner's battery mark, half again as large and charging, before the
percentage in **title** **text**, centered as one row at y 390; at y 506 the
speed in **label** **text-soft**, "Charging · 7.4 W · wall charger" ("Plugged
in · ..." with nothing going in, "Charged · ..." when full; "computer" on a
computer's port), shown only once the charger is named: the USB port names
it a moment after the cable comes, and some 6 s into a start, so the line
waits for it rather than say "charger" first (up to 8 s; "charger" after
that); at y 580 the time to full in **meta** **text-dim**, "Full
in about 1 h 10 min" (rounded up to 5 minutes; "Working out the time to full"
for its first 30 seconds, "Almost full", "Fully charged"); and at the foot
"Hold (POWER) to start" in **meta** **text-dim** with the button drawn
(11.9), the lock screen's bar (11.14) filling under it in **accent-ink** on
**track** while POWER is held.

Lit for **hold-charge-lit** from the last button, then dark, the side lights
showing the charge; any button lights it again. POWER held for
**hold-power**, one second, the same as Power Button's Hold (11.47; Tarik,
1.0.6: "Can we have the hold power to turn on on charging screen also be one
second?"), starts ZyperOS: a start the charger made goes on, and a
power-off on a charger starts afresh, back into a game as any start goes. The
cable out: "Unplugged" for **hold-short**, the screen dims to the ground and
the handheld turns off; the cable back while it dims, or in the 5 s after
it, brings the screen back, and back later in the power-off, the handheld
starts again onto it (plastron's last step of the power-off, as the charger
itself starts an off handheld only for a cable that comes while it is off).
POWER pressed from "Unplugged" to the end of those 5 s starts ZyperOS, as a
press starts an off handheld, without the hold the charging screen asks
for. src/charging.h has the words, the time to full (the current smoothed
over 30 s; the last 15% of the charge falling toward the end current, as a
battery's CV phase does) and the rules; a host shot:
`--charging MS [hold|unplugged|off|pending|kept|limit]`; a test from a
shell: `/tmp/zyperos_fake_charge` and `/tmp/zyperos_charge_boot`.

### 11.26 Charge limit

My picks in the charge limit lab (docs/design/mock/charge-limit-lab.html,
2026-11-06). A charge the battery stops at, to keep it healthy for longer: a
lithium battery ages slower when it is not kept full.

**Charge Limit** in MENU, directly under Auto Off and over Side Lights:
**Off**, the default on every card, a full charge as before; **80%**,
**85%**, **90%**. Left and right step it round, A opens its list (11.3), heard as a
ring, as Side Lights is. Only where the charger can stop short: plastron's
rk817 driver on the GKD Pixel 2 (`charge_control_end_threshold`), which at
the limit lets the cable give only 80 mA, less than the handheld draws, so
the battery takes no charge and slowly gives the rest, and 2 points under it
(`charge_control_start_threshold`) charges again: the battery holds between
the limit minus 2 and the limit, 78 to 80% at 80%, it does not sit full all
day on a desk, and it is never off the system (turning the charger off took
it off, and pulling the cable then cut the power, on a Pixel 2). The band was
5 points at first, and 80% fell to 75% before it charged again, which looked
like a limit that slipped (2026-11-07). 2 is safe: the level is the gauge's
coulomb counter, which moves only with the charge, so each switch waits for a
point of charge, minutes, and never flickers on and off. And it is kind to
the battery: held, it gives the handheld the same current whatever the band,
so a narrow band only cuts that into shorter top-ups, and a top-up of a point
near 80% stresses it little. The launcher writes the start as well as the
end, so a system from before the change holds the same band. The kernel
forgets the limit at a power-off, and the launcher sets it at every start.
The percentage stays true: 100% is still a full battery. With the cable out
the charger is on again, so a charger on a handheld that is off, with no
kernel to keep the limit, charges it.

On the charging screen (11.25), with a limit: the time is to the limit, "85%
in about 40 min" ("Working out the time to 85%", "Almost at 85%"); held at
it, the speed line is "Kept at 85% · wall charger", the time line "Charge
Limit in MENU", and the battery mark has the plug (11.12). With a limit
saved and the battery short of full, charging up to the limit or held at
it, the hint grows to "(Y) Charge to 100% · Hold (POWER) to start"; Y, on a
lit screen, is a full charge this time only: the hint says "Charging to 100%
this time" in its place, the time goes to full, and the saved limit is back
when the cable comes out or the battery is full (a file in /tmp, so a
restart ends it too). A Y that lights a dark screen only lights it.
*(Tarik, 2026-11-11: until then it was "Hold (A) for a full charge", and only
once held at the limit.)* A battery charged full before the limit was set is
held at 100%: the screen says "Charged" and "Fully charged" as any full one,
with the plug, and offers no full charge. The corner's battery has
the plug while it is held, everywhere.

### 11.27 Side lights

My picks in the side lights lab (`docs/design/mock/side-lights-lab.html`,
2026-11-06), after he asked whether the lights could be lit one by one for
more modes. The GKD Pixel 2's four lights under POWER, bottom to top: the
bottom one red or green (both at once read as yellow on the device, as I
saw it 2026-11-06), then three green. Each is on
or off, nothing between (GPIO pins, no PWM). plastron's gauge draws them
(package/gauge/src/lights.c, a pure function with host checks); the launcher
only tells it what happened, a line at a time on its FIFO `/run/gauge`
(src/sidelights.h), and never waits. Diatom does nothing for them, so a game
loses no frames. A step of a moving pattern lasts 100 ms ("Even, 100 ms").

- **Charging Bar**, on the cable: the lights held for each full quarter, then
  one more every 400 ms up to all four, and again, in games as on the shelf
  ("Moving, as today"). Still at all four when full, and still at the level
  (at least one light) while Charge Limit holds the battery ("Still at the
  limit"; the battery's status says Not charging); it moves again when
  charging starts again under the limit.
- **Low Battery**, off the cable ("Two stages"): under 10% the bottom light
  flashes red 200 ms every 5 s, under 5% twice (150 ms on, 150 off, 150 on)
  every 2 s. The first flash comes the moment it falls under each, and when
  the cable comes out.
- **Battery Level**, all the time (my ask after testing 1.0.6, 2026-11-17:
  with the cable out every light was dark although there was battery, and
  only L2+R2 showed it): the level L2+R2 flashes, still, at the same lights
  (all four from 75%, three from 50%, two from 25%, one green from 10%), on
  the shelf, in menus and in games. Off the cable, and on it when Charging
  Bar is off; on the cable the bar shows instead. Under 10% off the cable it
  gives way to Low Battery's flash, which says how low; with Low Battery off
  it is a steady red. Off to start, so a card that never turns it on sees
  what it saw before. Everything below shows over it for its time, then the
  level comes back; L2+R2 over it flashes it, so the press still shows.
- **Volume and Brightness**, while one changes ("Eight steps, a half
  blinks"): each light is two of eight steps; the one above the lit ones
  blinks (250 ms on, 250 off) for a half. Volume at 0 lights the bottom one
  red; the dimmest brightness is a half, never red. Held as long as the
  screen's bar (**hold-bar**, 1.5 s after the last change). From the volume
  keys on the shelf and in menus, from Diatom's LEVEL lines in a game (a
  move only: the levels Diatom reports as a game starts are not a press), and
  from portkeys over a port.
- **Files and Covers**, Done ("Climb twice"): one light runs up the four,
  twice, 0.8 s, when a File Transfer batch is in (files arrived, then none
  for 2 s: the page sends a batch one file at a time) and when a Scraper run
  ends without a problem.
- **Files and Covers**, files moving: my picks in the transfer lights lab
  (`docs/design/mock/transfer-lights-lab.html`, 2026-11-13), after I asked
  for an upload and download animation in Flash Drive and on the web page.
  While files move to or from the card, a wave fills the four lights and
  empties them ("Fill and empty"): from the top down while they come in to
  the card (Download), from the bottom up while they go out of it (Upload),
  as the Flash Drive screen's arrows point ("As the screen's arrows"). Eight
  steps a run, the last one dark. Its pace follows the speed ("Follows the
  speed"): under 1 MB/s 200 ms a step, under 10 MB/s 100 ms, faster 60 ms.
  With both ways moving, the way moving more bytes shows ("The bigger one"),
  chosen again as each run begins, so a run is never cut. It lasts as long
  as bytes move, and 1.5 s past the last word of it; then the lights show
  what they showed before ("Back to the battery": a Flash Drive copy ends
  with no Done, since a computer writes small files of its own when it
  opens the drive). Flash Drive (plastron's usbdrive) says so once a second
  from the gadget's own counters, a way under 64 KB/s taken as still; the
  web page at most every 500 ms, counting only a file's bytes (an upload or
  a download on its way), never the page, its covers or its lists. One row
  with Done in MENU ("With Files and Covers Done"), named Files and Covers.
- **Achievements** ("A sparkle"): the bottom and third lights, then the
  second and top, three times each, 150 ms a step, 0.9 s, the moment one
  unlocks in a game, while its pop-up waits for the menu.
- **No game light** ("Not on the lights"): nothing shows that a game runs.

Sharing them ("Newest on top"): a level, a Done or a sparkle takes the lights
for its time, over the charging bar (or Battery Level) and over files moving,
and the newest replaces the one before; then the wave, the bar or the battery
level comes back. Files moving is over the charging bar and Battery Level for
as long as they move. The low-battery red is laid over everything, a
pattern included (it is the bottom light's red, which no pattern uses but
silence), and is hidden only when Low Battery is off. L2+R2 flashes the
battery level over all of it, four times in 2 s, in every setting.

**Side Lights** in MENU, under Charge Limit, says how many parts are on ("All
on", "3 on", "Off"); A opens its page ("A page of switches"): Charging Bar,
Low Battery, Battery Level, Volume and Brightness, Files and Covers,
Achievements, each On or Off, stepped by Left, Right or A and heard as a
ring. All on counts every part a new card has on, so a card that kept the
default still reads All on with Battery Level off; once another part is
off, Battery Level counts like the rest. Every change is
written to the card at once and the gauge reads it again, so the lights
follow the row. The row under the cursor plays its part on the lights in a
loop, On or Off, for as long as the cursor stays on it (my ask, 2026-11-13):
the bar filling from none, one red flash then two, the battery's level as it
is now (still), the level up its eight steps then silence's red, the wave
down and up then Done, the sparkle. It
takes the lights over everything but L2+R2, and they go back to what they
show on their own as the page closes. Everything is on for a new card and for a card that said
Auto ("Everything on"), Battery Level aside; a card set to Charging, Low
Battery or Off before keeps what it said, the newer parts off. The setting is
a line in `.userdata/pixel2/side-lights`: the old word first (auto, charging,
low, off), which an older gauge reads alone, then `level`/`-level`,
`done`/`-done`, `cheer`/`-cheer`, `battery`/`-battery` (off unless named,
whatever the first word; an older gauge leaves the word alone); `done`
covers files moving too, so the line needed no new word for it.

### 11.28 Controls in a game

My picks in the controls lab (docs/design/mock/controls-lab.html,
2026-11-06), made over on 2026-11-13 for the in-game menu's tabs and button
remapping (11.45).
The in-game menu is MENU's tab row (11.3) over four tabs, in the order L1
and R1 go round them, and the panel is the same size on every tab:

| Tab | Rows |
|---|---|
| **Game** | Continue, Save, Load, Undo Load (after a load, below), Quick Slot, Disc (a game on more than one disc), Player 2 (an NES game, 11.43), Core Settings (11.68), Cheats (11.66), Achievements, Reset, Quit |
| **Display** | Display, Shader; a DS game's Screens, Swap Screens, Stylus |
| **Controls** | Applies To, D-pad as Stick, Pak, Rumble (11.54), Face Buttons, one row a button, Reset Buttons, Hotkeys (11.45) |
| **Achievements** | the game's achievements, or the sentence that says why it has none (11.46) |

**Undo Load** (1.0.6). Every load the player makes, Load's slot strip or
SELECT+L1, first saves the game as it is to `<game>.undo.state` (SLOT_UNDO),
then loads the slot over it. While that file is there the Game tab has a
plain Undo Load row right under Load, and A on it loads it and resumes. One
is kept, for each game: Undo Load itself writes none, so A on it twice goes
back to the same moment, and the next load replaces it. Delete Game, Rename
and Storage count it with the game's slots.

**Display's names and picture** (1.0.6). The modes say what they do: Full
Screen, Original Shape, Square Pixels (1.1.0), Sharp Pixels, Sharp Pixels,
Tall, Sharp Pixels, Zoomed, Fill, Edges Cut and Actual Size (the card keeps
display.<TAG>'s old names: stretch, aspect, square, integer,
integer-vertical, overscale, fill, native).
The Display row, on the Display tab and in the shelf's menu, says only the
mode; A opens their list, and beside it a picture of what the mode under the
cursor does with its sentence under it (11.3, a choice with a picture). In
a game the picture is the paused frame, at the size and shape Diatom says
(DISPLAY's `source=` and `aspect=`), placed as Diatom would; on a shelf, the
game under the cursor's last frame with the shape its system's games last
said, or a drawn scene for a game never played. Two lines under the row said
it until the list did (1.0.6's first builds): they pushed Shader down, and
said the mode in words where a picture shows it. The row is named Display, as
in game: "Display Mode" beside the longest names fitted in no Latin-letter
language.

**Square Pixels** (1.1.0). Every pixel of the game square, whatever shape
its core reports: the picture as tall as Diatom's surface, its width in
proportion, centred; a picture wider than the surface at that height (the
GBA's, the PSP's) fills the width at the same factor instead. It is
RetroArch's 1:1 PAR with integer scaling off: NES and SNES games at 8:7,
their 256x224, where fceumm and snes9x2010 ask for a CRT's wider shape; the
Game Gear at 10:9 where genesis_plus_gx asks for 4:3; the PC Engine at
16:15 (the shapes the cores ask for: diatom/docs/reference/core-facts.md).
Sharp Pixels draws square pixels too, but at whole multiples, so
it can leave a border all round; this is the full-height one, with no whole
step. The card's name is `square`. In the list it sits between Original
Shape and Sharp Pixels, the step between them: the one's height, the
other's square pixels. The filter stays the Shader row's, as for every
mode (mode and filter are two axes, diatom/src/diatom.h): at about 3.4
times (224 lines on 768) Off draws some rows three lines and some four, and
Sharp-Shimmerless evens them out, which the guide's Shaders part says. The
arithmetic is Diatom's (diatom/src/scale.c, `square`), written again in
src/dmodefit.c for the list's picture and in integers in both, so the two
agree to the pixel: make check-dmodefit holds the four systems above on the
Pixel 2's 640x480 and the Brick's 1024x768 (an NES game's 256x224 on the
Brick: 878x768), and diatom's check-stateplane the rect DISPLAY says.
*(moroboshi69's ask on the Brick, #346; my yes for 1.1.0 on 2026-11-19.)*

**Where it opens.** The first MENU of a game opens on Game, on Continue.
After that it opens on the tab and the row it was left on, until the game
ends (Quit, POWER, or another game), so tuning a button is change, MENU, try,
MENU, with the cursor where it was. Applies To keeps its value with them. The
panel stands 24 units from the screen's sides, the most 11.3 allows, to make
room for the handheld on the Controls tab: its content is 864 units wide
(`menu_most_w`; 860, 26 from the sides, off the grid, until 1.0.6).
Over a port (OpenBOR, PortMaster) the menu is as it was, Continue, Controls
and Quit with no tabs, and there Controls is the read-only pages below.

**X is Quit** from any row of any tab (gm_x_row, src/game_menu.h), in every
in-game menu ZyperOS draws: over a Diatom game, over a "run:" program or a
port, and over PortMaster. It does what A on Quit does and is heard as A is;
Quit asks nothing first, so neither does X. The Quit row carries X's glyph at
its right where a value goes (`MENU_KEY_X`, 11.9), so the menu teaches the
button, and the In a game page says it as "MENU, X  Quit". B and MENU
continue the game. *(2026-11-08: I wanted to leave a game without scrolling
down to Quit.)*

**The Controls tab** (src/ctltab.c is its rows, src/ctltab_screen.c its
screen), in this order, the rows in brackets only where they apply:

1. **Applies To**: **This Game**, or the system by name ("PlayStation"), or by
   its short label where the name does not fit beside the row.
2. [A quiet row, "This game keeps its own buttons.", under it, while it says
   the system and this game has buttons of its own.]
3. [**D-pad as Stick**, On or Off, this game's whatever Applies To says (11.45).]
4. [**Pak**, an N64 game's, as it was in the Game list.]
4b. [**Rumble**, Off, Low, Medium or Full for the scope Applies To shows, a
   game whose core asked for the motor on a handheld with one (11.54).]
5. [**Face Buttons**, **By Letter** or **By Place** (11.45).]
6. **A, B, X, Y, L1, R1, L2, R2, START, SELECT**, the handheld's button at the
   left and what it does in this game at the right, in the core's own name
   for it (src/controls.c, measured from the pinned cores), the PlayStation's
   and the PSP's faces drawn as their shapes (src/padglyph.h, drawn by ui.c
   as it draws MENU's key). Left and Right step it, A opens its choices, and a
   changed value is in the **accent-ink** (11.45).
7. [A quiet row, "Cross has no button now", under a button whose change left
   a game button with none.]
8. **Reset Buttons**, live only when what shows differs from its default; A
   asks first in the yes/cancel panel Delete Game uses, "Put back the
   PlayStation's buttons in Spyro the Dragon?" or "Put back ZyperOS's buttons
   for every PlayStation game?".
9. **Hotkeys**: A opens the Hotkeys list (11.61), at its own width, Applies
   To first; its last row, **All Controls**, opens the read-only pages
   below, as a panel of pages (11.3).

A row that cannot change here is a quiet row with its value: the N64's SELECT
("C buttons"), the Dreamcast's while D-pad as Stick is on ("Held: D-pad"), and
a button Diatom keeps for itself, a DS game's L2 and R2 ("Other screen",
"Stylus on/off"). A swap never lands on one, and what Diatom does with a
button is no choice. The PSP's R2 was one ("D-pad as stick") until the stick
became a button's choice: it is a live row now, **Stick On/Off** by default
(11.45).
The list goes round and scrolls as every list does (11.3), six rows in view
beside the handheld. The handheld stands in a 280-unit column at the left of
the rows, under the tab row, and is drawn only here and on a button's choices
(11.45).

**The read-only pages** are what the Controls row opened before the tab,
behind the Hotkeys row now and at their own width, with no handheld (their
rows do not fit beside it in every language). Over the paused game, in the
standard panel with the system's rule color: Left/Right turns the pages and
comes round, B goes back to the tab, MENU to the game, and POWER stops the
game as it does from the achievements. **By button**, as the home's pages
are: the handheld's button at the left, what it does here at the right. **This
game only**: what works in this game, on this system and core, and nothing
else. Which page of how many is at the heading's right, "1 / 3" (11.3). The
system's own buttons are the tab's rows, so they are not a page here.

- **What a system adds**, a page of its own: the Nintendo 64's C buttons
  (SELECT held with X, A, B, Y), and its own D-pad (SELECT, then the d-pad:
  the d-pad alone is its stick), the same on both its cores, with "Pak:
  Rumble Pak" under them, which Pak the controller holds (the Controls tab's
  Pak row chooses it); the DS's two screens (L2 the other screen, R2 the
  stylus, then the d-pad moves it and A touches). An FDS game adds L1/R1, the
  disk's side and eject, and a pop-up says what each did (11.58).
- **Hotkeys**, SELECT held with one more button (11.61): the game's own, as
  the player set them (SELECT+R1 quick save and SELECT+L1 quick load where
  the core keeps states, SELECT+R2 faster and SELECT+L2 slower until set
  otherwise), a row each set one, and under them "SELECT alone stays the
  game's" where SELECT is one of the system's buttons and the page has room.
- **In a game** last: MENU (the menu), MENU then X (Quit), the volume
  rocker, brightness (MENU+VOL on the Pixel 2, F1/F2 on the Brick), POWER.
  The same rows as the home's pages, from one place in src/controls.c.

**Six rows a page**, or five and a note (the count in the heading, no
footer, 11.3). A row more sends a list with no cursor past the panel, where
it scrolls itself, wrong for a table read a button at a time. So pairs are
written as one where they read at a glance, START/SELECT first, then the
shoulders (L2/R2 with L1/R1, so the two read alike), then the faces when the
names say it plainly ("X/Y  Turbo A/B", "A/B  A/B", "A/B/X/Y" when each is
its own name); otherwise the page splits, the faces on one and the rest on
the next. Genesis is one page: A C, B B, X Y, Y A, L1/R1 X/Z, START/SELECT
Start/Mode. (Built first with four rows and "Left/Right: more", until I
put the page in the heading.) "X/Y  Y/A" is a puzzle and never a row. A row's button and value
together are 26 characters at most: "START/SELECT  Right/Left soft key" ran past
the panel on the Pixel 2, so those two stay two rows. The launcher measures
every page with the font (`--controls-fit`, in check-controls-shots). The page
is made when it opens and when it turns, never in a frame of the game and
never at its start.

There is no save-state or screenshot hotkey: Save and Load are menu rows.
MENU+L1 no longer switches a DS game's screens: L2 is the one way, and
L1 under MENU is the DS's own L.

### 11.29 System order

My picks in the same lab. The home lists the systems **A to Z** by their
full name, as the Gallery shows them, on a new card and after an upgrade:
case and accents do not count, numbers count as numbers (3DO before 32X,
Atari 2600 before Atari Lynx). **Favorites** stays first; **Ports** and **MP4**
stay last, after the consoles; the arcade shelves (Arcade, MAME, NAOMI,
Atomiswave) take their places in A to Z with the rest. Sorted once when the
card is read (`cfg_sort_systems`), never per frame.

**System Order** in MENU, under Home Screen: **A to Z** or **Release Date**,
systems.cfg's own order (by US release date, its comments say why). Left,
right and A step it round, heard as a ring; the shelves are read again in the
new order at once, the cursor kept on its system, as File Transfer reads them
when it closes. It also decides what Up and Down do on the home (11.1): three
systems with Release Date, a letter with A to Z.

### 11.30 Languages

ZyperOS speaks **English**, **简体中文**, **Español**, **Português
(Brasil)**, **Français**, **Deutsch**, **日本語** and **Italiano** *(
2026-11-07: "the most famous 5 languages, including portuguese", his picks
in the languages lab, docs/design/mock/languages-lab.html; then "add german,
italian, and japanese")*: in that order wherever they are listed, the five
by how many speak them and the three he added after them, each named in its
own words. Hindi and Arabic would need a text shaper and right to left.
Every word a player reads comes from one table,
`res/strings/`, a block a string and a line a language, in the terms of
`res/strings/GLOSSARY.md`: the launcher's screens, the in-game menu, the
charging and low battery screens, the File Transfer page and the boot
screen's line (11.20). tools/i18n.py makes the tables; `T(id)` is a load
and an add, and a change of language swaps two pointers, so a frame costs
in French what it cost in English.

- **MENU > Language**, after Date & Time and Clock, the regional settings
  together. Its value is the language's own name, so a player who chose one
  they cannot read finds their own word there. A opens a panel of the eight,
  each in its own words and its own face, a tick (a stroke heavier than the
  finish's) on the one that is on and the cursor starting there; A chooses
  and the menu under it is in the new language at once; B leaves it.
- **The first start** asks before anything else: Zyper as on the welcome,
  his bubble saying hello in the language under the cursor, the languages
  beside him in **menu** rows of 75 with an A on the focused one, five shown
  and the panel's marks (11.3) where there are more. Until the first press
  the cursor steps to the next language every second, so the screen greets
  in each in turn *(My note)*; a press stops it, UP and DOWN look, the
  screen speaks the focused language at once, and A keeps it and goes on to
  the welcome in it, Zyper already there. START skips the rest, as it skips
  every step, keeping the language UP or DOWN moved to, or else the one that
  was on: the cursor going round alone chooses nothing. MENU > Show Tutorial
  starts here too.
- **Capitals** are each language's own (12): Title Case is English's.
- **The File Transfer page** speaks the handheld's language, served as a
  file a language with the page.
- English until a first start or the menu says otherwise. The setting is
  kept in the device database as its code (`pt-BR`), written to theme.txt
  for the boot screen (11.21) and to the card's `.userdata`.

`make check-strings` holds every string complete in all eight with English's
printf conversions, and every character in its face; `make
check-i18n-shots` shoots the main screens in each language and fails on a
string cut, broken past its lines, slid in a marquee or drawn off the
screen, and on a screen where a language cuts or slides more formatted
text ("vinculado: tarik") than English does. The
pseudo-locale, `--lang xx` on a host, writes every string accented, 40%
longer and in brackets, to find what a longer translation would meet.

### 11.31 Delete Game

A game's details (X on the shelf) end in **Delete Game**, after Cover and
Video (11.34) *(2026-11-07: "when clicking X on a game to see details, allow
deleting that game. delete the game, saves, and media")*. A plain row, as
Cover and Video is: live on every shelf, with no value, since what it
takes is the question's to say. Never where the page opens, and A on it
only asks. A BIOS set on a shelf (`neogeo.zip`) is never deleted: A says so
in a one-line message (11.8), "neogeo is a BIOS other games need. It stays."

**A port** (the Ports shelf) is its script and the folders it runs from,
which its folder's `port.json` names (PortMaster writes it: its "items",
`["stardewvalley/", "StardewValley.sh"]`), or, with none, the folder its
script's `GAMEDIR` names (src/portinfo.h). Its question says so, "Delete
StardewValley? Its folders go too, its saves with them.", since a port
keeps its saves in them. A folder another port on the shelf names stays.
PortMaster itself, which every port runs on, never goes: "PortMaster runs
every port. It stays."; nor does a port nothing names a folder for:
"Nothing says which folders are Mystery's. It stays." Both are said before
anything is asked. *(2026-11-08, from Tarik's Pixel 2: Storage showed each
port as its script alone, and deleting one from there left its folders.
Ports had had no Delete Game, since nothing had said what was a port's.)*

- **The question** is the confirm panel every destructive row asks with
  (Forget, Unlink, Delete All, Roll Back): over the shelf on **scrim.menu**,
  heading **Delete Game**, then the question, then **Delete** and
  **Cancel**, a centered list, the cursor starting on Cancel; B and MENU are
  Cancel, POWER turns the handheld off with nothing deleted. What is new is
  that the question is a paragraph: it names the game, says what goes with
  it and that it cannot be undone, "Delete Metal Gear Solid? Its saves and
  states, cover and video go too. This cannot be undone." Its lines are
  wrapped to the panel and centered, as the one-line question of the other
  confirms is, in **text-dim** as a question row is, a **menu** note line
  each; a rule (the heading's) divides it from the answers when there is
  room. A question too long for the lines over its answers would leave its
  start where no cursor goes, so a game whose name does not fit is named in
  the heading instead, as on its details page, and the question says "this
  game".
- **The yes** draws one frame, "Deleting..." (11.8), deletes, closes the
  details page onto the shelf as it is now, and says what came of it in a
  one-line message: "Deleted. 1.2 GB given back.", or that some of its
  files would not go, or that nothing was deleted and why.
- **The shelf** changes in place, with no read of the card: the game is off
  its own shelf and Favorites, the cursor on the next game or the one
  before at the end. A shelf left empty goes from the home, as an empty
  system always has, and the home stands on the system in its place; with
  no games left, the empty home (11.19). Favorites keeps the game its
  cursor was on.

**What goes**, every path built from the game's key - its system's folder
and tag, its launch path, its name - and none found by a pattern or a
listing (src/gamedel.h): the game's file, or its folder for a game in one
(a multi-disc set, a ScummVM game); the tracks a `.cue`, `.gdi` or `.toc` at
the top of the folder names, a `.cue`'s `.sbi`, a `.ccd`'s `.img` and
`.sub`; an arcade set's `.zip`, and on NAOMI and Atomiswave the folder of its
name holding its CHD; its saves (`Saves/<stem>.srm`, `.sav`, `.dsv`, and the
arcade cores' own folders); the six state slots and Auto with their
pictures; its cover and video snap, the cover Cover and Video put aside (11.34) and
what a fetch stopped halfway left;
its cached achievement set; and its rows in the library: details and
title, favorite, play sessions. A file another game on the card builds too
stays. A path that would leave `Roms/<console>/`, `Saves/` or the states
folder refuses the whole delete, with nothing touched. Earned achievements
stay: they are the player's.

Host shots: `--info-delete` (the details, the cursor on Delete Game),
`--delete-ask` (the question), `--gm-said K` (its messages and Rename's),
`--delete-now` (the shelf after the yes).

### 11.32 Storage

MENU > **Storage**, just above System Update, is how the card is used, as a
phone's Settings shows its storage *(2026-11-07: "an interface in our menu
that shows how our storage is. Like Apple does on the iPhone."; my picks in
the Storage lab, `docs/design/mock/storage-lab.html`)*. The row's value is
the room left, "7.4 GB free", asked of the card at most every 2 s, as About
asks it.

**The page** is a full page on **ground**, as System Update's (11.17):

- "Storage" in **label** **text-soft** centered at cap y 56, and the card's
  size as it is sold, "128 GB", at its right in **meta** **text-dim**, where
  a panel of pages keeps its count (11.3). A card of 127.9 GB is 128 GB: the
  next size cards come in when the device is within a tenth under it.
- At cap y 128 the room used, its number in **title** **text** and its words
  in **menu** **text-soft** ("120.6 GB used", the words where the language
  puts them), and the room left at the right in **menu** **text-soft**.
- **The bar**, x 64 to 960, y 172, 32 tall, a pill: the parts in a fixed
  order, square where they meet and 3 apart on the ground (**frame**), the
  free room the **track** under them all; a part with anything in it is at
  least 4 wide.

  | Part | Is | Color |
  |---|---|---|
  | Games | each system's `Roms/<folder>` but its `.media` | **store-games** |
  | Saves | `Saves/` and the states | **store-saves** |
  | Art | covers and video snaps, the systems' `.media` | **store-art** |
  | Media | Music, Videos, Audiobooks, Menu Music | **text-soft** |
  | ZyperOS | the `ZyperOS/` folder, `Emulators/` and the system's partitions beside the card's filesystem | **accent-ink** |
  | Other | BIOS, the library and settings, the filesystem's own tables: what is used less everything above | **text-dim** |

  Lime is ZyperOS, as everywhere; the three parts of games have a hue each,
  none of warm, pink or red-orange, whose meanings are taken (3.3). A
  system's own color would have been its own, but the two largest on my
  card, the PlayStation's and the Nintendo DS's, are both grey.
- **The legend** at cap y 240, **meta** **text-dim**, a 20-unit dot and the
  part's name, 26 between, centered; a longer language takes two lines, and
  the list a row less.
- **The list** from y 266: rows as a menu's (1.5 **menu** lines, the focus an
  **accent** fill that glides in **quick**, the window moving a whole row at
  a time, never round), five at a time with a rule's room. First at most two
  **recommendations**, a sparkle in **accent-ink** for their mark, the larger
  saving first, shown when one gives back 10 MB or more, a rule under them;
  then everything by size: each system with its home sprite (its games, its
  saves and states, its art), Videos, Music, Audiobooks and BIOS with
  their folder pictures from the File Transfer page (Music's vinyl album,
  Videos' tape, Audiobooks' book with headphones, BIOS' chip), ZyperOS with Zyper's
  face, and Other with the small folder. The more-rows triangles of 11.3.
- **Under the list**, the focused row's facts in **meta** **text-dim**, at
  most two balanced lines 40 apart, the last at cap y 708 (the caption
  inset): "116 games · saves and states 1.3 GB · art 900 MB", "318 songs",
  what a recommendation is. Their room is the page's tallest caption, and
  the list keeps the whole rows that fit over it, laid out by rules (src/ds/flex.h):
  a page whose captions take two lines in a longer language shows a row
  fewer, the same while the cursor moves, and the sort key never meets them.
  A row's text stands on the 64 margin, its fill 16 outside it. A name too
  long for its row ends in "..." as a menu row's does (`ui_fit_text`), its
  room ending 24 before the size; on the focused row it rests so, then
  slides to its end and back.

**A system's page**: its name as the heading, its sprite at the left (x 56,
y 92, 112 square) where System Update has Zyper, its size in **title** and its
games' count at the right, its own bar of games, saves and art with each
part's size in the legend. Then **Delete Video Snaps** when it has snaps
(Video Snaps' own delete, 11.19), a rule, and its games, largest first: a
game's size is what Delete Game gives back (11.31's plan, summed), its facts
"Played 22h 5m · last played 3mo ago · saves and states 69 MB" or "never
played". A on a game is Delete Game's question, as it is, over this page;
the page is what every panel it opens stands on. B goes back to the card,
on the system's row.

**On the Ports shelf's page** a port's size is its script with its own
folders and cover (11.31's plan), PortMaster's its script with its folder,
and each folder no script on the shelf names is a row of its own, by its
`port.json`'s title (a port whose script is gone, "Grand Theft Auto: Vice
City") or by its name: "Its script is not on the card, so the shelf cannot
start it." A on it asks before its folder goes; PortMaster's folder is never
one. On every system's page a last row, **Other Files**, holds what no game
on it owns (art of games gone, a loose file), so the rows come to the
system's whole. *(2026-11-08, from Tarik's Pixel 2.)*

**A media folder's page**: A on Music, Videos or Audiobooks *(2026-11-08:
"why can't I explore videos/songs in storage?")*. Its name as the heading,
its folder picture from the File Transfer page at the left (the first of its
two frames), its size in **title** and its songs, videos or books at the
right, and the bar its share of the card. Its rows are what MP4's shelf calls
an album (src/mp4lib.h): an album or a book in an artist's or an author's
folder, a folder at the top with no folder inside or with tracks of its own,
a track at the top; in Videos a film at the top, or a folder of them (a
series) whole. Each by its folder's name or its file's without the
extension; under the list "The Lowlights · 40 songs", "Jane Austen · 61
files", or a film's file name. Music's has **Menu Music** too, the menu's
own tracks in its `Menu Music` folder, which the card's Music row counts; A on it is
**deny** (MENU > Menu Music is where they are chosen). A last **Other
Files** holds what none of the rows does - a cover beside the albums, a
playlist, a hidden file - so the rows come to the folder's whole. A on an
album, a film or a book asks as Delete Game does, under the page's name:
"Delete Neon Harbor and everything in it? 812 MB. This cannot be undone."
(a file: "Delete Sintel? 4.2 GB."); MP4 stops first if it plays from it, and
the shelf is read again after. The walk finds the rows with the roots'
sizes, before the systems, from the cache too, so a page opened at once has
them; a delete takes its row and its bytes out at once. B goes back to the
card, on the folder's row.

**The order**, X on every list of the page: the card's, a system's (the
Ports shelf's with its folders), a media folder's *(2026-11-08: "can we have
like press X to change sorting? sort by size, by alphabetic, by last
played")*. X steps Size, A to Z, Last Played and round, with **move**'s
sound, and the cursor stays on its row. Size is the largest first; A to Z
by the row's name, case not counting; Last Played the newest first (a
system's by its newest game), then what was never played by size. A media
folder has no Last Played, as MP4 keeps no record of what was played, so X
there steps Size and A to Z. Every order falls back to the others and then
to the row's place, so a list never reshuffles between two draws (src/sort.h's
rule). The recommendations stay first on the card, and Delete Video Snaps on
a system's page. Each list keeps its order, as Sort By keeps a shelf's: the
card's, each system's by its tag, each media folder's. Under the list at the
right, on the more-rows mark's line, X's glyph and the order it is now
("Size", "A to Z", "Last Played") in **meta** **text-dim**: the button and
what it is, as the keyboard's action row names its keys (11.5).

The page is a base, as MP4's shelf is: MENU's panel leaves over it as it
opens, and a panel opened on it - a question, "Deleting...", a result - comes
from nothing, and leaves over it when it closes. *(2026-11-08, on the Pixel
2: the question first opened as a swap from the last panel drawn, which left
over the page before the question came, so a panel showed, went and came
back.)*

**The recommendations**, each a question as Delete Game's (a paragraph, a
rule, the answer, Cancel focused), then "Deleting...", then "Deleted. 1.2 GB
given back.":

- **Unused Art**: the covers and video snaps in a system's `.media` of no
  game on its shelf, by the name Delete Game uses for them, a fetch's
  leftovers with them; a dotfile (a Mac's `._` twin) is not a cover.
  "A fetch can bring them back."
- **Old Save States**: the states of a game none of whose states was written
  in a year and that was not played in one, or is no longer on the card.
  Saves never: "Their saves stay."

Each lists its folders again and holds each file to its rule at the moment
it deletes it, so nothing a stale number counted goes.

**Measuring** (src/storage.h): allocated bytes, the clusters a file holds
(128 KB on a 128 GB exFAT card), so the parts come to what the card says is
used. A thread walks the card at the lowest CPU and I/O priority there is,
only while the page is open, never while a game runs; each folder is kept
with its time in `ZYPEROS_SHARED/storage.cache`, and a refresh lists again
only the folders whose time moved, so the page opens at once with the last
numbers and they move to the new ones as the walk reports. When Other moves
more than 64 MB with no folder's time to say why (a card changed on a
computer that kept the times), every folder is listed again. The first time,
with nothing cached, the room used and left are there at once, the bar is
striped where the walk has not been, and Zyper, his head cocked, stands over
"Measuring the card", the system it is in, "PlayStation · 41 of 116", and
"Only the first time. After this it opens at once."

Host shots: `--storage main|system|art|states|delete|done|first|music|videos|books|media-delete|videos-delete [ROW]`
and `--storage-sort size|az|last`, on a stand-in card of 128 GB nearly full;
`tools/storage-shots.py` takes them all in both themes and every language.
The numbers, the cache, the media pages' rows and their deletes, and X's
orders are held by `make check-storage`; what fits, in every language, by
`make check-i18n-shots`.

---

### 11.33 Install PortMaster

The Ports shelf installs the official PortMaster (portmaster.games) when it
is not on the card (src/pminstall.h). It is the shelf's own entry and System
Update's page (11.17) with its own words, and its own art.

- **The art** (`res/portmaster/`, copies of the system art's
  `res/system-art/original/utility`), drawn for ZyperOS, with no part of
  PortMaster's logo or any brand's art: a dock crane lowering a game
  cartridge (ZyperOS's lime label with a gear, gold contacts at its foot)
  onto a stack of shipping containers in Ports' pink, blue and teal, on a
  quay over the water, in the home's pixel art. **The cover** is 512 by 656,
  a generated card's size, with its corners and the band of Ports' color
  across the top, so it stands in a row of generated cards as one of them;
  one file a theme, as a generated card is: its sky is the card's slab, with
  the sun in Beard and the moon in Salt & Pepper; the crane, the cartridge
  and the water are the same in both. It is the entry's cover on the card or not, on the Ports
  shelf, Favorites, any order of either and the port's loading screen, in
  place of box art: a scrape skips PortMaster, and its details have no Box
  Art row. MENU > Storage's Ports page lists games as words (11.32), so it
  has no picture there. **The icon** is the crane, cartridge and containers
  on a piece of quay with nothing behind them, on the Install PortMaster page
  where System Update's has Zyper, at 0.8 of its 256 square. *(2026-11-08: I wanted
  PortMaster to have art of its own in ZyperOS's style.)*

- **The entry** is PortMaster's, `Roms/Ports/PortMaster.sh`, on the Ports
  shelf whether it is on the card or not, wherever the card's ZyperOS has
  the installer (the Pixel 2): the same name, cover and favorite before and
  after, so installing it changes no place on the shelf. While it is not on
  the card, A's hint says **Install** and the line under its name **Not
  installed** (11.1's line, its first part). A shelf with nothing but this
  entry is still no game to the empty home (11.19) and to Random Game.
- **The page**, A on the entry: heading **Install PortMaster**; "PortMaster"
  in **title** where the versions go; under it the facts, "By the
  PortMaster team", or the version while it works; two
  points where What's New goes, what PortMaster is and that some ports need
  a game you own, kept up while it looks, downloads and unpacks; where the
  bar goes, "It downloads from GitHub over Wi-Fi." or, with no way out,
  "Connect to Wi-Fi to download." and no A. The keys are **Install**
  (Try Again after a problem) and **Back**; while it works only **Back, it
  goes on**, as System Update's download goes on.
- **The bar** is System Update's: "Downloading" and "9.8 of 24 MB", then
  "Checking the download" full, then "Unpacking" by bytes.
- **Done:** "Version 2026.06.23-0015 · installed", two points (it is on the
  Ports shelf; open it to get ports, it updates itself), **Open PortMaster**
  and **Back**. The shelf's hint says Play from then on.
- **A problem** is said in full where the points go, as System Update's
  (no GitHub, a download cut short, a checksum that does not match, a full
  card with the room it needs, a card that refused a write, a release list
  that makes no sense, a stop), with Try Again. A system with no Python
  (from before PortMaster could run) says to update it in MENU > System
  Update, and has only Back.

- **Reinstall PortMaster** is the same page with its own heading, for a
  PortMaster that is on the card (`pm-install.py --reinstall`): a fresh copy
