  of the latest stable release, the ports beside it and their saves never
  touched, its runtimes (`libs/*.squashfs`), downloaded themes and waiting
  autoinstall zips kept, its settings (`config/`) started over. The offer
  names the version on the card and has two points (what stays, the
  settings start over), **Reinstall** and **Back**; then the install's bar,
  "A fresh PortMaster is on the card." and **Open PortMaster**. A reinstall
  that fails or is cut off leaves the PortMaster that was there, and the
  page names it. It opens from a **Reinstall PortMaster** row on
  PortMaster's details page (**X**), where another game's page has Cover and
  Video, above Delete Game; and A on the entry while a reinstall still runs
  opens it again rather than a PortMaster about to be swapped.
  *(2026-11-12: a player's PortMaster closed at every start after its own
  update and a few ports, with no way back but deleting it.)*
- **A PortMaster that closed with an error** opens this page by itself when
  the launcher comes back (launch.sh's `/tmp/zyperos_pm_ran`, its log read
  by `pm_failure`): the problem in full where the points go ("PortMaster
  closed with an error. Reinstall it to fix it: your ports and their saves
  stay."), the line of its log that says how where the bar goes, in
  PortMaster's own English, and **Reinstall**. An error is its program
  killed by a signal, a fatal Python error, an exception nothing caught
  with no more than a few lines after it in the log (a traceback it logged
  and went on from is none), or no display; and a start that left no log
  and ended within 20 s.
- **Before each start**, the launcher removes a file of PortMaster's
  `config/` that a cut-off write left (empty, cut short, zeros: the whole of
  the ones up to 256 KB checked, the ends of the bigger), and its settings
  with a cut-off source list; PortMaster makes them again. Its stable
  release 2026.06.23-0015 never starts again with a cut-off
  `runtimes.json`. And launch.sh puts PortMaster's own `PortMaster.sh` back
  over the shelf's each start, as PortMaster's update replaces only the
  folder's copy on a system it has no layout for.

Host shots: `--portmaster [running,nonet,nopython,installed,repair,failed]`,
the page as the file ZYPEROS_PM_STATUS names says (`failed` with the log
line ZYPEROS_PM_WHY gives).

### 11.34 Cover and Video

A game's details (X on the shelf) have **Cover and Video** where Get Box Art
and Replace Box Art were, and it opens a page of its own: the cover on the
shelf beside what the sources offer, each named with its source, picked with
the d-pad and A; and the game's video snap. *(2026-11-09: "Currently I just
see 'Trocar capa' which is confusing. I don't know what it is changing to, I
can't pick, and I don't know what goes on with the video preview." Approved
as mocked the same day, with libretro asked first and one level of undo.)*
The row is live with no network too, since what is on the card still works
there; its value is what is missing, "no cover" or "no video", and none when
both are there. PortMaster's entry, whose art is its own (11.33), has no row.

**The page** is a full page on **ground** under the top bar (11.12), the
game's name in the bar's row between the clock and the marks, as a video's
title is (11.14), sliding when it is longer. A base, as Storage's page is
(11.32): the details panel leaves over it, and a question opens on it.

- **Now**, the heading in **meta** **text-dim** at x 64, cap y 116, and the
  cover the shelf shows in a 264 x 232 box standing on y 384, in its own
  shape, never stretched; under it at cap y 420 its source ("libretro",
  "ScreenScraper", "SteamGridDB") when the card knows it, "No cover yet" with
  the generated card (11.1) when there is none.
- **Found**, the heading at x 352, and at its right the count, "1 / 9", or
  while a source is asked, "Asking ScreenScraper" in **text-soft** with the
  status corner's three dots (11.12); the source's name alone where the
  words would meet the heading. Under them **the strip**: the focused
  picture in a 264 x 232 box with an **accent** ring 7 out and
  **focus-small**'s lift, the others at three quarters and a little faded,
  24 apart, one before the cursor and as many after as the screen holds,
  every picture standing on y 384. A picture still coming is its place, a
  **raised** card. Under the strip the focused one's kind and region in
  **menu** **text** at cap y 420 ("Box Front · USA"; in **meta** where
  that does not fit), and its source in **meta** **text-dim** at cap y 462
  ("SteamGridDB · Midgarsormr", the artist's name dropped where it would
  meet the hint), with A's glyph and **Look Closer** at the right.
- **Before**, first in the strip after a change: the cover there was, with a
  **Before** chip (a **surface** chip with its **edge**, **radius-chip**,
  29-unit words); A on it is **Put Back**, at once. A picture the same as the
  one on the shelf carries a **Now** chip.
- **Look Closer** (A on a picture): the picture as large as fits 592 x 488
  at x 368, lifted, Now beside it at its own size with a chevron between;
  its kind and region in **menu** **text** centered under it at cap y 640,
  its source and "4 / 9" in **meta** at cap y 682; B's **Back** at the
  left and A's **Use as Cover** at the right of the foot, cap y 724.
  Left/Right go through the pictures there. The small copy stands in, with a
  bar under it, until the whole picture has come. A makes it the cover; the
  page comes back with it as Now and the one there was first, as Before.
- **The video**, under a 2-unit **edge** rule at y 488: **Video** in
  **meta** at cap y 524, its picture in a 256 x 192 tile at x 64, y 552, in
  its own shape, with the shelf's play mark (11.19) while the cursor is on
  the strip; it plays muted while a video row has the cursor. Its rows from
  y 504, x 344 to 960, as a menu's: **Watch** ("12 s"), **Replace** (the
  size on offer) and **Delete** (its size) for a game with one;
  **Download** for one with none. A row's value falls to **meta** where it
  would meet its label. With no video, the tile says why in **meta**:
  "No video", "Looking it up" while ScreenScraper is asked, "None found";
  while one downloads, a bar under the tile fills and the row says "1 MB /
  2 MB". **Watch** plays it on the whole screen under the top bar, with its
  sound (none while MP4 plays music), B's **Back** at the foot. **Delete**
  asks first, as 11.31's question does.
- **No network** (Wi-Fi not connected, which on a Pixel 2 with no adapter is
  always, though its USB cable's network is up; or every source asked gave no
  answer): where the strip would be, "Connect to Wi-Fi first." in
  **menu** (on two lines where one does not hold it) and "Or add covers from
  a computer with File Transfer." in **meta**, Zyper at the right under
  them. Before still shows and still puts back.
- **The video's rows** are only the ones that work: Watch and Delete with a
  video on the card, Download (Replace, with one) only while ScreenScraper
  offers a video or one is coming (2026-11-09: Download stood there with
  nothing to download). With no rows, the empty tile says "No video", "None
  found" or "Looking it up", and a line in **meta** where the rows would be
  says why: no Wi-Fi, ScreenScraper not linked (and where to link it), or
  ScreenScraper has no video of the game.
- **Nothing found**: "Nothing found." and a line a source in **meta**,
  "libretro · no match", "ScreenScraper · unknown game", "SteamGridDB · not
  linked" (or "no answer"), with Zyper.

Up and Down go between the strip and the rows, the strip one stop above
them; Left and Right move along the strip, stopping at its ends (11.3's
rule). B leaves Look Closer, then the page. The cursor opens on the strip,
or on the video's first row when there is nothing to pick (no network and no
Before).

**The sources**, asked when the page opens, one request at a time (net.c's
one slot), each answer appended so no picture moves: **libretro** first,
needing no account and answering at once (its box, title screen and
screenshot, by the game's name, else by its catalog's, as a Box Art run
matches); then **ScreenScraper** with an account, one lookup that also
keeps the game's text and offers its box in three regions (the shelf's order
of regions), its 3D box, title screen and screenshot, and its video snap;
then **SteamGridDB** with a key, three of its covers. ScreenScraper's
pictures are each a request on the account, so only the small copies of the
pictures on screen are fetched (at most 256 across), and the whole (512) only
for Look Closer; libretro's come whole with the answer. They land in RAM and
are decoded off the frame (src/mediatex.h).

**On the card**, the cover is `.media/<name>.png` as before; Use as Cover
copies the picture in beside it and renames it over, the one there was
renamed to `.media/.before/<name>.png` first; Put Back swaps the two. Where
each came from is kept in the library database (src/artsrc.h), and a Box Art
run records its covers' too. Delete Game takes Before with the game (11.31),
Storage counts it as the game's art and as unused art once the game is gone
(11.32). The video is `.media/<name>.mp4`, downloaded beside it and renamed
over only once it starts as an MP4 does.

Host shots: `--media page|look|video|videoget|fetching|offline|nothing|after|novideo|delete|failed [TILE]`,
`--media live` (the real sources for the game under the cursor, over the network, then the page; never in a check),
from a fixture, with `--media-art DIR`'s pictures (now.png, before.png,
c0.png..., snap.png) or the generated card; `--info-art`, the details as for
a game with a cover and no video.

### 11.35 File Transfer's heading and steps
The transfer panel (11.3) of USB and Wi-Fi File Transfer, 2026-11-10. Its
heading, in **label** **text-soft**, has the handheld's name on the network
beside its address and the PIN. Over the cable the name comes first:
"http://zyperos.local" on the first line, "10.42.0.1 · PIN 4071" on the
second, since only the computer is on that link. Over Wi-Fi the address
comes first, as it was, and the name beside the PIN: "192.168.1.42" over
"PIN 4071 · zyperos.local", since anyone on the network could answer for a
name, and what a person types should come from the handheld's own screen.
The name over the cable is written with http:// so a browser opens it
rather than searching for it; with a port other than 80 both carry it. With
no name (multicast DNS did not start, or gave the name up after three taken
in a session) or no link (the heading then says "Cable unplugged" or "Wi-Fi
went away"), the heading is the address over the PIN.

**The way in** (2026-11-12: "while nobody inputted the PIN yet ... show the
URL/IP/PIN full size in the popup; only after someone connected we show the
stats"). Until someone has come in (a browser gave the right PIN, or, over
the cable, an SSH session opened), the panel is not the stats but what to
type, at full size. In a header of 352 under no heading: the screen's name
in **meta** **text-dim** where a heading would be; the first thing to type
in **title** **text**, a step down to **label** and then **menu** where it
does not fit whole (a port on the name, a long address), and sliding past
that, never cut; the other way to reach it under it in **menu**
**text-soft**; "PIN" in **meta** **text-dim**; and the PIN as one key a
digit, 80 by 96, a **raised** fill in an **edge** frame (DS_FRAME) with
DS_RADIUS_KEY, the digit in **title** **text**, as the page's four boxes
take it. The order is the heading's: over the cable "http://zyperos.local"
first and "10.42.0.1" under it; over Wi-Fi the address first and the name
under it. With no name the address is alone. Under the header one live row
says where the link is (**Cable** "unplugged" or "waiting", **Wi-Fi** "not
connected", **Address** "waiting", **Browser** "waiting", or **PIN**
"waiting" once a browser has the page), then the rule and the key legend
(11.4), "B: close".

**Connecting** (2026-11-12: "usb file transfer takes a while to start, we
need a loading state"). Over the cable the address and the PIN cannot work
until the computer has taken the handheld, opened its network on it and
taken its address: a few seconds, and more the first time Windows sees it.
Until then, on a device that can see those steps (the Pixel 2), the way in's
panel says so in the same place and size: the screen's name, "Connecting to
the computer" in **label** **text** (**menu** where a language's words do not
fit it whole) with its cap centre 160 down the header, and under it a
**bar** 420 wide at 232 on **track**, filling with **accent-ink**, a third
for each of the three. While one is waited on, its third creeps toward three
quarters of the way, 63% of that in 3 s (never the whole: the computer has
not done it yet); no computer, or one waiting for power, does not creep.
The fill eases up in 150 ms and drops at once when a step is lost (the cable
out). The step row under the rule says which step it is on. Once the address
is in, the bar runs to its end and the way in takes its place.

The hotspot screen (11.16) keeps its code, network and password and PIN
until someone comes in; it is its own way in.

Once someone has come in, the news, and it stays when every browser
leaves: the way in comes back only when the screen opens again, with a new
PIN. The news is Flash Drive's page (11.18), not a panel *(2026-11-13: "can't
we use the same upload/download popup design system in wifi file transfer
as we do for flash drive mode?")*: the panel is at most 648 tall, and over
the cable its heading and rows already took 487 of it, so Flash Drive's
tiles did not fit beside them. It opens, closes and is kept for its way out
as the hotspot's screen is (PANEL_MOVE), the whole screen being the panel.
Its top is Flash Drive's, the page frame of `src/ds/page.h` (section 15):
Zyper (124) at (123, 168), and beside him at x 208 the two-line heading
above (DS_HEAD_TWO), its first line in **title** **text** and its
second in **title** **text-soft** (**label** where a line does not fit
whole, sliding past that, never cut). Then Flash Drive's two tiles where it
has them, drawn by the same code (ud_tile): **Download**, to the card (a
browser's upload), and **Upload**, from it (a browser's download), each with
its mark, its speed once a second and its total since the screen opened, in
Flash Drive's words, units and rules (1000-based, "0 MB/s" when still). A
file's bytes only, as the side lights count them (11.27): the page's own
lists and covers are not a transfer. A way that moved in the last 2 s has
its mark in **accent-ink** and keeps its speed through a pause between two
files. What Flash Drive cannot know, File Transfer shows: while an upload of
a known size is on its way, a **bar** at Download's foot (22 up from the
tile's bottom, the tile's padding in from each side) on **track**, filled in
**accent-ink** as far as it has come; and where Flash Drive's eject line is
(592), **Now** and what happened ("receiving Tobu Tobu Girl.gb", the file's
name), the label **text-dim** and the value **text-soft** in **meta**; at
640 **Browsers** and, over the cable, **SSH** on one line, a dot between
them; and B's hint at the foot (720) as Flash Drive's: "close", "keep going
or stop" while files move, "hide" while it closes after the file on its
way. Either line slides where it is too long. The In / out row is gone: the
tiles say it. Play after this file's wait (11.23) is the same page, its
heading the screen's name and "Finishing Tobu Tobu Girl.gb..." where the
news is, B "back to the shelf". On the hotspot the code goes and the heading
is the network and its password over the PIN, for another phone to join;
where the two do not fit one line (Chinese), the password moves down beside
the PIN, so neither slides. Host shots: `--hare ADDR WHO FILE --hare-move
idle|in|out|both|closing|finish|gone` (10.42.0.1 for the cable, with SSH),
and `--hotspot ... --hotspot-in`.

**The steps.** A step before the browser that waits too long (the cable 5
seconds with no computer and 10 with one, the address 10) means the address
cannot work yet, so its hint takes the way in's place: under the two-line
heading the steps of the connection as the handheld sees it
(src/linksteps.h), over the cable **Cable**, **Address** and **Browser**,
over Wi-Fi **Wi-Fi** and **Browser**. A step that is done has MENU_TICK as
its value; the step waited on is a live row, its value "waiting" (or
"unplugged" for a cable with no computer, "not connected" for no Wi-Fi);
under it the hint, one sentence saying what to do about that step, in
**menu** **text-dim**, centered and balanced onto at most three lines, in
place of the other steps, then the rule and the legend. Three steps and a
three-line hint do not fit under a two-line heading, and a hint is about
one step. The hints name the things a person can act on: another cable or
port, a USB-A to USB-C cable (for a computer that did not connect, 11.36),
Windows' Device Manager and its Network adapters, a VPN or proxy. A
browser that does not come has no hint: the way in already shows what to
type, at full size. The way in comes back as the step moves on, and the
steps come back if the cable or the Wi-Fi goes before anyone came in.

Host shots: `--hare-steps usb|wifi STATE [noname]`, STATE one of unplugged,
taking, opening, nocomputer, noanswer, nodriver, address, noaddress,
browser, nobrowser, waits, replug, nonetwork and pin (a stalled step shows
the steps; over the cable a step before the browser shows Connecting, its
step waited on for 1.5 s; the rest the way in); `--hare-addr
ADDR` for the way in's address (a long one, for the fit); `--hotspot SSID
PASS PHONES NOW --hotspot-in` for the hotspot after someone came in.

### 11.36 What did you plug in?
The question the shelf asks when something on the Pixel 2's USB port wants
power and gives none (plastron's usbrole, src/usbpower.h), 2026-11-10: a
USB accessory (headphones, a Wi-Fi adapter, a keyboard), a computer
whose port took the sink side, or a phone, which the handheld cannot tell
apart without powering it. A list since 2026-11-13 (Tarik: "improve the
what did you plug in screen design"; three tiles 272 wide left USB
Accessory's examples three lines and the other two none): heading **What
did you plug in?** over its rule, then three rows the panel's width (the
screen's insets), as a phone's "Use USB for" is, each **raised**
(**accent** when focused) with **radius.card**, 100 tall at least: its mark
(64) centered in a 96 square at the left in **text-soft** (**on-accent**
focused), and beside it its name in **menu** **text** over what it does in
**meta** **text-dim** (both **on-accent** focused), one line, two where a
language needs them: **USB Accessory**, headphones, "headphones, Wi-Fi,
keyboard"; **Computer**, a laptop, "files, or the card as a flash drive";
**Phone**, a phone, "files in the phone's browser". Up and Down move (Left
and Right too, as the tiles did), so the button hints (11.4) are "A Choose
B Computer", as a menu's. It starts on Computer, so a quick A or B never
powers a computer. Host shot: `--usb-power [0|1|2]`. Asked once per plug, two seconds after usbrole says it may be,
over the lit shelf, never over a game (a plug made in a game is asked about
back on the shelf); it goes by itself when the plug stops wanting power.
Every plug is its own: nothing carries over from the plug before, however
soon (a cable swapped for an adapter in a second, 2026-11-10). One thing does: an accessory powered when the handheld turns off is
powered again at the next start, with no question, when it is the first
plug and there from the start (usbpower_resume; the handheld cannot see
what happened while it was off, so a computer put in its place gets the 2 s
Automatic gives one). And a question that comes by itself, this one or
Computer Connected (11.18), came while the player was pressing through the
menus, and a press meant for them answered it (Discord, 2026-11-11): a
button that was down as it appeared counts only once let go and pressed
again, and so does one pressed in its first half second; after that a
press answers at once, POWER always. Its button hints show from the half
second, their room kept.

**Phone.** A phone takes the side that waits for power at every plug: its
port prefers it, where a computer's takes either at random (Tarik, an iPhone
and an Android phone, 2026-11-13: Computer, then the replug message, again
and again, for good). The handheld has no Type-C controller and no USB Power
Delivery, so it cannot ask the phone to swap, and on a USB-C to USB-C cable
a phone is always the device; Android's "USB controlled by: This device" did
not swap either (an AYN Thor, 2026-11-13). But powered, a phone is on the
handheld's bus as a USB device (the iPhone, 05ac:12a8 "iPhone"; the Thor,
18d1:4e11), so the handheld is its host, and a phone's **USB tethering**
makes it a USB network device that shares its network over the cable
(src/phonelink.h). So Phone powers it as USB Accessory does and puts up the
phone message, the replug message's slab with the phone mark under
**Phone**: "On the phone, turn on USB tethering (Settings, Hotspot and
tethering). USB File Transfer then opens here.", and under it, where
Computer's cable line goes and up to three lines, "No USB tethering? Use
Wi-Fi File Transfer, or a USB-C to USB-A adapter on the phone for Flash
Drive." Some Android devices have no USB tethering at all (the Thor offers
it neither in Settings nor in its USB notification, 2026-11-13), and Flash
Drive needs the adapter on any phone (the phone is the device here, never
the computer that opens a drive). Its driver (rndis_host for Android's
RNDIS, cdc_ncm or cdc_ether for NCM and ECM; plastron's modules) is loaded by usbrole by the phone's alias;
the launcher renames its interface `phone0` while it is down (the kernel
named it `usb0` when that was free, and USB File Transfer's gadget needs
that name), brings it up and asks the phone for a lease with udhcpc, as
Wi-Fi does (its default route through the phone too: the clock syncs). With
an address, **USB File Transfer** opens by itself on the shelf, once a link
(phonelink_new), its way in the address the phone gave in **title**, "in the
phone's browser" under it where the cable's shows the name (no mDNS there),
and the PIN; no steps (the link is there before it opens), no SSH; the
phone message goes. Only for the plug answered Phone: a USB network on a
plug powered as USB Accessory or by Automatic gets no lease and no File
Transfer by itself (anything can say it is one); MENU's row is **USB File
Transfer** while the phone's link has its address, and opens it again. An **iPhone** shares its network over USB only with
a computer it trusts (paired, through Apple's usbmuxd; Linux's ipheth answers
nothing until then), which the handheld cannot be: once the bus shows one
(Apple's and not "Mac", phonelink_is_iphone), its own message takes Phone's
place: "An iPhone can't open the handheld's files on a USB-C to USB-C cable.
Use Wi-Fi File Transfer, or a USB-C to USB-A adapter on the iPhone and a
USB-A to USB-C cable." (no smaller line: it names the adapter). The phone's
messages stay while the port powers the phone, whose power reading is then
the port's own, and go with the cable out, another plug, power in while the
port is no host, or File Transfer opening (usbpower_said_ends). In **menu**
up to four balanced lines, else in **meta** up to six. Never on Automatic,
which powers a phone as any plug and asks nothing. Host shots: `--replug
phone|iphone`, `--hare-steps phone browser`.

**The phone's internet.** The phone's lease gives the handheld a default
route through the phone (udhcpc's default script; the Pixel 9 Pro XL's,
2026-11-13: 10.44.47.242/24, default via 10.44.47.248 dev phone0), so a
phone's tethering is a way out to the internet as Wi-Fi is. One rule says so
for every fetch, net_internet (phonelink_way_out): Wi-Fi joined, or a
default route on a link other than lo; never the USB cable to a computer,
which gives the handheld no route (it leads nowhere, 2026-11-09). It gates
MENU > Downloads, a game's Cover and Video, Box Art, Album Art, Achievements,
System Update and PortMaster's install, and with no radio Achievements, Box
Art and Album Art come back while it holds (sys_ui.online). The phone may be
on mobile data: when the route out with the lowest metric is phone0's
(metered), nothing large starts by itself. System Update still looks, as
that is small, but a download cut short waits for A (su_auto_step). Closing
USB File Transfer (B) leaves the link, its lease and its route (xfer_stop
never touches phone0), and File Transfer does not open again for the same
link (phonelink_new). The rows keep "needs Wi-Fi": with no radio and no
tethering they are not there at all.

**Asking again.** While a plug waits for an answer (put off, made in a
game, Computer by mistake), the corner shows the USB plug mark (11.12) and
MENU's first row is **USB Device**, value "choose": A asks the question
again for the same plug, no replug needed. Both go when the plug ends or is
powered.

**The setting.** MENU > **USB Accessories**, under File Transfer, Ask (the
default) or Automatic, stepped round as Clock is, as on a phone's USB
preferences. Automatic: usbrole powers a plug that has waited 2 s, under
every safety rule, and the question, the mark and USB Device are never
shown. Chosen, it says once, as a message of up to four balanced lines,
what it does and its one risk: a computer that has not connected yet gets
about 2 seconds of power before it is seen (on the bus, "Product: Mac") and
the port goes back for the rest of that plug. A computer seen pauses
Automatic until the port has rested empty 3 s or the player answers: a plug
meanwhile gets the question and the replug message (the Mac's port dropped
and came back half a second after each feed, and was fed every 4.5 s, on
the device, 2026-11-10). Told to usbrole in
/run/usbrole/mode, as the request is. Host shots: `--usb-menu
[auto|said]`, with `--status ... usb=on` for the mark.

**The offer.** The same Wi-Fi adapter, plugged in again and again, was
asked about every time (my ask, 2026-11-13: "cant zyper remember?"). So
the player's USB Accessory answers are counted on the device
(`usb_accessory_answers`), and after the second, while the setting is Ask
and the offer was never answered, the shelf offers Automatic once the
accessory has its power (the power waits for nothing): Delete Game's
confirm panel, **Power without asking?** over "Automatic powers what you
plug in after 2 seconds. A computer not connected yet gets about 2
seconds of power." in up to four balanced lines and the rule, then
**Automatic** and **Keep Asking**, the cursor on Keep Asking. It comes by
itself, so it takes a press as the question does (a button held through
its start, or pressed in its first half second, counts once let go).
Automatic is the setting's own, told to usbrole. Either answer, B too, is
kept (`usb_auto_offer`) and it is never offered again; POWER, Auto Off or
a quit answer nothing, and the next USB Accessory offers it again. On the
shelf only, after the question, so never over a game (an answer in MENU >
USB Device offers it back on the shelf). Host shot: `--usb-offer`.

**The replug message.** Computer, B or MENU, and a plug a computer showed
up on (fed by USB Accessory by mistake, or by Automatic), bring up "Your
computer didn't connect. It can take a few tries: unplug the cable and plug
it back in." (a few tries: the two ports settle which side gives power
at each plug, at random, 2026-11-11) over
whatever screen is up, never over a game, and it stays until the plug
ends: the cable out, another plug, or the computer connected. On the launcher it is a slab at
the top under the header, 960 wide at y 104: **surface** with the
**panel** lift, the laptop mark (64) at its left in **text-soft**,
**Computer** in **meta** **text-dim** over the words in **menu** **text**,
up to four balanced lines, and under them, 8 lower, in **meta**
**text-dim** up to two lines, the cable that always connects: "A USB-A to
USB-C cable connects every time." Some computers' USB-C ports take the side
that waits for power at every plug, as a phone's does, and then the tries
never end (a Windows 11 laptop with USB-C cables only, on Discord,
2026-11-13); a USB-A port always gives power, so with USB-A at the
computer's end the handheld gets power and Computer Connected asks at once.
Phone's message has no such line: it names the cable itself. The slab is
drawn by every screen's frame, under the
volume bar (plat_set_overlay), and last on a frame that skipped the bar
(plat_overlay_present); the home's focus frames, which redraw the machine
alone, stop while it is up (it was drawn under the animated machine on the
device, 2026-11-10; tools/home-shot-check.py holds it, `--replug`). USB File Transfer's Cable step says
it shorter after 5 seconds (11.35), under its "waiting" row, in the
hint's three lines: "Unplug the cable and plug it back in, or use a USB-A
to USB-C cable: it connects every time." The user-facing words never say "power".
*(My wording after the plug test, 2026-11-10.)* Host shots: `--usb-power
[0|1|2|said|phone|iphone]`, `--replug [phone|iphone]`.

### 11.37 Screensaver
After a while with nothing pressed on the shelf, the video snaps of random
games play over the whole screen, one after another, each named; **START**
boots the game on screen, as A on its card would, and any other button goes
back to the shelf as it was. *(A request, 2026-11-12: "a screensaver that
plays videos from random games in the collection; if you press Start on one
of the videos, that game boots".)* Host shot: `--saver [cover]`, with
`--saver-pic FILE` and `--saver-alpha F`.

**When.** **MENU > Screensaver**, on Display after Battery Icon: **off**, 30s, 1m, 2m,
5m or 10m with nothing pressed, Auto Off's ladder, stepped with Left and
Right; **1m** to start with, shorter than Auto Off's two minutes so that a
handheld on battery shows it before it powers off. It starts only on the
shelf, the home or a games shelf, once its delay has passed with no button
down. Never in a game, over a menu, MP4's shelf, its Now Playing or File Transfer, and
not while music plays, File Transfer is on behind the shelf, a long task
runs (11.38) or the Video view plays a snap (11.57: somebody watching it,
whose first press the screensaver would take): the countdown does not run
while any of them does, and a whole delay passes after it ends, as Auto Off's does on the charger. A game,
a menu or any other screen in between restarts it. The charger does not
hold it: a handheld on the cable shows it too. Under 20% on battery it does
not start (src/saver.h).

**What.** The shelf's loop goes on, and where it would draw the shelf it
draws this, so every other thing the shelf does keeps working: the power
button, Auto Off, the dark screen on the cable, a computer plugged in,
px2-open's commands. The picture on **ink**, which both themes keep for
content (MP4's full view, 11.14), as large as the screen takes it, in its
own shape, never stretched, fading up from black over **SAVER_IN_MS**
(400 ms, **out**), shown for 15 s (it loops when it is shorter) and fading
to black over 300 ms (**fade**, linear) before the next. The next is picked
at random from every game on the systems row's menu that has a snap on the
card and on for its console, never one of the last twelve shown; a clip
whose first picture does not come in 4 s is skipped. It is muted always,
whatever Video Snaps' Sound says for the shelf: the snap is asked of the
daemon the shelf's own snap uses (11.19), and a muted one opens no sound.

Two slabs in the notice's shape (11.7, **surface** at 85%, **radius.panel**),
fading with the clip. At the foot, 48 from the left and the bottom: the
game's system in **meta** **text-dim** behind a disc of its mark (as the
rail's), over its name in **label** **text**, as a notice says its heading
over its body, up to the screen's width less the margins, cut with an
ellipsis. At the top right, 48 in: **START**'s hint with **Play** (the games
shelf's word) over "Any other button goes back" in **chrome** **text-dim**.

**With no snap on the card** it shows the games' covers instead, one at a
time, each for 8 s, the cover in its own shape at most a third larger than it
is and above the foot's slab, on the same ink. A game with no cover is not
picked. With neither, **nothing**: it does not start, the shelf stays as it
is, and it does not try again until a button is pressed.

**Auto Off and the battery.** It never keeps the device on past Auto Off:
every pass of the shelf's loop asks idle_due before it asks the
screensaver, so Auto Off fires at its own time with the screensaver up as
with the shelf up, and nothing here touches its clock. On the charger Auto
Off's time darkens the screen (11.25), which ends the screensaver. A
delay as long as Auto Off's or longer is never seen on battery, since Auto
Off comes first. Its cost is kept low by what it does not do: it plays muted,
draws a frame only when the daemon has put out a new picture, while a fade
moves, for the volume bar and once a second (a cover is one drawn frame
for 8 s), and ends by itself, until a button, when the chip reports
"Getting hot" (11.23), the battery falls under 20% off the cable, a long
task begins, or - on battery only - it has run for 10 minutes, so a
forgotten handheld with Auto Off at never does not play video until it is
flat.

**Buttons.** START boots the game as Random Game's pick does (pick_launch:
the shelf is put on the game and A's launch runs). The volume and brightness
buttons do their own work and leave it be. POWER powers off as it does
everywhere. Any other button, MENU and SELECT too, leaves, and is spent
doing so: it opens nothing. Nothing is heard. *(Defaults mine: a minute, 15 s
a clip, START and then anything.)*

### 11.38 Long tasks and Auto Off
**One rule.** A job the device was asked to do holds Auto Off the way the
charger does: the countdown does not run while it works, and a whole fresh
one starts when it ends. The jobs: the scraper's art and video downloads
(a run of the Scraper's page, from MENU > Downloads or a console's Box Art),
System Update's download, check of its files and install, PortMaster's install (whichever
screen is up, or a game), Storage's walk of the card, File Transfer's copies
and connected browsers, and MP4's album covers. A Scraper or Achievements
run holds it behind the screens too, its page closed (11.19, src/dlq.h). The rule is one function,
`long_task_busy` in src/zyperdraw.c, asked by `idle_due` for every screen
and by the game's SETIDLE; a new long job is a line there or a `task_note`
from its loop (src/taskhold.h), never a clock reset of its own. A job that
is a process or a state of its own is asked; a loop says it each pass and the
note lasts 2 s, so a loop that ends by any road out stops holding by itself.
The battery running flat still ends everything, and the power button always
does. The log says it once when it changes: "idle: Auto Off waits, Scraper is
running", "idle: Auto Off is back, nothing long is running".
*(2026-11-12, a request: "While the scraper runs, Auto Off must not put
the device to sleep." It had been the other way on purpose - "a library left
scraping unattended on battery is exactly what Auto Off is for" - and was
the one job of these that did not hold.)*

### 11.39 Folders on a shelf, and the list

**Folders** (src/shelfdir.h, src/library.c). A folder in a system's folder
on the card is a folder of games on its shelf, to any depth: someone's
"Homebrew and Aftermarket", "Commercial Owned", "Rom Hacks/Translations".
The shelf shows one folder at a time: the folders in it first, A to Z, then
its own games in the shelf's Sort By order (a sort orders games, never the
folders). A on a folder opens it, on its first entry; B goes up one, its
cursor back on the folder it left, and at the top B goes to the systems as
it always did. X and Y on a folder have nothing to do and play **deny**.
The heading names the way down, "Game Boy Advance / Rom Hacks / Pokemon",
in the heading's place and style, sliding when it is too long; the count
beside it is the folder's entries. A folder with no game at any depth is
left out, as an empty system is, and is one line in the log's left-out
names.

A folder that **is** a game stays one game: on a shelf that takes discs, a
folder holding the game's set - an `.m3u`, a PSP game's `EBOOT.PBP`, two or
more discs of the one game (their names the same game's, "Disc 2" and
"Track 01" aside), or a sheet with its tracks beside it - or one disc image
named as the folder is ("God of War/God of War (USA).iso", by the same
cleaned name), launched by its best image as before, and a Dreamcast
`.gdi` set by its sheet. A folder with one image named otherwise is a
folder of games with one game in it (2026-11-13: "i created a folder in PSP
with one game in it, when i clicked to open it it went straight to the
game"); on
ScummVM's, a folder with a file in it (a folder of game folders is a
folder of games); on Ports', every folder is a port's own and is never
looked into. A folder of several games' discs is a folder of games, each
image a game in it. A shelf that takes no disc (the Atari 2600's and the
Genesis's `.bin` are cartridges) has only folders of games. The folders
EmulationStation keeps a system's art in (images, videos, media, boxart,
snaps, wheel, box3d and the rest, lib_es_media_dir) and MinUI's Imgs are
never folders of games, and are not counted as left out.

**A game with a loose `.m3u`** is one entry too, the `.m3u` *(metior and
Davistoa on the Discord, 2026-11-19; my OK)*: the disc images it names are
not entries, so they are off the shelf, the Grid, the list, Search Games and
Random Game, and the `.m3u` starts the game with its discs to swap. Its names
are read as Delete Game and File Transfer read them (src/sheet.c): relative
to the `.m3u`'s folder, into the folders under it (a set folder whose launch
disc it names goes whole; a folder of games keeps the games it does not
name; discs kept in a hidden folder were never entries), `#` lines and CRLF
ends skipped, matched without case. A disc no `.m3u` names stays, a name
that is not on the card or that leaves the system's folder hides nothing,
two `.m3u` may name one disc, and every `.m3u` stays. Disc images only
(src/library.c's SET_EXTS): Doom's `.m3u` names WADs, and each is a game too.
In the scan, once per read of the card (hide_m3u_discs, after the walk), so
the saved library and the check have it, and a shelf with no `.m3u` pays
nothing but a look at each entry's extension: on the Mac, 1,000 loose
three-disc sets (4,000 files) read in 36-39 ms against 3.4 ms, and 4,000
games with no `.m3u` in the same time as before. `check-titles` holds the
cases.

Everything about a system's games rather than what its shelf shows goes by
all of them, in every folder: the home's count, Favorites, Search Games,
Random Game (which opens the folder of the game it picked), Storage,
Delete Game (a game in a folder of games goes alone, the folder stays),
px2-open, the Scraper, the saved library and the place a start comes back
to, folder and all. Saves and states go by the game's file name, as they
always have.

**A folder's card** (ui_make_folder_card, src/folderart.h): the folder
itself in pixel art in the system's color, or the one color Folder Color
gives every folder (3.5), on no slab: a folder on the
shelf is a folder, not a card with a folder on it. Drawn on a grid of 128 x
112 cells and scaled four times whole (nearest), so a cell is 4 units on
the 512 x 448 card and about 2 panel pixels on the focused cover: its back
with a tab at its top left (the color at 74%), two sheets of paper standing
out of it (`#dcd9cc` and `#f5f4ee`, a line of writing on the front one),
then its front (the color itself), each in a 1-cell edge of the color at
42%, the front's top edge lit (the color 35% of the way to white) and its
foot shaded (86%), its corners a cell in as a pixel artist rounds them. On
the front's foot at the right a paper label (`#f5f4ee` in the edge color,
its shadow a cell down and right) holds the count of games in pixel digits
5 x 7 in **ink**: part of the picture, the same in both themes, as the
folder's color is. The folder's name on its front in the card's title face
from x 40, y 144, in **ink** on a light color and paper on a dark one. A
system with no color has its folders in its marks' **text-dim**, unless
Folder Color gives every folder an accent. The list's small folder takes
the same color. Under the
row the name is the title and the line under it "12 games" (shelf.games);
A's hint is **Open** (games.open), and in a folder B's is **Back**
(games.back). *(2026-11-13: "can the folder actually look like a folder in
the system? maybe a pixel art folder". I picked the folder with papers of
three looks drawn: a plain folder, this, and a manila folder dyed. When the
system art came with a gold folder of its own, a large one with the count on
its label and a small one for rows, I kept this one: it carries the folder's
name and its system's color.)*

**The list** (MENU > View > List, src/gamelist.h). A system's menu has
**View** under Sort By: **Covers**, the row as it always was, **Grid**
(11.47), **List**, or **Video** (11.57).
Each shelf keeps its own, Favorites too, in the device's database by the
system's tag (`view.<tag>`): the choice belongs with Sort By and Display
Mode, which are per shelf, because it follows what a shelf holds - a
shelf with good covers reads as a row, an Arcade or a long library as a
list. Right steps it on, Covers to Grid to List to Video and round, Left
back, and A opens the four as a list (11.3's option rows); the shelf under
the menu turns with it, on the same entry.

**START** on the shelf steps it on too, Covers to Grid to List to Video and round,
with no menu *(2026-11-13: "make clicking start in a system change view
mode to list and vice versa"; the grid between them, 2026-11-15)*: the
row's own setting, saved the same way, the shelf on the same entry, with
the move sound. Favorites, folders and Ports alike; MP4's shelf too, as it
is let go and round its three (11.67). Not with MENU or SELECT held:
MENU + START is the way out (tools/zyhome.c) and SELECT + START MP4's lock.
Before, START did nothing on a shelf. On the home it steps the Home
Screen's layout the same way (11.1). The foot names where it goes:
**START Grid** under the covers, **START List** under the grid, **START
Video** under the list, **START Covers** on the Video view (11.57); MENU >
Controls has it
on the shelf's page, as **Home Screen or View**: the two MENU rows START
steps, the home's first, as A's **Open or play** puts the home's first. The tutorial is as it was: it teaches one button a
step, its START skips it, and its last page sends you to MENU > Controls.

The heading and the foot are the cover row's (START's word apart), so
turning View moves only the middle:
- Rows down the left: the focus plate from x 40, 552 wide, rows 56 tall
  from y 200, eight in sight; names in **meta** at x 120, **text-soft**,
  the focused one on an **accent** plate in **on-accent** (radius.row) -
  with Highlight's Bar, as it ships; with Border a ring of the row's corners
  inside the plate's place, with Lift a plate of **surface** on a soft lift,
  with None no plate and the name 12 in, each in **text** (3.5) - sliding
  when too long; the others fitted. The plate and the window glide
  in **ms.move** and **out**; a jump of more than a row lands with a short
  glide, as the cover row's jumps do. More rows above or below: the menus'
  small triangle in **text-dim**.
- Each row's mark, centered at x 80: a game's, a small card 36 x 46 of
  **surface** in an **edge** frame, its band and its name's first letter in
  the system's mark color (ui_sys_mark) - the generated card, small,
  drawn rather than loaded, so a list of a thousand games costs no art
  reads and no textures, and a 512-unit cover is never squeezed to 40
  units; a folder's, the card's folder drawn small (32 x 28 cells, two
  units a cell, no label), its count at the row's end (x 568) in
  **chrome**. A favorite has the heart at the row's
  end, not on Favorites.
- The focused entry on the right: its card or box art whole in a box 320
  x 320 from x 640, y 200; its facts under it from cap y 552, 32 apart, in
  **chrome** **text-dim** (its system on Favorites, Played, Achievements,
  or a folder's "12 games"); A's hint at cap y 664.
- Up and Down step, Left and Right jump a letter, L1 and R1 a page: the
  row turned, as the cover row's d-pad turns with it. It comes round past
  its ends: a shelf, not a menu (11.3).
- The focused game's video snap plays in its cover's place on the panel,
  as on the cover row (11.19): the same wait, its bar under the cover, the
  same turn from the cover to the video in a frame that grows from one to
  the other, and the play mark on a cover that has one. *(2026-11-13: "the
  list mode should also play the video preview".)*

*(2026-11-12: I wanted my folders in a system kept on the shelf, with
their own look in the pixel style, and a list to switch to from MENU. Host
shots:
`--folder DIR`, `--game FILE`, `--view list|covers` and `--shelf-press
a,b,up...`.)*

### 11.40 EmulationStation's folders and media

A card, or a folder, copied from a system built on EmulationStation (ES) -
Knulli and Batocera, ROCKNIX, ArkOS, AmberELEC, ROGUE OS, RetroPie, Recalbox
- is read as it is *(2026-11-12, from a player: "All of my games had scraped
data already from previous installations of RogueOS and Knulli, the videos
and images folders don't get used by this OS ... It'd be great if it also
used or recognized the folders like they're named for ES (nds instead of
Nintendo DS for example)")*. Nothing on the card is moved or written but the
library's rows, so the card still works in the other system.

- **Folders.** Each system has ES's names for its folder beside ZyperOS's
  (config/systems.cfg, `es` lines: `nds` for Nintendo DS, `megadrive` and
  `md` for Genesis, `pcengine`, `tg16`, `pcenginecd` for TurboGrafx-16, ROGUE
  OS's as tools/from-rogue.sh copies them). A shelf reads ONE folder: its
  own while that has a game, else the ES folder with the most games, the
  first listed on a tie. Every new card has ZyperOS's folders, empty
  (mk/payload.sh), so a copied ES folder is read; a card set up for ZyperOS
  reads as it always did. The log names the folder a shelf reads and any it
  leaves, with its game count. The choice is made when the card is read (a
  rescan, the check after a start) and kept by the saved library, so a
  start reads nothing more. States, the library's rows and covers go with
  the folder read, as they always went with a shelf's folder; the libretro
  and ScreenScraper tables still know the system by ZyperOS's name.
- **Covers and video snaps.** ZyperOS's own come first (`.media/`). A game
  with none shows ES's: `gamelist.xml`'s `<thumbnail>` (the box, on
  Batocera), `<image>`, then the files ES's scrapers name after the game in
  `images/`, `media/box2dfront/`, `media/images/`, `boxart/`,
  `downloaded_images/`, and its logo (`<marquee>`) last; `<video>`, then
  `videos/` and `media/videos/` (src/esmedia.h has the whole order). Found on
  the cover worker, never on the frame: a folder's `gamelist.xml` is read
  once, the first time one of its games asks. A cover with an ES video has
  the play mark, and the video plays as a snap does.
- **Box Art** counts an ES cover as a cover: it fetches only for games with
  neither, and **Cover and Video** (11.34) shows an ES cover as Now,
  credited "EmulationStation". A cover chosen there is ZyperOS's own, and
  comes first from then on.
- **Names and details.** After a start's first frame, with the check of the
  saved library, each folder's `gamelist.xml` is written into the card's
  library for its games with no row there yet: `<name>` as the title (not
  when it is only the file's name, which ZyperOS tidies better),
  `<desc>`, `<releasedate>`'s year, `<genre>`, `<players>`, `<developer>`,
  `<publisher>` and `<rating>` out of 20; `<favorite>` makes it a favorite.
  The shelf takes the names at once, in place. A game with a row - a scrape,
  a rename, an earlier import - keeps it, and a scrape later writes over
  what came from ES. Until the import has run, the details page reads the
  game's entry itself.

Host shots: an ES card is a fixture of `Roms/nds` with `gamelist.xml`,
`images/` and `videos/`; `--screen games` and `--info` draw from it.

### 11.41 Rename

A game's details (X on the shelf) have **Rename** above Delete Game
*(2026-11-12: a player asked to rename a game on the device, for a name no
scrape could find)*: a plain row, live, with no value. A opens the keyboard
(11.5) with the game's name, its file's name without the extension; START
renames. The details page stays open on the game, under its new name.

- **What follows the name**, every path built from the game's key as Delete
  Game's are (11.31, src/gamern.h): its file (the extension kept), or its
  folder for a game that is one (a disc set, a ScummVM game; the disc inside
  keeps its name); what its format keeps beside it by name (a `.ccd`'s
  `.img` and `.sub`, a `.cue`'s `.sbi`); its saves (`Saves/<name>.srm`,
  `.sav`, `.dsv`, OpenBOR's); the six slots and Auto with their pictures and
  Auto's DS language; its cover, video snap and Before; its cached
  achievement set; the ES pictures and video named after it, and its
  `gamelist.xml` entry, so the other system still finds it; and its rows in
  the library: details, favorite, play sessions, Pak, where its covers came
  from. The title is emptied, so the shelf shows the new name, and a scrape
  can name it again. A file another game builds too (a `.gb` and a `.gbc`
  of one name share a save) is copied, and that game keeps its own.
- **In a folder of games** (11.39) the folder stays where it is: only the
  game's own part takes the name, its file or its disc folder, and what its
  format keeps beside it is looked for in that folder. Its saves, states and
  cover are by name, as at the top, so a name another game has in any
  folder of the shelf is taken.
- **Refused, with nothing moved**: a name no card takes (empty, a dot first
  or last, `/ \ : * ? " < > |`), another game's name on the shelf, or a
  name whose save, state or cover is already another file's. The message
  says which (11.8), and the keyboard opens again on what was typed. A
  change of case alone is a rename, though the card ignores case.
- **No Rename** for an arcade set, a BIOS set or a port: their names are
  what the core or PortMaster runs them by.

Host shots: `--info-delete` shows the row above Delete Game.

### 11.42 Display's Theme, Screen Colors and Night Mode

**Theme** (MENU > Display) is a menu panel of eight rows over the shelf:
**Accent**, **Light Background**, **Dark Background**, **Folder Color**,
**Corners**, **Highlight**, **Zyper** and **Reset to Default** (3.5). Left and Right step a
row round and apply it at once, saved; A opens a row's list (11.3), and on
Reset to Default puts them all back as they ship. Between the heading's rule and the rows, both
themes as the look has them, small, side by side, Light at the left
whatever is on: each the home (its name and count, A, the art, the dock and
its mark, as Highlight has it) with a menu over it (its heading and the tab
bar, three rows, one in focus), drawn from shapes since text is never under
37 units. The whole
screen is in the theme that is on, so a change to that theme is seen on
everything at once, and a change to the other in its picture. Before a
value, a mark of what it is: the accent's dot on Accent, the theme's chip
(its ground in its edge, the accent on it, 64 by 36) on each background's
row, and on Folder Color a folder drawn from shapes as the chips are (its
back and tab at 74% of the color, its front the color with its top edge
lit, in an edge at 42%, 38 wide): one in the color every folder takes, or
for System Colors three of the card's systems' folders stacked, the shelf's
in front. Display's own **Theme** row shows both chips and the accent's
name. Accent's, Light Background's, Dark Background's and Folder Color's
lists show the choice under the cursor on the whole screen as it moves
(11.3's what the cursor shows): the look in memory, no setting and no theme
file, the list's own focus bar in the accent too; a ground of the theme
that is not on shows that theme while its list is open, so the choice can
be seen at all. Folder Color's list (System Colors, then the ten accents by
name) has the picture column too (src/ds/preview.h), since no folder is on
the list itself: the theme's ground and three of the shelf's small pixel
folders, the middle one at three units a cell and the two beside it at two
and faded, as the shelf's are, in the color under the cursor, or System
Colors' three systems (the shelf's in the middle). B, MENU or POWER put back
the look as it was; A keeps the one chosen. *(Tarik's for 1.0.6 rc.2: "Can
we have the theme accent preview on hover? same for all other theme
options"; Folder Color for rc.3: "What sets the folders colors? can we
customize them in theme to be per system or a custom color for all?")*

**Appearance** is Light, Dark or Auto, three, so A opens their list, each
with what it does under it (Light and Dark day and night; Auto dark in Night
Mode's hours, light the rest of the day), and Left and Right step them round.
Auto is Salt & Pepper from **Night From** up to **Until**, Night Mode's own
two times below, by the handheld's clock to the minute, and Beard the rest of
the day: one schedule for both, set in one place (Night Mode's page), each
on its own setting. Auto only reads the Night Hours and never writes a
Night Mode setting; Night Mode decides the warmth on its own and is the
same under Light, Dark or Auto. At From and Until the theme turns on the
same second as the warmth, on the same pass of the launcher's loop, with
the one table load Night Mode makes and none of the theme's. It turns on
every screen and over a game (the bar's file, the in-game menu when it
opens), with no redraw while the game runs, and on the Pixel 2 the boot
screen follows it (11.21). *(1.0.6: Auto, light from 7:00 to 19:00, went,
and its hours became Night Mode's below. 1.1.0: back on those hours. A 1.0.5
player on Auto keeps Auto, the hours it had becoming the Night Hours; one
whose Auto 1.0.6 turned into Light or Dark keeps that until they pick Auto.)*
*(On 2026-11-20 a player missed Auto; I brought it back, on the Night Hours.)*

**Screen Colors** (MENU > Display, its last row; where the display has a
gamma table, `plat_screen_colors_here`: the Pixel 2's VOP, the Brick's TCON) is a page of six rows beside a color chart (the
24 patches of a camera's, a grey ramp in sixteen steps and a hue band):
**Look**, then **Temperature** (cool to warm), **Tint** (green to magenta),
**Contrast**, **Saturation** and **Gamma**, each -10 to +10 with 0 the frame
as drawn and the number signed. The Look is a ready-made set of all five:
Standard, Vivid, Intense, Warm, Cool and Muted, and Custom while a
row was moved by hand; the row in Display says it. The colors are the whole
screen's, the shelf's and every game's and port's, made by the display's
gamma table (src/panelgamma.h) over the dimming of the two bottom brightness
rungs (11.6): Temperature and Tint are gains in light, every one 1 or under
so white keeps its brightest channel (+10 warm: white 255, 230, 177; -10
cool: 230, 241, 255), Contrast an S about the middle and Gamma a power
(black, white and mid grey kept, and every table rising at every value),
and Saturation the display controller's BCSH block, 5% a step (plastron's
kernel patch 9-px2-px30-vop-bcsh-saturation; no row on a system image
without it). No Hue, which turns every color round the wheel together,
and no brightness offset, which lifts black: the backlight is the
brightness. Standard changes nothing and puts no table on the display at
all, as before: colors change only when the player asks, because the one
change ZyperOS made for everyone (1.0.4's correction) took the warmth out
of every game. A row's step is applied and kept at once, with the
one-frame blink of a table load, at most one every 120 ms while a key
repeats (Saturation never blinks). The Look's list shows the look under
its cursor on the whole screen as the cursor moves, with what it does
under the chart; A keeps it, B puts back what was set, and a look under
the cursor never reaches Diatom or portkeys. Screenshots and the pictures
of save states never show the colors, being taken before the table; nor do
the boot splash's first second and USB Drive's screen. *(Asked for on the
Discord; the first page approved by Tarik, 2026-11-16, "All approved"; the
second, with "more options and granularity", negative numbers and a
preview on hover, approved the same day.)*

**Night Mode** (MENU > Display, under Screen Colors, where the display has
a table) makes the whole screen warmer for the night, over the player's own
colors. Display has one row for it, its value the mode (**Off**, **On** or
**By Hours**); A opens its page, as Screen Colors' row does, and Left and
Right do nothing there. The page, headed Night Mode, has every setting of
it, each an option row (DESIGN.md 9): **Night Mode**, the three; **Night
Warmth**, 1 to 10, 8 as it ships: the gains of Temperature +n, multiplied
into the player's, so it adds warmth to their colors, never clips against
their Temperature and changes nothing else; and **Night From** and
**Until**, each a time of day by the half hour, written as MENU > Clock
says; 9:00 PM and 7:00 AM as it ships, and a 1.0.5 player's Auto hours
carry over. They are Appearance's Auto's too: with Appearance on Auto they
are never quiet, and a change turns the theme at once where the new hours
say. A row the mode does not use is quiet, its value still read:
Night Warmth and the hours while Off, the hours while On. So the page holds
its shape as the mode changes, and says what On or By Hours would use;
the cursor passes the quiet rows, as any quiet row. Night Warmth's list
shows the warmth under the cursor on the whole screen as the Look list
does, with no picture beside it (menu_choice's `focus`), whatever the hour;
B puts back what was set. A change is on the
display at once, and By Hours turns at the hour in games and ports too:
one frame's blink, as any table load, and no fade, whose every step would
blink. Night Mode never changes the theme (Appearance's Auto does, by the
same hours), and the Look list has no Night
look. *(Tarik's move from Screen Colors' Night look and Appearance's Auto,
approved on the sheet 2026-11-16; one row and its page for 1.0.6 rc.2, his:
"Can the night mode settings be all in night mode row".)*

### 11.43 Player 2
A friend plays an NES game's second controller in a browser, with nothing
to install: a phone on the handheld's own Wi-Fi (a USB Wi-Fi adapter makes
it, as 11.16's), or a computer on the USB cable. The handheld's own buttons
stay player 1. The Pixel 2 only (`plat_player2`: its hotspot and its cable
link are the ones Player 2 is built on), and only for a game on a core in the
system table below: the NES's FCEUmm today. *(The design was approved on 2026-11-12. This section
is what is built: plain HTTP, the touch pad and the keyboard.)*

**On the handheld.** The in-game menu has a **Player 2** row on its Game tab, after Quick Slot,
its value `off`, `waiting` or `connected`. A opens its page over the paused
game, a panel as the in-game menu's (11.3): **Player 2** in **label**
**text-soft** over a rule in **edge**, a line that says what to do,
a line for each player a friend can be ("Player 2" in **text-soft**, its
state at the right in **text-dim**), a second rule and the key legend
(11.4): A to turn it on or stop it, B to close the page. Continue goes back
to the game with Player 2 on. On the hotspot, once it is up, the page is
laid out as 11.16 lays out its code, 944 wide: "Scan with your phone's
camera; the game opens." in **meta** **text-dim** under the rule, the
Wi-Fi's QR code at the left at 9 units a module (333 units with its quiet
zone, 4.1 cm on the Pixel 2), and beside it the network and its password as
11.16's entries, with "Or open 10.42.1.1/play" under them in **meta**
**text-dim** for a phone whose sign-in sheet does not open by itself. On the
cable the line says "Open zyperos.local/play on the computer."; with
neither plugged in, "Plug in a Wi-Fi adapter or a computer." and only B. A
notice over the game (11.7), **Multiplayer** over "Player 2 joined" or
"Player 2 left", says when a browser comes and goes, and "Player 2 stopped"
when it stopped by itself. A guest is said to have left only once it has
been gone 5 s (src/p2.c's LEAVE_GRACE_MS), the row saying connected until
then: a link that drops and comes back by itself is never said to go. On
the first test with an iPhone the handheld said "left" and "joined" one
after the other over a game nobody had left, most likely such a drop as
the iPhone set up its sign-in sheet; the launcher's log says "player 2:
player N back after T ms" for each one, which shows how long they last. A
page that goes quiet is let go by p2d after its own 5 s, so its "left"
comes some 10 s after it went. src/p2panel.c draws the page; src/main.c's
`player2_page` runs it.

**On the friend's screen** (res/web/play), the File Transfer page's clothes
(11.15): the same token block, Lexend from the card. The picture is the
game's own, drawn as pixels, as large as its room takes it: whole device
pixels unless that leaves it under 85% of the room, then the room itself.
Held upright, the status and **Picture off** in a bar at the top, the
picture right under it, and the pad in the rest: the d-pad left, B and A
right, SELECT and START under them, each control 48 CSS px or more, a
thumb's, **raised** on **ground** and filled with **accent** while it is
held, with a 15 ms tick under the finger as it goes down where the browser
can: Chrome on Android, not on silent. Android's sign-in sheet is a
WebView in an app without the right to vibrate, so there the call does
nothing; Safari has no Vibration API, and its one haptic, a switch
control's, comes on a finished tap, so on the lift. Nothing zooms the page
(a double tap in iOS's sign-in sheet had zoomed it with no way back): the
viewport's limits, which the sign-in sheet keeps and Safari drops;
`touch-action: none` on every element; and play.js's noZoom, which cancels
Safari's gesture events, a double tap's second tap (never on Picture off)
and two fingers moving off the pad, and where the limits hold undoes a
zoom that came anyway by putting them again. On its side, and on a
computer, the picture takes the whole height
between the pad's halves. Picture off drops the picture and the pad grows
into the room, for a friend who watches the handheld. A computer with a
mouse shows its keys in the bar: the arrows, Z for B, X for A, Shift for
SELECT, Enter for START, by the key printed on them on any layout. The
status says "Connecting", "Player 2", "The game is full", "The host turned
multiplayer off" or "The game ended". "The host turned multiplayer off" is
the handheld stopping Player 2 while the game goes on: the page stays on it
and keeps trying every 2 seconds, so on the cable it plays again by itself
when Player 2 is turned on again (on the hotspot, once the phone is on the
new network: its password is new each time). "The game ended" is final.
Picture off switched off, or the tab shown again, the picture is back at
once: the page asks for a KEY (`'K'`, below) rather than wait for the next.
A page left for another (Back, a link) closes its socket as it goes, so its
place is free at once, and one that Back brings back from the browser's
cache connects again. A browser that cannot unpack a frame (Safari before
16.4) says it is too old, and opens no connection. The words are the
handheld's language, and the page wears the handheld's theme (below).

**The parts.**

- **Diatom** (diatom/src/player2.c), on `SETPLAYER2 on=1` from the launcher
  while the game runs, makes the share, one file in /tmp mapped by both
  sides (src/p2share.h): three frame slots, each seqlocked, which it fills
  after every frame it draws as XRGB8888, and a pad word for each guest's
  port, which it reads once a frame. A frame copy and a word read, no
  syscall and no lock: nothing on the game's thread waits for a guest. Off
  for every game until asked, and gone, the file removed, however the game
  ends. Off, nothing of it runs: a game without Player 2 polls and reads
  its pads as before it. The file's pages are taken as it is made
  (`posix_fallocate` on the devices), so a full /tmp is a refusal (PLAYER2
  on=0), not a crash at the first frame written into a sparse file. The
  first time Player 2 turns on in a game, each guest's port is given the
  core's joypad (`retro_set_controller_port_device`), as port 0 is at the
  load and as RetroArch does for every port, unless the core says it has no
  such port.
- **p2d** (src/p2d/), a program of its own beside the launcher on the card,
  so the network is on neither the game's thread nor the launcher's loop,
  at `nice(5)`: one thread, never blocking. It serves the page, its words
  (`/i18n.js`) and the theme (`/play/theme.css`) over plain HTTP, runs one
  WebSocket a browser on `/p2ws`, and on the hotspot answers DNS as the
  handheld's own network does (every name the handheld's), so a phone's
  internet check is sent to the page and its sign-in sheet opens it. It ends
  by itself when the share goes, and tells its guests "ended". On SIGTERM
  with the share still there (the handheld's A stopped Player 2, the game
  goes on) it tells them "stopped" instead. With no guest and no browser on
  the way it waits in `poll()` for one, for a DNS question, or for its next
  beat or look at the share, some 20 wakeups a second for a game nobody
  joins, not a 2 ms loop: a handheld's battery. With a guest, the 2 ms loop.
- **The page** (res/web/play): plain JavaScript, no framework and no build
  step, as File Transfer's page.
- **The launcher** (src/p2.c, no SDL, every process and protocol action
  through `p2_ops` so tools/p2-check.c drives it on fakes): the row and the
  page, the start in order (any PLAYER2 answer still waiting read away
  first, then SETPLAYER2, the theme file, then the hotspot without its own
  DNS and p2d once it is up; or the cable's link, zyperos.local and p2d),
  the notices, and the stop in the reverse order at every end of the game.
  Diatom answers a stop's or a failure's `on=0` too, and nothing reads its
  lines while the game is paused under the page, so that answer waits; a
  start that took it for its own answer failed once, with Diatom on. At the
  game's end the stop removes the share's file before p2d's SIGTERM, so a
  Diatom killed before it could still has its guests told "ended".

**The wire.** One WebSocket, little-endian:

- *Frames, to the page*, binary (src/p2d/codec.h): `'F'`, flags (bit 0
  KEY, bit 1 PAL), width and height (u16), the frame's number (u32), the
  palette when it changed (a u16 count up to 256, then R, G, B each), then
  the frame's palette indices, raw deflate (zlib level 1), XORed with the
  frame before unless KEY. The browser inflates with
  `DecompressionStream('deflate-raw')`, so the page ships no decoder. A
  KEY comes with a new color, a new size, a new browser, a `'K'`, and every
  120 frames.
- *Status, to the page*, text: `S` and a line of JSON,
  `{"state":"playing","player":2,"buttons":509}`, `{"state":"taken"}`,
  `{"state":"stopped"}` (Player 2 turned off, the game going on: the page
  keeps trying) or `{"state":"ended"}` (the game's end: the page stops).
- *From the page*, binary only: `'A'` and a frame's number once that frame
  is drawn (or skipped, with Picture off or the tab hidden), `'P'` and the
  RetroPad bits held, on every change and every 100 ms while any is held,
  and `'K'` alone, one byte: a KEY next, from a page that drew nothing for
  a while and has no frame to add a delta to, sent as it draws again (and
  at the first delta it cannot use). p2d resets that guest's encoder and
  sends the newest frame again, as KEY with PAL, at once, even while the
  game's frames stand still. Anything else closes the socket. p2d sends a
  frame only while fewer than two wait for their ack, so a slow link gets
  fewer frames, never a queue.

**No stuck buttons, and no strangers.**

- Diatom reads a guest's pad only while p2d's beat (`pad_beat`, bumped
  every 50 ms) moved in the last 500 ms, so a p2d that dies or hangs lets go
  of every button.
- p2d lets a pad go after a second with no `'P'` while buttons are held (a
  phone that locked mid-press), and when its socket closes. A guest with a
  frame waiting for its ack that has sent nothing for 5 seconds is gone (a
  phone out of range sends no FIN), so its place is free. p2d's log says
  when each player came and went and why ("player 2 left at 10953 ms: 5 s
  silent with a frame not acked"). A page that lost its link while it
  played tries again after a quarter of a second, not 2 s, and sends the
  buttons still held the moment it is back: a blip, or a phone so busy it
  was let go, costs as few presses as can be. GitHub's macOS runner starved
  Chrome long enough for p2d to let the page go mid-test, which is how
  this was found.
- The page lets go of everything when it loses the focus or is hidden, and
  of its held keys when a Mac's Cmd goes down (macOS sends no key-up under
  it).
- A guest has the system's buttons and nothing else: Diatom and p2d each
  mask the pad with them, never Diatom's hotkeys or MENU.
- Only the page's own origin may open the WebSocket (`http://` the
  handheld's address or `zyperos.local`, on p2d's port): on the cable the
  computer has the internet, and a site in another tab could otherwise take
  player 2 or watch the game.
- The page is never drawn inside another site's page: every reply says
  `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'` and
  `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, so a site in another tab cannot hold it in a
  hidden frame and take player 2's place.
- p2d listens on the link's address alone (`--addr`, httpd's
  `httpd_start_on`), never on every address: on the cable the handheld's
  Wi-Fi may still be on a home or public network, and a client there,
  browser or not, cannot reach it at the address the handheld has on that
  network. Its DNS binds the same address.

**Made to grow.** The NES is the first system, not the only shape:

- *The system table* (src/p2.c's `SYSTEMS`): a core, the RetroPad buttons a
  guest gets (within `P2S_PAD_CEILING`, the twelve from B to R), and how
  many play at once, the handheld's player included. The NES is `fceumm`,
  `0x01FD` (B, SELECT, START, the d-pad, A) and 2. SETPLAYER2 carries both
  to Diatom, which writes them into the share for p2d, which tells the page.
- *Up to four players:* the share has a pad word for each of ports 1 to 3
  and a bit for each in `guests`; p2d gives a newcomer the lowest free port
  and says "taken" only when every one is in use.
- *The page's pad* is built from a table (play.js's `PAD`): each button's
  bit, its place (the d-pad, the round face buttons, the pills), its label
  and its keys (Y, X, L and R on A, S, Q and W). A game draws, reads and
  sends only the buttons in its mask. A system with other buttons is a row
  there, and a place in play.css if it needs one.
- *The codec's 256 colors:* a frame of more colors than that is not sent
  (the encoder resets and the next frame that fits goes as a KEY), and a
  palette that fills with old colors starts fresh. An NES frame fits in
  it. A 16-bit system such as the SNES can put more than 256 colors in a
  frame (its color math does), and needs a direct-color mode: a new flag
  bit and the pixels as they are, behind the same `p2c_*` functions. Not
  built yet.

**The theme file.** The page wears the handheld's look, not the phone's:
the launcher writes the theme it shows now (light or dark by the handheld,
the accent, the background tones and the corners, 3.5) as one `:root` rule
of play.css's custom properties into /tmp/zyperos-webtheme.css
(src/webtheme.c), at the start and again on a change, and p2d serves it as
`/play/theme.css`, read afresh at each request. With no file, the page
keeps play.css's own tokens, light or dark by the browser.

**Later** (the design's Plans 2 and 3): HTTPS, with a certificate of each
Pixel's own under `play.zyperos.com`, asked for once and only when the owner
wants it; the captive portal API (RFC 8908 and 8910), so a phone opens the
page by itself on joining; and a game controller paired with the phone,
through the browser's Gamepad API, which needs HTTPS.

### 11.44 Menu music of your own
The owner's own music behind the shelf, from a folder of its own, and a
choice of whether ZyperOS's ten tracks play with it *(2026-11-13: "a
specific BGM folder for those custom songs in flash drive mode/sd card/web
portal", and "being able to disable the standard tracks")*.

**The folder.** `Menu Music`, at the top of the card beside `Music`,
`Videos` and `Audiobooks`, named as MENU calls it (my pick, 2026-11-13), and
filled the way they are: on the card in a computer, in Flash Drive, or on
File Transfer's page, where it is in the sidebar between Videos and BIOS,
in the red cassette (#116). A new card has it, and the
launcher makes it on a card that does not. Its tracks play from it and from
its folders, two deep (`Menu Music/Album/Disc 1/01 Song.mp3`):
MP3 and Ogg, as before. It is the only folder that plays: ROGUE OS's
`Roms/BGM`, which played until 1.0.5, is an ordinary folder now, and
nothing in it is moved (my pick, 2026-11-13: "if we are getting rid of
roms/bgm, then only menu music"); tools/from-rogue.sh copies ROGUE's BGM
into Menu Music. Storage counts it with the media. A track copied on is heard once File Transfer closes, or
after Flash Drive, with no restart.

**ZyperOS Tracks.** A row of MENU > Menu Music, between Volume and Track:
**On**, ZyperOS's ten play in the shuffle with the owner's; **Off**, the
owner's alone. On as it ships (my pick, 2026-11-13). It is there only when
the card has music of its own: with none, ZyperOS's play whatever it says,
and the page is as it was. Track steps through what plays, the owner's
first, then ZyperOS's. The note under the rows counts them ("330 of yours,
10 ZyperOS", "330 tracks of yours"), or with none of the owner's says where
they go ("Add MP3 or OGG to Menu Music"); the panel's rows leave it in the
window once the list goes round.

### 11.46 The Achievements tab

The in-game menu's fourth tab (1.0.5), one L1 from Game, after Controls. It
shows the running game's set where it is wanted, without a screen of its own
over the menu (src/achtab.h, src/achtab_screen.h).

- **The counts**, in **meta** **text-soft**, centered under the tab row and
  pinned there while the list scrolls: "3 / 36 · 25 / 415 points"; "3 / 36 ·
  2 waiting to send" while unlocks earned offline wait; "Mastered · 415
  points" in **accent-ink** when every one is earned and sent.
- **The list**, a row an achievement, the list over the shelf's rows: its
  badge (or the plain trophy until the card has it) at the left, its title,
  its points at the right, or its count while one moves ("74 / 100"). Earned
  rows lift their label and take the earned color (**accent-ink**) on their
  value. Every row is a stop, earned or not (`visits_all`), and the list goes
  round (11.3). L1 and R1 turn the tabs, so it has no paging of its own.
- **A** on a row opens the achievement's card, as in the list over the
  shelf. X quits from here too.
- **No list**: a sentence where the list would be, **menu** **text-soft**,
  filled across the list's width onto up to six lines, A doing nothing on it:
  each line as far as it goes, to the panel's right margin, as the panel's
  other text is (Tarik, testing 1.0.6: "The empty achievements text in the
  menu is not using the full menu's width"; balanced, it stood in a narrow
  column at the left of a panel as wide as every tab). In the order that
  decides it: a set on the card is always the list; no hash for the system,
  or for the kind of file; nobody signed in; the set on its way (a first play
  with Wi-Fi); never played with Wi-Fi ("not checked yet"'s sentence); and
  RetroAchievements has none for this copy. The tab never hides.
- **The Game tab's Achievements row** stays, with its count or its state at a
  glance, and A on it turns to this tab in every state.

### 11.45 Button remapping

What each button does in a game, changed from the in-game menu's Controls
tab (11.28), for one game or every game of a system, kept on the card
*(2026-11-13: from the ZyperOS Discord, "change what each button does for one
system, or for one game, right from the in-game menu, and keep it on the
card", and the d-pad as the left stick Nindzusek asked for)*. The rules are pure
(src/remap.c, src/remapstore.c, `make check-remap`), Diatom's SETMAP and
SETSTICK are the way they reach a game, at once while it is paused.

**Steps.** Left and Right step a button's value through the row's own
table, in this order: the buttons the system's core names (in src/controls.c's
order for that system: faces, shoulders, Start, Select), then Turbo of each
face button, then Nothing, and last, on the four systems with D-pad as Stick,
**Stick On/Off** and **Stick While Held** (below; `ct_choices`). A
Dreamcast's list is A, B, X, Y, L trigger, R trigger, Start, Turbo A to Turbo
Y, Nothing, Stick On/Off, Stick While Held, and no more. SELECT is offered no
stick: it holds the hotkeys, and a press of it is never a turn of the stick
and a hotkey at once. A opens the same
choices as a list of their own, headed "Y: PlayStation", the handheld still
beside it, a tick on what the button does now and "default" in **text-dim**
on the scope's default; A on a choice takes it and goes back (Y too, on a
choice another button gives: below), B goes back with no change. Each Left or Right works from the table as it was when the cursor
came onto the row, so stepping through the list leaves the other buttons as
they were except for the one swap at the value it stops on.

**Swap, not copy**, unless the player asks for both. When button P is given
game button G, and another button Q already gives G, Q takes what P gave
before: Y given Cross makes B give Square, and both rows show it. Turbo and Nothing never swap (Y can be Turbo
Cross while B stays Cross), and a swap never lands on a quiet row: it keeps
what it gives. The stick's two swap as a game button does, so one button
turns the stick unless the player gives the other one to a second button.

**Two buttons on one game button** *(issue #305, moroboshi69 on the Brick:
"both A and Y on the NES's B"; my picks in the Controls lab, 2026-11-19)*.
Diatom always ORs every handheld button that targets a game button; only
the swap stopped it. Left and Right on a row still swap, so a quick look
never leaves two buttons on one game button by surprise. **In a button's
list, A swaps and Y shares**: on a choice another button already gives
(`ct_can_share`: plainly, and not the row's own value or Nothing), **A**
takes it with the swap and **Y** takes it as well, so both give it
(`rm_share`). The list's foot, under a rule and kept still (pin_foot), says
what the choice under the cursor does in two lines - "A: B takes Square." and
