"Y: Y and B give Cross." - and its keys as hints, "A Swap" and "Y Both";
on any other choice one line, "Y gives Cross.", and "A Take". The list is
as tall as the page it opens over, so the panel holds still: the foot comes
out of the page's rows (three choices show, and scroll) and stands in the
bottom pad down to a note's own air (`pad / 3`). **A shared row
carries its partner's glyph after the value** (`MENU_PARTNER_MARK`, 32
units, at 70%: B, then a small Y), and the handheld lights both buttons while
the cursor is on either row. **Removing one** is giving that row anything
else (Left, Right, its list) or Nothing: the partner keeps the game button,
so no warning row. A swap from a shared row hands its old value to the other
button, which then shares it: still one rule. Reset Buttons removes every
share. A share is stored as the pair it is (`y:b`), as every change. With the
hotkeys (11.61) a chord does one action and an action has one chord, so a
clash there offers Swap or Cancel, never Both; a handheld button can give a
game button and be part of a chord, and SELECT's rule decides, as before. SELECT keeps the hotkeys' rule whatever it gives: Diatom's
chord gives a lone SELECT to the game as it is let go, or once held 400 ms,
and never under a shoulder (pressed after it, or up to 100 ms before it).

**The warning row.** A game button that no button gives now (Nothing on the
only button that gave it, and no turbo of it either) gets a quiet row,
"Cross has no button now", right under the row of the button whose change
caused it. A turbo of it counts as a button. The stick's two are no game button: none is lost when a button stops
giving one, and one given in place of a game button can leave that game
button with none. The row is not selectable, so
the cursor steps over it.

**Scope and storage.** Two tables can exist for a system, the system's
(`map.<TAG>`) and a game's (`map.<TAG>.<file>`, the file as the shelf names it,
as `pak.` does). A game uses its own if it has one, else the system's, else
ZyperOS's default (systems.cfg's `map|` line joined with `turbo.<TAG>`).
**This Game** shows the map in force for the game and its first change copies
it into the game's own table, so later changes to the system's do not reach
it; its default, for "changed" and "default", is the system's. **The
system's name** edits `map.<TAG>`, which reaches every game with no table of
its own at its next start (and this one at once), its default ZyperOS's. A
table is stored as the pairs in which it differs from its default, in SETMAP's
form (`b:y,y:b,r1:b~3`) after a version, `1;`, so a later release's default
still reaches the buttons a player never set. **No key equals its default**:
when a change leaves a table equal to its default (Left and then Right again),
its key is deleted, so undoing a change undoes "own buttons" too. "Own
buttons" is the map alone: `stick.<TAG>.<file>` ("1" or "0"; absent is the
system's default) is per game on every game and does not count, and is
deleted when it equals the default. **Reset Buttons** deletes the shown
scope's key (and in This Game puts D-pad as Stick back to the system's). A
game with buttons of its own shows Applies To in the **accent-ink**, and on
the shelf its details page gets a **Buttons | This Game** row after Favorite,
only for such a game; A on it opens the same list as a page of its own (the
game's name as heading, no tab row, the handheld beside it), Applies To fixed on
This Game, changes kept for the next start. Rename moves both keys with the
game and Delete Game deletes them (src/gamern.c). A table Diatom refuses is
logged by both sides and the game plays with ZyperOS's default.

**D-pad as Stick**, one switch per game on the systems whose pad has a left
stick the core reads: the Nintendo 64 (on to start with; SELECT, then the
d-pad, is the N64's own D-pad), the Dreamcast (off; the same SELECT rule, and
the SELECT row says "Held: D-pad"), the PlayStation (off; the plain stick, no
SELECT rule, and the d-pad stays the d-pad too, so a game that keeps its pad
digital still moves) and the PSP (off; its R2 turns it in the game, and the
row agrees). It takes effect at once through SETSTICK. The Saturn has none: its
stick is a controller type of its own.

**Stick On/Off and Stick While Held**, a button's choices on those four
systems *(2026-11-18: from the Discord, metior's "a button that turns D-pad as
Stick on and off, or does it while held", Nindzusek's "as a choice"; my
review: all good)*. **Stick On/Off**: each press turns the d-pad into the
stick and the next back, and the side it was left on is kept for the game,
as the D-pad as Stick row is, which says it; a pop-up names the side ("Stick
on"), as R2's always did on the PSP. **Stick While Held**: while the button
is held the d-pad is on its other side (the stick, or with D-pad as Stick on
the d-pad), and back when it is let go, with no pop-up and nothing kept. The
PSP's R2 is Stick On/Off by default (`map|PSP|r2:stick`), so nothing changes
for a player who does not touch it; it can go to another button, or be
Nothing. Neither reaches the game: in the map they are `stick` and
`stickhold`, and Diatom hides the button from the core and turns the d-pad
itself (diatom/src/env.c), under MENU a button being MENU's and under SELECT a
hotkey's. A stick that is off with such a button is sent on the d-pad's side
(RUN and SETSTICK `dpad-pad`, `plain-pad`), so the stick's own rules (the
SELECT rule, the PlayStation's both) hold on its stick side; with none it is
sent as before. Nothing is done in a frame but read a bit the pad already
gives.

**Face Buttons**, where the caps' letters and the system's buttons can
disagree (Dreamcast, Saturn, Neo Geo Pocket and Pocket Color): **By Letter**,
each cap gives the button it names, ZyperOS's choice, or **By Place**, each
cap gives the button in its place on the original pad. It sets all four faces
as one swap and reads **Mixed** when they match neither.

**Changed values** are drawn in the **accent-ink** (VCOL_EARNED, as an earned
achievement's value is) and **on-accent** on the focus fill, as every value.

**The handheld**, on the Controls tab and on a button's choices only, in the
tutorial's front pose (10 degrees), lights the keys of the row under the
cursor in the tutorial's lit look (accent fill, ring, halo): a button row its
button; D-pad as Stick the d-pad (and SELECT on the N64 and the Dreamcast);
Face Buttons the four faces; Reset Buttons every changed button; a swap
both buttons while the cursor stays on the row, and a shared game button
both buttons that give it; Applies To, Pak, Rumble and Hotkeys nothing. A
lit shoulder stands up out of the top edge. It **leans**
toward the side of the keys lit: the faces (A, B, X, Y) turn it 12 degrees
from the front pose to show its right side (22 degrees), the d-pad 22 the
other way (-12). A shoulder row turns it 12 toward its side (R1 and R2 to 22
degrees, L1 and L2 to -2) and **tilts it back 25 degrees**, as seen from a
little above, so the top shows with the four shoulders standing on it and
the lit one standing up out of it in the lit look; its upright edges stay
upright. SELECT, START, keys on both sides and no keys bring it back to the
front pose, level; a side key with a centre key leans to its side (the N64's
d-pad and SELECT as the d-pad), and a face with a right shoulder as the face,
level. Which side a key is on is where the device's model has it
(`ct_lean`): on the front, left or right of the middle, SELECT and START
within 8 mm of it being the centre (11 mm on the Brick, where MENU, F1 and
F2 are the middle too); on the top edge, a shoulder left or right; a key on
a side face is a centre key here. The Brick's shoulders are on its back
ledge, which no front pose shows: a row lighting any of them, alone or
with another shoulder, turns it round to its back view (160 degrees, tilted
back 20), the same for all four, so a step from one shoulder's row to the
next only moves the light. With a front key, a centre key too, it leans as
that key does and then turns round to the back view and back again, as the
tutorial's lessons do (`ct_spin`, **hold-spin** and **spin**), so both keys
show; a step to another such row with the same lean keeps the turn going. The pose's size and turn are the model's (the Pixel 2 at
3.15 units a millimetre, the Brick as tall at 2.3, both turned 10), and so
is the kept texture's box.

| What | Token and how |
|---|---|
| R1 or L1 into Controls | the handheld comes with the rows, 64 units the press's way and faded up in **move**, and turns from 40 to 10 degrees, plus the lean of its first row, in **turn**; a shoulder row's tilt is there from the start |
| Out of Controls | it leaves with the old tab's name, 96 units the press's way, gone in the first two fifths of **move** |
| Up or Down | the fill glides in **quick**; the old key's light goes out and the new key's comes in, in **quick**; the lean (the turn and the tilt together) eases to the new row's pose in **turn**, from where it is |
| Left or Right on a button | the key presses in **press** (in, then out); a swapped partner lights in **quick** |
| A shoulder row | the shoulder stands up in **quick** |
| A to a button's choices, and back | the handheld holds still while the list beside it changes |
| A on Hotkeys, and back | the handheld goes and comes with the panel |

Nothing overshoots, every motion is interruptible and tells the redraw clock
when it ends (8.2). The handheld is drawn into its kept texture when its view
changes and copied otherwise; it is drawn only in the menu, never in a frame
of the game.

**What the Hotkeys row opens** is the Hotkeys list of 11.61, and through its
All Controls row the read-only pages of 11.28: what a system adds, Hotkeys
and In a game, at their own width and with no handheld. Hotkeys, SELECT with
one more button, stay on the physical buttons whatever the map says; MENU,
the volume keys and POWER cannot be remapped.

### 11.47 The grid

**The grid** (MENU > View > Grid, src/gamegrid.h; the rule src/gridrule.h).
A shelf's covers in two rows of cells of one shape, more of them at once and
each at least as large as the cover row's near side covers: Covers shows
five, the four beside the focused one turned and smaller, the grid six to
twelve. *(2026-11-15: Bruno Jenso, Discord, on 1.0.5:
"all art ... feels a wee bit too small"; I asked for a layout more dense that
looks as good as the others, and for every shape of art and a mix of them to
look meant. Picked from three mocked layouts, the shape rule, the fill, the
cut and START's order as recommended.)*

**The shape comes from the art.** Every cover's shape (its width over its
opaque height) is read when its picture arrives. A folder, a game with no
art and a cut-out (below) say nothing. Three groups: **portrait** under
0.83 (2:3 grids, the boxes of the NES, the Genesis and American cases, tall
PSP cases), **square** 0.83 to 1.2 (square thumbnails, jewel cases, Game
Boy and DS boxes), **landscape** over it (SNES and N64 boxes, screenshots,
title screens, 16:9 heroes). The group that holds at least half of a
shelf's art gives its shape, as its middle cover's (the median); kept between
1:2 and 2:1. **An even mix** - no group with half - takes square cells: a
portrait or a landscape cover fills about 70% of a square cell, where a
landscape cover in a portrait cell fills half. A shelf of cut-outs alone (an
EmulationStation wheel set) takes landscape cards of 1.4; one with no art at
all the generated card's portrait. The shape is measured from the covers in
hand, nearest the cursor first, until every cover in sight is in or 1.5 s
have passed, and kept in the device's database by the shelf
(`grid.shape.<tag>`, and `/<folder>` for a folder of it), so the next time
the shelf opens in its shape at once and nothing moves as covers arrive.

**The cells.** Two rows. The cells fill the width between the margins (64),
DS_GRID_GAP (24) apart, no taller than two rows allow in the room from the
top bar's band and a section (96) to where the band under it must start; the
columns are the fewest that show at least six covers with cells within 12%
of the shelf's shape, and the grid stands in the middle of its room. On the
1024 x 768 canvas:

| Art on the shelf | Cell | Across | Each cover | On the Pixel 2 | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2:3 grids, portrait boxes | 0.71 | 5 | 160 x 224 | 100 x 140 px | 10 |
| Tall cases (PSP) | 0.58 | 6 | 129 x 222 | 81 x 139 px | 12 |
| Square, an even mix | 1.0 | 4 | 206 x 206 | 129 x 129 px | 8 |
| DS boxes | 1.11 | 4 | 206 x 185 | 129 x 116 px | 8 |
| Landscape boxes, cut-outs alone | 1.4 | 3 | 282 x 201 | 176 x 126 px | 6 |
| 4:3 screenshots | 1.33 | 3 | 282 x 212 | 176 x 132 px | 6 |
| 16:9 heroes | 1.78 | 3 | 282 x 159 | 176 x 99 px | 6 |

A wider canvas (a screen of another shape in the 768-high design space) has
more columns by the same rule. `check-gridrule` holds the shapes and this
table.

**Each cover in its cell**, corners DS_RADIUS_CELL (14):
- A cover within 12% of its cell's shape **fills** it; the sliver over, less
  than 6% of a side, is cut from its middle.
- A cover of another shape sits **whole** in the middle, over **its own
  colors, blurred** (sampled to 48 across and blurred three passes on the
  frame as the picture arrives, src/gamegrid.c's gg_note), toned toward
  **ground** (35% in Salt & Pepper, 20% in Beard) so the art stands in front;
  the cover's own corners at half the cell's. A plain **surface** card behind
  it until its colors are in hand.
- A **cut-out** - a quarter of its edge clear, or a sixth of all of it: a
  logo, a box in 3D on nothing - sits whole on a plain **surface** card,
  12% of the cell's width round it.
- A game with **no art** gets its card (11.1) at the cell's shape: the
  card's ground, kept a quarter of its size with no name on it, and the name
  set at the cell's own size by the card's rules - hard left at 44/512 of the
  cell, its first line at 38%, on the dark band, the rule under it - in
  **chrome**, one size in every cell: a cell is 160 wide, and in **support**
  or **action** a word as plain as "Castlevania" is wider than that. So the
  names of a Grid sit on one reading line.
- A **folder** is its pixel folder (11.39), whole, on the ground.
- A cover still coming is its cell in **raised**.

**The focus**: Highlight's (3.5; my picks in the focus lab, 2026-11-19).
The focused cover grows - 4% with Bar, 3% with Border, 8% with Lift, 6% with
None - to where its edges and its marks land on whole panel pixels, on a
soft lift (Lift's deeper, one light crossing it as it lands); Bar's bar 8
under it, Border's ring round it, focus-ring past a gap of focus-gap, its
corners the cell's plus the gap plus the ring; round a folder's picture for a
folder. Every cover has its hairline, and the covers out of focus step back,
a fifth of the way to the ground. *(Until 1.0.6 a ring of 4 and a gap of 4,
2.5 pixels on the Pixel 2, and no cover dimmed.)* A cover's grow, its lift
and the step back move with its weight in the focus's glide, and the marks
glide from cell to cell, all in **ms.quick**, the window of rows in
**ms.move**, both **out**; a jump of more than a row lands with a short
glide, as the row's jumps do. A game's card with no art is made at its
cell's size and drawn at the grown one, so its name is not laid out again
as it grows. The rail at the right, 23 from the edge (11.2's, standing), when
the shelf has more rows than two.

**The band** under the grid, DS_GRID_BAND_GAP (24) under its room: the
focused entry's name in **game-title**, in a 40-unit line box, a favorite's
heart before it (not on Favorites), sliding when it is too long, and A's hint
at its right; 8 under it, the facts in **support** in a 36-unit line box, 32
apart: the heading (the system, and the folders down to the one on screen,
"Game Boy Advance / Rom Hacks"), the game's own system on Favorites, a
folder's count, PortMaster's not installed, Played, the achievements; a part
that would pass the right margin is left out with the ones after it, and a
heading wider than the line slides over the whole of it alone. The grid has
no heading line of its own: the facts carry it, so the covers have the room.
The keys on the shelf's foot (720): B, START List, Y, X, spread as the
cover row's are (11.4).

**The video snap** (11.19): after the same wait, shown as the bar along the
focused cover's foot inside its cell, the cell opens to two cells across (no
wider and no taller than the grid) over its neighbors, at the video's own
shape, growing from the cell as the cover row's turns wide, the ring and a
card's lift (UI_LIFT_FOCUS) going with it. The play mark on the focused
cover alone.

**The keys** (src/shelf_keys.c): Left and Right step along the rows, round
the ends as the row does; Up and Down a row, stopping at the top and the
foot with the edge sound, Down from over a short last row landing on its
last cover; L1 and R1 a screenful in the same column, or to the last (first)
cover where less is left, and from the last round to the first, so a grid
that fits on one screen gets its two ends (`gg_page`). The four directions are
the grid's, so it has no letter jump. A, B, X, Y, START as on the cover row.
The covers a press can slide into sight are decoded before it comes: the two
rows in sight and two rows either side of them stay, counted round the ends
(Left from the first cover, Right from the last and L1/R1 at the ends come
round), and one more round an end the window is at, which the glide into the
other end's page shows a sliver of (`gg_row_kept`). The rows ahead are asked
for once every cover in sight is in, the nearest first, so a row, a page or
the other end slides in whole, never cover by cover *(2026-11-15: "when we go
down a page, the arts flicker while the animation is scrolling down")*. A row
a glide still shows stays until it ends. Held, the steps can outrun the
decoding; then the covers in sight come first. Before a game starts the grid
lets go of all but the rows in sight, so the game has that memory; the rows
ahead are asked for again when the grid is back. The row keeps eight.

## 12. Copy

- Headings and rows in Title Case; status values begun with a capital, as
  every value in a row's value column is; lines and messages in sentence
  case, ending with a full stop.
- One name for one thing: "Needs Wi-Fi" in a value column, "Connect to Wi-Fi
  first." as a message. *(The audit found three names for no network.)*
- Pairs of buttons with a slash and no spaces ("L1/R1", "Left/Right"), chords
  with a plus ("MENU+VOL").
- Counts as "3 / 12"; lists of facts separated by " · ".
- No exclamation marks, except in Zyper's bubble.
- Errors say what happened and what to do, without apology.
- In every language (11.30, `res/strings/GLOSSARY.md`): the same term for
  the same thing; button names as printed (A, START, MENU), the d-pad's
  directions translated; product names never. Title Case is English's:
  Spanish, Portuguese and French write headings and rows in sentence case,
  and every value begins with a capital as English's does; Chinese uses full-width
  punctuation with a space between Chinese and Latin letters or digits;
  French a no-break space before `! ? : ;`. Spanish says *tú*, Portuguese
  *você*, French *vous*.
- No text is cut to fit. A cut word loses its meaning *(
  2026-11-07)*: a narrow place gets a short form of its own that still says
  the thing, in every language (*Transferir USB* on the empty home's card
  and in MENU). A UI string that slides in a marquee is cut too, only
  slowly: a marquee is for names, which are data.
- A menu row is sized with a row's height of air between its label and its
  value, and may close it to half before anything is cut: the Pixel 2's
  fonts measure a little wider than the host's ("auto (alto-falante)", 3
  units to spare on the host, was cut on the device, 2026-11-10). And the
  host's check says "tight" for a row within 1% of the most it can have, so
  it is caught before a device shows it.

---

## 13. A new screen, checked

- [ ] Every color, size, radius, duration and curve is a token from
      `src/design.h`, and every place a layout token from `src/ds/layout.h`.
- [ ] It is built from the parts of section 15 by name - a page from the
      page frame, a question from the tiles, a mark from the marks - and a
      number of its own says why beside it. A part it needs that section 15
      lacks goes into `src/ds/` first.
- [ ] A style or a mark is the caller's word, never read off a label or the
      first row's mark.
- [ ] `make pixel-gate` lists only the frames it means to change.
- [ ] No word comes within 3 units of a key or a more mark (`check-i18n-shots`
      says where), in any language: a foot moved closer to a caption met it.
- [ ] It works in both themes.
- [ ] Every press on it does something visible and audible in the next frame.
- [ ] Its motion starts from where things are, can be interrupted, and tells
      the redraw clock when it ends.
- [ ] No text under **chrome**, except the letter inside a button glyph (11.9),
      which is part of the mark and is sized from the glyph; nothing
      truncated.
- [ ] A host shot of it in each theme (`--shot ... --theme light|dark`).
- [ ] Every word from `res/strings`, in all five languages, and its shot in
      `check-i18n-shots` (`--lang CODE`), the pseudo-locale's (`--lang xx`)
      looked at.
- [ ] If it adds a pattern, the pattern is added here.

## 14. Not yet consistent

What this document describes and the code does not do yet. Each line goes
when its change lands.

- A page's two-line heading is laid out three ways (`DS_HEAD_BALANCED`,
  `DS_HEAD_TWO`, `DS_HEAD_SUB`).
- The keys on every screen stand on one foot since 1.0.6, `DS_FOOT_Y`
  (720: the last row's middle, 32 from the screen's foot to the glyph's),
  where a page had 704, Cover and Video and the MP4 player 724 and the
  shelves 720. Left apart: the charging screen's larger hints at 680, and
  Storage, which has no keys and its caption's last line at 708 (the
  caption inset, on the grid; 702 before).
- The tutorial draws its bars and more marks in its own layer, at `ds_bar`'s
  and `ds_more`'s sizes. Every other bar goes through `ds_bar` or
  `ds_bar_in` (File Transfer's load and Developer Mode's hold since 1.0.6,
  the hold in **accent-ink** as every hold is).
- Player 2's page draws its slab through `ds_panel_slab` inside
  `p2panel_draw`; its moving path lifts and opens the pass itself
  (`p2_panel`, src/player2_screen.c) rather than through `ds_panel_open`,
  which would draw the slab a second time.
- Words whose ink comes within 3 units of a key or a more mark, found when
  the host began to check it (1.0.6), none left: Storage's two-line caption
  under its sort key left with the layout by rules (its list keeps whole
  rows in the room the page's tallest caption leaves, `list_room`,
  src/storage_page.c), and the video controls' keys with the one-view
  player, which keeps them in a band that grows. `check-i18n-shots` fails on
  any screen whose words meet a key or a mark (`KNOWN_KEYS` is empty).
- A heart is drawn three ways: filled (`draw_heart`, `src/heart.c`, the
  favorites' mark), as a stroke mark (`UI_GLYPH_HEART`, the Favorites tile),
  and as a distance field for a cover badge that only its check draws
  (`AB_FAVORITE`, `src/artbadge.c`).

- Menus opening and closing; the snappy durations. Both are new with this
  document.
- The bar of 11.6 draws from the tokens in the launcher and in portkeys
  (tarikbc/ZyperOS#7) and in Diatom on the Pixel 2 (the Diatom bar PR in this
  repo: Diatom now lives here, under `diatom/`, and the PR that was
  tarikbc/diatom#1 moved with it), in the launcher's theme (11.6 says how
  each reads it). Diatom on the Brick (its `diatom/port/brick.c`) still draws
  the old bar, a 6-pixel line across the top in the cyan and warm, and does
  not read the file.
- Over a game the fallback Diatom runs, the bar keeps the theme it started
  in when Auto passes 7:00 or 19:00: the launcher waits for the fallback
  without asking the clock, so `/tmp/zyperos-theme` is not written again
  until it is back.
- The loading loop's frames (tools/genloading.py, tarikbc/ZyperOS#6) are
  drawn for a dark ground; Beard needs them with Zyper's shadow (section 10).
- The keyboard's press pop of 11.5 is not built; it comes with motion. Its
  sounds are (section 9).
- The shelf's menus draw about 849 wide in Lexend, not 5's 800: "Audio
  Output" and "auto (speaker)" are 741 of content with the row_h gap between
  them, and the panel widens rather than let a row scroll
  (`menu_std_width`).
- The port loading screen's band (11.11) moves toward **text** where it is
  under 3:1 on the backdrop, a twentieth at a time (darker in Beard, lighter
  in Salt & Pepper): Ports' pink moves a twentieth over a dark cover in Beard,
  and Nintendo DS's silver four or five. So the band can differ slightly from
  the system's rail and wash, which do not move.
- Zyper's loop on the loading screen (11.11): the words and the cover stay
  out of his frame, but the still under it is the blurred backdrop, and
  plastron's splash has to copy the still back under his frame before it draws
  each frame of the loop, not fill it with the ground. That is pending in
  tarikbc/ZyperOS#11 and plastron; until it lands his frame may show as a box
  of flat ground on the backdrop.
- Over a paused game whose frame is white at the top right, two of the
  status corner's marks (11.12) are under 3:1 on scrim.game: **warning**
  (2.45:1, as the low-battery dot there was) and the not-connected fan's
  **text-dim** (2.7:1). A frame that bright in that corner is rare; holding
  them would take a shape behind the corner, which the lab did not pick.
- Five one-line message holds (900 to 2200 ms), where 11.8 has two.

### 11.23 File Transfer while files move
File Transfer is a session of its own, and its screen (the transfer panel,
11.3, or the hotspot screen, 11.16) is a view of it *(2026-11-06:
"maybe instead of closing the channel it should minimize?"; his picks in the
File Transfer 2 lab, `docs/design/mock/transfer2-lab.html`)*:

- **B with nothing moving** closes it, as before. The panel's legend says
  "B: close".
- **B while files move** asks, in three tiles as Computer Connected (11.18),
  heading **Files are still moving**, "Receiving Tobu Tobu Girl.gb" under the
  rule: **Keep going** (two arrows: the screen goes and the files keep moving
  behind the shelf), **Stop after this file** (a page with a tick: nothing
  new starts, the browser is told, and File Transfer closes once the file on
  its way is in) and **Cancel now** (a ring crossed: closed at once; the file
  that was half written is a `.part` file, deleted with its connection, and
  never shows on the shelf as a broken game). Under the tiles, what the
  focused one does; button hints (11.4) "< > Move   A Choose   B Keep Going".
  The legend over the panel says "B: keep going or stop", and "B: hide" while
  it closes after the file.
- **MENU** while files move keeps them going and goes to the shelf, as MENU
  goes there from anything.
- **Behind the shelf** it runs from every screen's loop, its mark is in the
  status corner (11.12), and MENU's row under its own name says "on",
  "receiving", "sending" or "closing"; A on it opens the screen again with the
  same PIN. The shelf reads the card again once arrived files have been still
  3 seconds. It closes by itself once the file the question let finish is in,
  when the hotspot stops or the cable has been out 10 seconds (with an upload
  paused, the link gone 10 minutes), and after half an hour with no browser
  asking anything.
- **An upload whose connection breaks** *(a Wi-Fi drop that lost a 517 MB
  disc whole, 2026-11-14)* is paused, not lost: its part stays on the card
  for the page to go on from (`GET /api/upload`, `src/hare.c`), the Now row
  says "paused: NAME" and the Download tile keeps its bar where the file
  stopped. For the 10 minutes the page waits for the handheld it counts as a
  file still moving (B asks, Auto Off waits); Cancel now drops it, and a part
  nobody goes on with goes after 30 minutes. The page says **Waiting for the
  handheld** with what is kept and **Stop sending**, then "Connection back.
  Going on from 58%.", or after 10 minutes with no answer **Try again**.
- **A game** while files move asks first, in the same tiles: **Not now**
  (back to the shelf), **Play after this file** (a wait on the news's page,
  11.35, "Finishing Tobu Tobu Girl.gb...", then the game) or **Cancel and
  play**. With nothing
  moving, File Transfer closes for the game and a one-line message says so
  (11.8): "File Transfer closed for the game." A game takes the loop that
  moves the files, and a port restarts the launcher, so the two never run
  together.

Host shots: `--xfer-ask [N] [game]`, tile N focused, and `--status '...
xfer=on'` or `xfer=62` for the corner's mark.

---

## 15. Where each part lives

A new screen is built from these parts by name and copies none of their
numbers. A part it needs that is not here goes into `src/ds/` first, with
its line in this table. `check-ds` (`tools/ds-check.py`) holds what the compiler
cannot: `src/ds/` includes nothing of `src/main.c`, and outside it no
foot is typed as a number (704, or the old 714, 720, 724), no Zyper at a page's (123, 168), and no tile's mark or
the tiles' style is read off a name.

| Part | Where | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| Tokens | `tools/designdef.py` → `src/design.h` | color, type, space, shape, elevation, motion and sound (sections 3 to 9). Diatom and portkeys carry copies of `design.h`. |
| Layout tokens | `tools/designdef.py` → `src/ds/layout.h` | where the launcher's own parts sit (section 5, Layout): `DS_PAGE_*`, `DS_TILE_*`, `DS_LIST_*`, `DS_BADGE*`, `DS_MARK_*`, `DS_HINT*` |
| Primitives | `src/ui.c`, `src/ui.h` | text and its faces, the rounded rect, the panel's slab (`ui_panel`), lift, scrim, the rail, button glyphs (`ui_button`, 11.9), the generated cards |
| Layout engine | `src/ds/flex.c`, `flex.h` | boxes laid out by rules (section 5, The system): `ds_flex_begin`, `ds_flex_add`, `ds_flex_solve`, `ds_flex_rect`; `check-flex` holds its rules |
| Stroke marks | `src/ds/glyph.c`, `glyph.h` | the 25 `ui_glyph` marks and their cache; `check-glyphs` holds each to 32 strokes and 4 arrowheads |
| Page frame | `src/ds/page.c`, `page.h` | `ds_page_frame_of`, one column of boxes on the layout engine (the heading's band **page-body-y** tall, the body with two tiles and a line of words **page-news-drop** under them, the line of marks **page-line-over** over the keys, the keys on the foot inset); the heading (`ds_page_head`: `DS_HEAD_BALANCED`, `DS_HEAD_TWO`, `DS_HEAD_SUB`); the line of marks over the keys (`ds_page_line`, its tones `DS_TONE_*`); a line of words under the tiles (`ds_page_meta`); the foot (`ds_page_foot`). `page_top` (`src/usbdrive_screen.c`) draws the ground and Zyper and hands back the frame. |
| Tiles and the list | `src/ds/tiles.c`, `tiles.h` | `ds_tiles_draw`: `DS_TILES_ROW`, `DS_TILES_GRID`, `DS_TILES_LIST`. In `src/main.c`, `tiles_show` takes the style as the panel's kind (`PANEL_TILES`, `PANEL_TILES_GRID`, `PANEL_LIST`) and each tile's mark. |
| The grid | `src/gamegrid.c`, `gamegrid.h`; the rule `src/gridrule.c`, `gridrule.h` | 11.47: the shape from the art, the cells, the covers in them, the band; `check-gridrule` |
| The focus | `src/ds/focus.c`, `focus.h`; its pixels `src/focusgeo.c`, `focusgeo.h` | 3.5's Highlight on the home and the shelves: `ds_focus_marks_of` (what a choice draws for a cover or a still), `ds_focus_tile` (the focused item grown on whole pixels, or on its way), `ds_focus_ring`, `ds_focus_ring_in`, `ds_focus_bar`, `ds_focus_hairline`, `ds_focus_dim`, `ds_focus_sheen`, `ds_round_ring`, the glide's weights (`ds_focus_weights`); `ui_lift_focus` in `src/ui.c`. `check-focusgeo` holds the pixels to the Pixel 2's filter. |
| Panel | `src/ds/panel.c`, `panel.h` | `ds_panel_open` / `ds_panel_close` (the slab and its lift, at rest or posed through the caller's `ds_pass`), `ds_panel_slab`, `ds_panel_rule`, `ds_panel_legend`: the menus, the tiles, the list, Player 2's panel, the hotspot |
| Marks | `src/ds/mark.c`, `mark.h` | `ds_badge`, `ds_switch` (a switch as a mark), `ds_play_disc` (a cover's video snap), `ds_more` (more rows above or below, `DS_MORE_W` by `DS_MORE_H`, 24 by 12), `ds_bar` and `ds_bar_in` (a bar of any size and colors; one rule for a small fill: never shorter than its own round end) |
| Distances | `src/ds/sdf.h` | the segment, polygon and rounded-box distances the distance-field marks share: the status corner, the cover badges |
| Button hints | `src/keyhints.c`, `src/keyhints_draw.c`; `page_hint` in `src/home.c` | 11.4 |
| Menu panel | `src/menu_panel.c` (its slab `ds_panel_open`, its more marks `ds_more`) | 11.3 |
| What a list's cursor shows | `src/ds/hover.c`, `hover.h` | 11.3: a list's `ds_hover` (`menu_choice.hover`) - `show(k)` the choice in memory, `undo(kept)` - run by `ds_hover_run`: a show once per choice the cursor comes to, the kept one back on the cursor's return and when the list closes, before A's choice is applied. The runner tells a list its cursor after the keys and before the frame (`menu_style.at`). `ds_hover_showing` holds what would write a shown state down. `check-hover` (its rules), `check-hover-sweep` (Theme's lists, nothing written). |
| A choice's picture | `src/ds/preview.c`, `preview.h` | 11.3: the column at an option list's left (`menu_pick_preview`): `ds_preview_layout` on the layout engine (the small screen a 4:3 leaf, **preview-note-gap**, the note's room in meta lines, the set's tallest), `ds_preview_screen_begin` / `ds_preview_screen_end` (the screen's ink, its **edge** frame of `DS_FRAME` and its corners `DS_RADIUS_ROW`, the focus fill's beside it), `ds_preview_note`; the column **preview-col-w**, to **preview-col-min** where the choices run long (`ds_preview_col_w`), **preview-gap** from the rows. The Display list's picture is `src/dmodepreview.c`, each mode placed as Diatom places it (`src/dmodefit.c`, `check-dmodefit`). |
| A row's scale | `src/ds/scale.c`, `scale.h` | 11.3: the graded bar a row carries as its value (`ds_scale_value`, `ds_scale_parse`), drawn by `menu_draw` in the page's scales' column (`ds_scale_draw`, `DS_SCALE_MIN_W`, `ds_scale_num_w`); `check-scale` holds the value's round trip and range and the knob's place in both themes. |
| A color chart | `src/ds/colorref.c`, `colorref.h` | 11.3: `ds_color_ref_draw`, the 24-patch chart, a grey ramp and the hues, tall in a column or wide in a list's picture. |
| Status corner | `src/status.c` | 11.12. Its marks keep drawings of their own, by decision (2026-11-14, round 2 of the design system): at 24 units, 15 pixels on the panel, each shows a state - a signal's lit arcs, a charge, a sweep - and is drawn for that size as a distance field, where `ui_glyph`'s are 56 to 72-unit marks for a choice. They share their distances (`src/ds/sdf.h`), not their shapes. |
| Zyper | `tools/zyperdef.py` → `src/zypermark.h`; `src/zyperdraw.c` | section 10 |
| Art | `res/` | the table below |

A page, as the Scraper's plan is built:

```c
ds_page_frame f = page_top(a, 1.0f);   /* the ground, Zyper, the frame */

ds_page_head(a->r, &f, DS_HEAD_SUB, title, plan_time, UI_GLYPH_CLOCK, 0);
/* the page's own content, from f.body_y between f.x0 and f.x1 */
ds_page_line(a->r, &f, items, 1);       /* marks and words over the keys */
ds_page_foot(a->r, &f, keys, words, 4, DS_PAGE_FOOT_GAP, DS_PAGE_FOOT_GAP_TIGHT);
```

A question in tiles says its style and each tile's mark; nothing reads a
name to decide either (`tile_glyph` did, and `tiles_are_list` took the
list's style from the first tile's mark, which cut "ScreenScraper"):

```c
static const int MARKS[3] = { UI_GLYPH_ARROWS, UI_GLYPH_FINISH, UI_GLYPH_CANCEL };

tiles_show(a, PANEL_TILES, heading, rows, n + 3, MARKS, 3, sel);
```

The art, where it comes from and what holds it:

| Set | Source | Made by | Read by | Held by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `res/system-icons` | `res/system-art/original/animations` | `make system-art` (`tools/systemart.py`, which writes `src/systemart_data.h` too) | `src/home.c`, Storage, File Transfer's page | `check-systemart` |
| Zyper's pictures | `tools/zyperdef.py` | `tools/genzyper.py`: `src/zypermark.h`, `res/readme/`, `res/web/*.svg` | `src/zyperdraw.c`, `src/noticeart.c` | `checkmark`, `check-zyper` |
| `res/fonts` | Lexend; Noto Sans SC and JP | as they come; `make cjk-font` | `ui_init`, `ui_fonts_for` (`DS_FONT_*`) | |
| `res/sounds` | `tools/designdef.py`'s voices | `make sounds` | `src/uisound_out.c` | `check-sounds` |
| `res/loading` | `tools/zyperdef.py` | `tools/genloading.py` | plastron's splash, once Zyper's loop lands (14) | `check-genloading`, by hand |
| `res/boot/first-start` | `tools/zyperdef.py`, `res/strings` | `tools/genfirststart.py` | plastron's splash | `check-genfirststart`, by hand (its text needs Pillow with Raqm) |
| `res/portmaster` pictures | `res/system-art/original/utility` | copies | PortMaster's install | `check-pmart` |
| `res/tour` | the launcher's own shots | `make tour-previews` | the tutorial | `check-tour-previews` |

Every system icon's source is in its catalogue and manifest with its
sha256 (`check-systemart`), and every one is ZyperOS's own: art that is not
ours to give never goes in, however well it would fit.

A change that moves a part and means to change nothing on the screen is
held to `make pixel-gate BASE=origin/main` (`tools/pixel-gate.py`): every
shot of `check-i18n-shots`, in every language and both themes, built at the
base and here and compared as pixels. A change meant to change the screen
shows the frames it changes in the before and after sheets it writes.

### 11.47 Power Button
*(Asked for on the Discord, 2026-11-15: "A long press on the power button
should be required to shut down. My Pixel shuts down after a momentary press,
which is too easy to do accidentally." My pick: a MENU setting.)*
**Power Button** in MENU's System tab, over Auto Off, the first of the rows
about the handheld going off: **Press**, the default on every card, POWER
pressed saves the game and turns the handheld off, as it always has; **Hold**,
POWER held for **hold-power**, one second (Tarik, 2026-11-17: "so make it 1
second"; the charging screen's Hold (POWER) to start is the same second,
11.25), with the pill of 11.6 filling while it is held. Two choices, so A switches it, as Left and Right do, heard as a ring.

One rule wherever POWER is read (src/powerhold.h): every screen of the
launcher, its watch over a game and over a program it waits for, and
portkeys over a port, so a hold is the same second whichever of them
reads it, and one begun in a game and ended in the in-game menu is still one
hold. What is not a turn-off stays a press: POWER starts an off handheld (the
power chip's), and during the charging screen's send-off a press starts
ZyperOS, as from off; the hold that starts ZyperOS from the charging screen
is taken by it: the turn-off's second is that screen's second too, and the
charging screen reads no turn-off, so it never turns it off. Holding
POWER about 10 s is still the power chip's own cut, for a handheld that does
not answer. With Hold on, Controls says "Hold: save and turn off" ("Hold:
turn off", "Hold: quit and turn off" over a port), and the tutorial's POWER
step says "Hold POWER to save and turn off." and lands on the hold.

### 11.48 Home Systems and Recents
*(Asked for on the Discord, 2026-11-15: "can we have the option to hide/reveal
ROM sections on the main menu? I'd like to hide the Ports menu". My picks: a
page of switches in MENU, and a new group, Recents, beside Favorites.)*

**Recents** is a group like Favorites: the ten games played last, from the
play time's sessions (src/stats.h, `stats_recent`), newest first, from every
system with games, on the home or turned off it. It sits right after Favorites, first when there is
none, and is named **Recents** in the language that is on. Its card is a
placeholder until a real design comes: a pixel-art clock with ZyperOS's lime
rim, its minute hand stepping between twelve and one in two poses of 400 ms,
as Favorites' heart beats (res/system-art/original/placeholders/recents.py).
Its shelf's order is **Last Played** as it ships, so the game just played is
first; it is built again when a game ends, and MENU > Sort By sorts it
otherwise, kept by its tag as Favorites' is. Its MENU is Favorites': Games,
Random Game, Sort By, View, and one more, Card Picture (below). With nothing
played yet there is no Recents card, as an empty Favorites shows none: a
card should lead somewhere.

**Card Picture** *(NextUI's top ten, and my pick in the Library lab,
2026-11-19: covers on a new card, "allow the user to pick between the two by
opening the menu in the recents page")*, the fifth row of Recents' MENU,
under View: **Cover**, as it ships, or **Last Screen**. Two choices, and A
opens them as a list all the same (11.3), the Recents game under the cursor
as each one shows it in the picture beside them and a sentence under it
("The screen where you stopped, the cover small in its corner."); Left and
Right turn it over, and the shelf under the menu turns with it. Kept in the
device's database, `recents.picture` ("screen"; absent is Cover).
**Last Screen** is each card the screen the game was left on: the picture
Diatom writes beside the resume point as the game ends (Quit, POWER, Auto
Off: `<rom>.auto.bmp`, the core's own frame), scaled up whole by the
nearest pixel toward 448 units high, its pixels kept sharp, with the
game's cover in its lower left corner, 30% of the card's height, inset 4%,
over a soft shadow (40% ink, down and right), its corners round by 5% of
its height; a game with no cover file has its generated card there, as its
shelf shows it (src/screencard.h). A card is as wide as its picture is:
a Game Boy Advance's 3:2, a SNES's 8:7. A game with no resume point shows
its cover. On the cover row, the Grid and the list alike. Nothing new is
saved and nothing runs while a game plays: the picture is the resume
point's own, and the card is made once on the art workers (texload's
`texload_want_screen`), never on the frame. It is art of no theme, so a
change of theme keeps it; it has no sheen or sharpen (8.2), which are a
generated card's. Host shots: `shelf-recents-screen`,
`shelf-recents-screen-grid`, `recents-menu-picture`, `recents-picture-list`
and `recents-picture-list-cover`, each game's resume picture made up by
tools/i18n-shots.py (`scene_bmp`).

**Home Systems** in MENU's Display tab, under System Order: its value
**All**, or how many of them are on ("8 of 10"); A opens its page. The page
lists Favorites, Recents and Collections (11.56; its row once the card has
a collection, as a system has one only with games), a rule, then every system
the card has games for, in the home's order (a system with none is not on the home, so not on
the page either), each **On** or **Off**, Left, Right or A turning it over,
heard as a ring, as Side Lights' parts are. Under a rule, the note: "Games
stay on the card." All of them On as they ship. A system turned off leaves
the home, and with it Random Game and Search; its games stay on Favorites,
Recents and Collections (11.56), start from there on the system's own core and display, and resume
there at the next start after a power-off (on Recents, else Favorites), as
they stay on the card and in File Transfer; its shelf comes back the moment it
is on again. *(Davistoa on the Discord, 2026-11-19, hides every system and
lives in Favorites; my OK.)* The systems turned off keep their listings after
the home's shelves (`app.parked`, `sys_kept`), so Favorites, Recents and
Collections resolve against them as against the home's; `make check-homesys-groups`
holds it on the host launcher. On a handheld without GPU frames (the Brick's profile today,
`plat_gpu_frames`), a system whose cores need them (config/systems.cfg's
`needs` lines: the Dreamcast, NAOMI, Atomiswave, the N64, the PSP and
Saturn) leaves the home the same way, and is no row of this page, since it
could not be turned on; its games stay on the card, in File Transfer and on
the Storage page. The last system on cannot be turned off, since a home with no system
is the empty home's, which says the card has no games: its row is dead, and a
second note says why ("One system stays on the home."). The home is built
again once, as the page closes on a change, from the saved library, keeping
the player's place (`shelves_reload`). The setting is one word in the device
database, `home.hidden`: the tags that are off ("PRT,RECENT"; Collections' is COLL),
so a new system is On without anyone saying so (src/homesys.h). Host shots:
`--homesys-page ROW`.


### 11.49 Achievements for offline
*(Asked for on the Discord, 2026-11-15: "being able to cache achievements in
advance vs having to load the game while on WiFi would greatly enhance RA
offline feature. Think of it like a scraper for achievements." So the page is
the Scraper's (11.19), in its look.)*

MENU > Downloads > **Achievements** (11.53; MENU > **Achievements** until
the Downloads row took its place) opens a page where A once went straight to the
sign-in keyboard; the sign-in is now behind **Y** (**A** while nobody is
signed in). Three states, as the Scraper's page:

**The plan.** The heading **Achievements**, under it about how long a
download takes (`about 3 min`), `Every game checked`, `Sign in to download`
or `needs Wi-Fi`. Each system with games RetroAchievements could know (a
console id in src/radl.c's table) is its icon over one tick, lit when every
game of it was asked about. Two tiles, Flash Drive's: **Ready Offline** (a
tick) with the games whose set is on the card and on how many systems, and
**To Download** (an arrow into a tray) with the games never asked and what
they will add to the card (`+14 MB`, 0.12 MB a game: a set and its badges for
the seven in ten RetroAchievements knows), `all checked` at none. Over the
keys the size to fetch (the card's room beside it only under 2 GB free) and
the games RetroAchievements has nothing for. Keys: A Download, Y Account, B
Close.

**The run** (src/radl.c, a step a frame). The game being asked about beside
Zyper; its system's icon and the bar, `214 / 553` games looked at. The body
is the set that came last, `Just added: Mega Man 2 · 82 achievements` (the
heading has moved on to the next game by then), its first twelve
badges in two rows of six, each landing 45 ms after the one before (420 ms,
from 80% and 24 px low). Over the keys the counts as marks (ready, new,
none) and the time left at the run's own pace; or, in **warning**, why it
waits: `Waiting for Wi-Fi`, `RetroAchievements asked for a pause · 0:42`
(a 429, counted down), `No answer · trying again in 0:12`; or
`Identifying games · 12 / 31` while a system's new files are hashed. B
is **Continue in background**: the page closes and the run goes on behind
the screens, as the Scraper's does (11.19), on a network slot of its own
beside it; Y stops, and the next download starts from what is missing.

**Done.** Titled by what happened: **All done** when the run ended on its
own; **Stopped** when B stopped it, the card filled or RetroAchievements
stopped answering, the reason under it (`Not enough room on the card`,
`RetroAchievements is not answering`); **Sign in again** when it refused the
account (`RetroAchievements refused the account`), and then A signs in and
goes back to the plan. A wall of the first badge of each set that came, two
rows of seven; the counts as marks; over the keys `393 games ready offline ·
57 earned on your account`. A Again, B Close. The side lights' **done** at a
run that ended well.

The "not checked yet" message (A on a game's Achievements row) names it:
"...or with Download in MENU > Downloads." Host shots: `--radl-page
STATE`, 0 to 14 (src/radl_screen.c).

### 11.50 The screens' shader
*(Asked for 2026-11-17 and 11-18: "the launcher shader menu setting is not
applying to the launcher view (only to games)"; the Shader row opened from
the home is for "the menus themselves", and from a system or a game it is
for that system or game.)*

**MENU > Display > Shader**, opened from the home, is ZyperOS's own screens'
shader: the home, the shelves, the menus, the pages, the charging screen,
every screen while no game is up. Its choices are **Off** and the four old
screens the handheld draws (CRT, CRT Curved, Scanlines, LCD Grid);
Sharp-Shimmerless sizes a game's pixels, and these screens have none to
size, so it is not offered. **Strength** under it, 10% to 100%, while it is
an old screen. Off and 100% to start with, so nothing changes for anybody who
never picks one. On a handheld whose GPU draws no old screen (the Brick)
both rows are gone from MENU.

**A system's menu and the in-game menu** set that system's shader, for its
games, as they did (9; shader.<TAG>). Up to 1.0.6 MENU's row set every
system's at once, kept as `shader`; that key stays what a system with no
choice of its own plays with, so every game looks as it did before the
update. The screens' own are new keys, `launcher_shader` and
`launcher_strength`, and start Off.

**What it draws** (src/screenfx.h). A game's shader redraws a few hundred
pixels as an old screen shows them; ZyperOS's screens are drawn at the
panel's own pixels, words and all, and drawn smaller they would blur. So
here the old screen is the glass: the frame as it is, times what Diatom's
own shader makes of a white 320x240 picture filling the panel (its
scanlines, its grille, its grid, its dark curved edge), and for CRT Curved
the frame bent by Diatom's curve as well. On the Pixel 2 each line of that
picture is two rows of the panel, its middle on a row (Diatom's own phase
there would light both rows alike, and the CRT would show no lines). The
words stay sharp. make check-screenfx holds the picture to Diatom's
arithmetic (tools/shaderdemo.c's port of its GLSL) at a game's size.

**What it costs.** The picture is made once a shader, strength and size,
on the CPU, into a texture of its own (writing into the one the last frame
drew with waits for the GPU): on the Pixel 2 14 to 28 ms, CRT Curved about
65 ms, 44 at the launcher's start, from tables so no pixel takes a cosine
or a root. The last six made are kept (1.2 MB each at 640x480, 7 MB at
most), so a list's cursor pays for each choice once, the first time it
lands on it, and never again while it stays among the six. Each frame shows it with one more textured pass at the panel's
size, a multiply; CRT Curved draws the frame through a 16 x 12 mesh in
place of the plain copy, its edge moved in onto the picture's (SDL 2
draws nothing for a mesh with a texture point outside it). Off is the
plain copy alone, exactly as before: no picture, no pass. Measured on the
Pixel 2 with CRT Curved, the costliest: the home in Spotlight, Gallery and
Grid and a shelf with art and with name cards all at the panel's 60, as
with Off; the GPU's clock up to 60 MHz higher while the screen moves, and
on a still screen the same 200 MHz as Off.

**While a game is up** (from its start to its end, the in-game menu and
what it opens, a port's menu) the screens' shader is off: what ZyperOS draws
then is over the game, whose own shader is its frame's. A video in the
player plays as it is.

**The list.** A on the row opens its choices, as Theme's lists (11.42): the
choice under the cursor is on the whole screen at once, the list and the
menu under it too, nothing saved until A, the kept one back when the list
closes. No picture column: the screen is the picture. One note under every
choice, what the row is for: "For the home and the menus. Each system has
its own." The Strength list the same, its step on the whole screen.

Host shots: `screens-crt-curved-home`, `screens-scanlines-shelf`,
`screens-lcd-menu`, and MENU's `menu-shader`, `menu-shader-list`,
`menu-strength` with the screens' shader on (tools/i18n-shots.py).

### 11.51 LCD No Backlight
*(Asked for on Discord, 2026-11-18: the Game Boy Color and the Game Boy
Advance had no backlight, so white was the screen itself, not a glow. An
idea from mattakins's Pixel Transparency for RetroArch; ZyperOS's shader is
its own, LCD Grid's with a step after its read.)*

**What it is.** A shader for the Game Boy's three alone (GB, GBC, GBA): their
LCD grid as the screen looked with nothing lit behind it. A light pixel let
the light through to the reflective backing, so a white background was that
backing's dull, slightly green grey (0.78, 0.80, 0.71), and the dark pixels
sat on it. Each pixel goes toward the backing by its luma squared, a soft
curve: white is the backing, black stays black, a dark grey moves a little.
The liquid crystal sits above the backing, so a dark pixel casts a shadow on
it, 0.4 of a game pixel down and to the right, 35% dark at most, with a
quarter-pixel soft edge and no blur; it shows only through light pixels.
LCD Grid's grid over all of it. Strength scales the backing's share, the
shadow and the grid together; 10% is close to Sharp-Shimmerless.

**Where it is offered.** SHADERS' `only` (src/app.h, `shader_for`): the
systems' rows (the system menu's Shader and the in-game Display tab's) list
it on those three shelves and their games, a Favorite's by its own system,
and nowhere else. Never ZyperOS's own screens (11.50): it changes what a
game's light pixels are, and the screens' white is the theme's. A card
that names it for another system, `shader.<TAG>` or the old `shader` key
(an edited database), gets that system's default instead: the old key's
where that is for it, else Off. `launcher_shader` naming it is Off. Off by
default everywhere, as every shader. The Pixel 2 only, as the old screens;
the Brick has no row for it.

**What it costs.** Diatom's `lcd-unlit` filter (diatom/port/pixel2.c): LCD
Grid's one texture read and its arithmetic, then one more read for the
shadow, a sharp-bilinear point at the texel up and left, and a few sums.
Two reads a pixel, where the others but the CRT take one; a Game Boy's
picture is small, so the GPU has room. Measured on the Pixel 2 (2026-11-18,
Metroid Fusion, a fresh start each, a minute of px2-perf, power medium):
LCD Grid 60.1 frames a second, the GPU at 300 MHz, the CPU at 790-797 MHz;
LCD No Backlight the same 60.1, 300 MHz and 772-814 MHz, no clock climbed
in either. With the GPU held at its lowest step, 200 MHz, both still show
60.1 and miss no refresh. tools/shaderdemo-device.sh puts the device's
picture within 1 level of tools/shaderdemo.c's (mean 0.27).

**Its picture.** The Shader list's picture of it (11.3, a choice with a
picture) is a Game Boy Color's 160x144 scene of ZyperOS's own on white,
at Original Shape's square pixels (tools/shaderdemo.c `draw_handheld`): the
same Zyper, score and ledge as the others' scene, which has almost no white
to change.

Host shots: `gamemenu-shader-lcd-unlit`, `gamemenu-shader-list-lcd-unlit`,
`system-menu-shader-list-gba`, and where it is not offered with a card that
names it, `system-menu-shader-list-snes` and `menu-shader-list-unlit`
(tools/i18n-shots.py).

### 11.52 On-Screen Stats: what FPS and Speed say
*(Found on 1.0.6: a Dreamcast game that runs at 30 fps showed 0.5x, at full
speed.)*

**FPS is the pictures.** The new pictures the game makes a second: the core's
video callbacks that brought one, over half a second of the game's time (the
menu's is left out), said twice a second. A repeat, a NULL frame, is not one.
A game that draws every second frame reads 30, and one that draws every third
reads 20. In fast forward it is what the core made, not what the panel showed:
2x of a game at 60 reads 120. A core that resends the same picture, not a NULL
frame, is counted at every call, since a picture cannot be told from the last
without a compare, which a frame does not pay for.

**Speed is the emulation.** The frames the core emulated a second, against the
frame rate it announces (`retro_get_system_av_info`): 1.00x at the game's own
speed, 0.67x for a core that gets two thirds of it done, the step in fast
forward (1.5x, 2x, 3x). It does not move with how often the game draws, so a
game at 30 fps at full speed is 30 fps and 1.00x, and shows no speed at all
(the line says Speed only when it is off 0.98x to 1.02x, or is the only item).

**What a frame is.** A core says a game draws less than it runs in one of two
ways. Its `retro_run` is one frame and the picture is repeated: the calls are
the frames. Or its `retro_run` runs until the game shows a new picture, so at
30 fps it is two frames long, and at 20 three: PPSSPP, Flycast,
ParallelN64's `fullspeed` (`pixel2_coreopts.c`). Such a core is called 30 times
a second at full speed, and counting the calls said 0.5x. A longer call has no
sign in the API, but it brings its frames' sound, and a core's sound is its
emulated time, on whichever thread it makes it: the frames of a window are the
sound it made, as frames, when that is more than its calls (`emumeter.h`).
Never fewer than the calls, so a core that made no sound (muted; Flycast's in
fast forward) is counted by its calls as before; a core too slow for its game
makes less sound and reads under 1x. The sound is judged over the window whole,
and a little over the calls, 5%, is the sound's pieces and not a longer call.

**The same in the now file.** `frames` in Diatom's now file (`now.h`), which
px2-perf turns into the game's speed against the clock, counts the frames
emulated, so it says what the overlay says.

**What it costs.** A frame pays two adds, the sound (relaxed and atomic, since
a core's thread makes sound too) and the pictures. A turn pays one compare. A
window, twice a second, a few multiplications. Off or on, nothing else.

Checked with the stub core, which can repeat every second picture
(`STUBCORE_DUPE`) or run two or three frames in a call (`STUBCORE_SPAN`):
`make check-hudspeed` (end to end, 1.00x and 30 fps for each, below 1x for a
slow core and the step in fast forward) and `make check-emumeter` (the rule).

### 11.53 Downloads
*(My picks in the downloads lab, 2026-11-19: one place for every download of
game data, its name **Downloads**, one door - the Scraper and Achievements
rows go - a list with Download on top, everything but videos in it from the
start, side by side, one stream, continue in the background and wait in a
game, the rest goes on when ScreenScraper's day runs out. 1.1.0's first page
was a MENU panel; I saw it on the Pixel 2 and it was not the design I picked,
so this is the lab's page, screen by screen.)*

MENU > Games > **Downloads**, where Scraper was, opens a page (the Flash
Drive pages' frame, src/ds/page.h; src/dl_screen.c): Zyper beside the
heading, a second line under it in **meta** **text-soft** with its mark - a
clock and `about 12 min`, the Wi-Fi mark and `Needs Wi-Fi`, a tick and
`Everything is here`, the arrow into a tray and `Downloading · 41%` - the
body, one line over the keys, the keys. A console's menu has the same page
for that console alone where Box Art was (its row is **Downloads**, its
value the console's count), its name as the heading.

**The hub.** A list in rows DS_LINE_MENU tall on the ground, the focus fill
(**accent**, **radius-row**) gliding over **quick**: **Download**, a rule,
then each kind with its mark - **Covers** (a cover), **Videos** (a screen and
a play mark), **Names and Details** (an open book), **Achievements** (a cup),
**Cheats** (a wand) -
its name in **menu** and its value in **meta** **text-dim**: `41 missing`,
`63 to fetch`, `199 to look up`, `150 to download`, `Complete`, `Ready
offline`, `Off` (the row in **text-dim**), `Needs ScreenScraper`, `Sign in
first`, and while its lane runs how far it has got (`37%`). Download's value
is the size and the time of every kind that is in, the lanes side by side so
the longer of the two (`186 MB · about 12 min`), `Nothing to fetch`, `Needs
Wi-Fi` or `Downloading · 41%`. Over the keys: on Download what it adds and
the card's room (`186 MB to fetch · 11.2 GB free`, in **warning** when it
would leave less than 200 MB, and then A says "Not enough room on the
card"), or `It all works offline now`; on a kind where it comes from and its
room (`150 MB on the card · libretro first`); with no network the Pixel 2's
"A Wi-Fi adapter or a phone by USB works too." or the Brick's "Connect in
MENU > Connections > Wi-Fi.". Keys: A Download (Open while a run goes on), Y
Systems, START Settings, B Close; on a kind A Open, `< >` On/Off, Y Systems,
START Settings, B Close. **LEFT** and **RIGHT** on a kind take it in or out of
Download (`dl.off.covers`, `dl.off.videos`, `dl.off.ach`; Names and Details is
the Scraper's own `scraper.names`) and its size and time change at once; in
with no system's switch on, every system's goes on (the lab's rule). The
cursor opens on Download, so A, A from MENU gets everything.

**Cheats**, the fifth kind (a wand): libretro's cheat list (src/cheatsdl.h),
on its own lane and network slot beside the other two (DLQ_CHT,
NET_SLOT_CHT). A run reads the last 64 KB of the buildbot's `cheats.zip`
(where its index is), then the 4.3 MB index once a rebuild (kept on the card
with its place and size), then only the entries that match the card's games,
by byte ranges, each inflated and checked against the index's CRC-32. A game
matches by its ROM's name, then that name with a code type after it, then
libretro's loose title rule and the same region. The files are the in-game
list's (11.66) contract: `Cheats/<Roms folder>/.libretro/<ROM base>/<the
entry's name>` written as .part and renamed, `.none` for a game libretro has
none for, your own `Cheats/<Roms folder>/<ROM base>.cht` never touched. Its
row says `640 to check` or `Ready offline`; its page Ready Offline and To
Download, `From libretro-database · CC BY-SA 4.0` or `CC BY-SA 4.0 · 19 games
have none`; Systems has its column (`dl.off.cheats.<folder>`), a game its
mark and its panel row; On the Card deletes the downloaded ones (and the
in-game list's switches: cheats that were on turn off). In Download from the
start (my pick: everything but videos). An update: Download runs the lane at
least once a day while some games have cheats, and a game's files come again
only when their checksums changed. `cheats_dl_one_begin` and `_step` get one
game's, for the in-game list's empty state.

**Loading.** The page draws at once. Its counts (src/dlcount.h) are the
Scraper's and the achievements download's own, a system at a time, at most
DL_COUNT_MS of a frame: until the first count ends the second line and every
value say `Counting…`; after it, the last count shows at once and a new one
goes behind it. The achievements count reads each system's .cheevos folder
once (radl_plan_one) instead of looking for a game's set and its .none one by
one, which on a FAT card walked the folder for every game. The switches are
read when the totals are made, so Left and Right never count again. The log
says how long a count took (`downloads: counted 12 systems in 840 ms`).

**A kind's page** (A on Covers, Videos, Names and Details): the kind's name
as the heading, the time under it; each system's icon over a tick (lit when
it lacks nothing, the icon dim when the system's switch is off; nine at
most, or eight and `+N`); two **raised** tiles, what the card has (On the
Card, Named) and what a download adds (Missing, To Download, To Look Up) with
its size; over the keys what it adds and from where. A downloads that kind
alone, X is Videos for (Videos), Y Systems, B Back. **Achievements** opens
1.0.6's page (11.49), its B Back.

**Systems** (Y): a row per system (its icon and name), a column per kind
under the kind's mark: the count missing in **text**, a tick in
**accent-ink**, a dash in **text-dim** when it is off. A ring of
**accent-ink** on the cell; over the keys the cell in words (`NES · Names and
Details · 30 to look up`). Up and Down go round, Left and Right stop at the
ends. A switches the cell (covers `scraper.covers.<folder>`, videos
`snaps.get.<folder>`, names `dl.names.<folder>` - the covers switch until
set apart -, achievements `dl.off.ach.<folder>`); X deletes the system's
videos or achievement sets after a question; Y opens its games.

**A system's games** (Y on a row): the system's name, `96 games`, a list of
its games with a mark per kind at its right - lit (on the card), dim
(missing), struck (RetroAchievements has none) - and under it what the game
has and lacks. A opens **the game's panel** (11.3): Cover, Video, Names and
Details, Achievements, Cheats with their values (a video not on the card is
`None`), and **Get What's Missing** (no rule: six rows fill a panel). A on
Cover or Video opens its Cover and Video page (11.34); A on Names or
Achievements gets them, and Get What's Missing everything it lacks, in the
panel, each value `Getting…` until its file lands: names with the cover and
the video in one ScreenScraper request, the set by the game's hash as a
first play gets it.

**Settings** (START), a panel: **ScreenScraper**, **RetroAchievements**,
**SteamGridDB** with their accounts, **Video Snaps** (11.19's page),
**Names and Details** (On or Off), **On the Card** (each kind's room; A on
Videos or Achievements deletes them all after a question, cursor on Cancel;
Covers and Names say why they are not deleted here). RetroAchievements' page
says who is signed in, signs in again, and **Sign Out** after a question:
earned achievements stay on the account, unlocks still waiting stay in their
queue for the next sign-in (there was no sign-out before).

**The run**, one stream for every lane (src/dlq.h): the game being asked
about as the heading (the art lane's, else the achievements lane's); under
it its system's icon, the kind's mark, the source's mark (a cloud
ScreenScraper, books on a shelf libretro, a grid SteamGridDB, the cup
RetroAchievements), the bar and `214 / 573`. What came last lands big at the
left over 520 ms while the six before it move one place along (the
Scraper's run, 11.19): each is the game's cover, or its card with no art (11.1's
one name treatment, painted on a thread of its own) when it has none, with a
lime corner mark for what came - a play mark, the
book, the cup and the set's count. Over the keys: the counts per kind as
marks and the time left beside a clock, or in **warning** why it waits -
`Waiting for Wi-Fi`, `RetroAchievements asked for a pause · 0:42`. B
Continue in background (the page closes, the corner's ring shows it, MENU's
row says `Downloading · 41%`), Y Stop. **Done**: **All done**, **Stopped**
(why under it) or **Sign in again**; the wall of what came (fourteen at
most), the counts, and over the keys `It all works offline now` or `The
next download starts from what is missing`. A Again (from what is missing,
once the card is counted), B Close. A run that ends well behind the screens
says so in Zyper's bubble on the shelf: `Downloads done`, `41 covers · 150
achievement sets`.

The top bar's download mark (11.12) is the row's whole 24: its ring and its
arrow take the battery outline's 3-unit stroke and its ring is whole in
**text-dim**, swept in **accent-ink**, so it stands as tall and as heavy as
the fan and the battery.

Host shots: `--downloads STATE`, 0 the hub, 1 a run going on, 2 everything
done, 3 no network and nobody signed in, 4 Settings, 5 counting, 6 on
Covers, 7 Covers out, 8 to 10 the kinds' pages, 11 Systems, 12 a system's
games, 13 a game's panel, 14 the run, 15 waiting for Wi-Fi, 16 paused, 17
done, 18 stopped, 19 On the Card, 20 RetroAchievements' account, 21 a
console's page, 22 Videos for, 23 Cheats' page.

### 11.54 Rumble
*(Asked for on the Discord after 1.0.6: turn the motor down or off. My picks
in the Sound lab, 2026-11-19: four levels, Low 35% and Medium 65% of what a
game asks, a row in MENU and in a game's Controls tab.)*

**The levels.** **Off**, **Low**, **Medium**, **Full**: none, 35% and 65% of
the strength a game asks the motor for, and all of it, as ZyperOS always ran
it. A new card is Full, so nobody who leaves the row alone feels a change.
The level multiplies a system's own share (systems.cfg's `rumble|` lines:
Doom's 35%, whose every shot rumbled), so Low on Doom is 12%; a level above
Off never rounds to nothing (src/rumble.h, `rumble_run_pct`).

**MENU > Sound > Rumble**, the Sound tab's last row, under Menu Music: the
level for every game. Left and Right step it and stop at Off and Full,
heard as Auto Off is (move when it moved, edge when it did not); A opens the
four as a list with a note under it: "No rumble at all.", "35% of what a
game asks.", "65% of what a game asks." or "As strong as each game asks."
(one sentence each: the note has two lines, and German's and French's
second sentence did not fit). **Each step is felt**: the motor buzzes
once, 160 ms, at the level it comes to, and in the list the level under the
cursor buzzes as the cursor comes onto it (Off is still). Only on a handheld
with a motor ZyperOS runs (the profile's `rumble`): the Pixel 2. The Brick's
motor (GPIO 227 on the axp2202's bldo3 rail) waits for its tester's first
buzz; its profile turns the rows on with it.

**In a game, the Controls tab's Rumble row**, under the Pak: only for a game
whose core asked for the motor (Diatom answers PAUSED with `rumble=1`), on a
handheld with one. It is the level of what Applies To shows, This Game or
the system; a game without its own follows its system's, and a system
without its own follows MENU's. A value the scope set itself is in the
**accent-ink**, as a changed button is. A opens "Rumble: This Game" (or the
system's name): the four levels, the tick on the scope's and **Default** on
what it falls back to (MENU's, or the system's for a game that has one);
choosing that one takes the scope's own level away. Left and Right step it
as in MENU. Each change reaches the paused game at once (`SETRUMBLE`) and
buzzes once at what the game will feel, its system's share included.

**What it costs.** Nothing a frame: a level is one number Diatom multiplies
by when a core changes its motor. The port is handed what the core asks and
a 0 to 1 scale beside it (`diatom_port_rumble`): the Pixel 2's force
feedback runs at the product; a motor whose strength is a voltage, as the
Brick's, takes the scale as its step.

**Kept**: `rumble` in the device's database (MENU's; no key at Full),
`rumble.<TAG>` and `rumble.<TAG>.<file>` in the library's, each gone when it
would equal what it falls back to; Rename and Delete Game move and remove a
game's with its file. Host shots: `menu-rumble`, `menu-rumble-list` (and
`-low`, `-off`: the list opened on those levels, with their notes),
`gamemenu-rumble`, `gamemenu-rumble-list`, `gamemenu-rumble-own-list`
(tools/i18n-shots.py; `--gm-rumble` stands in for the core's asking).

### 11.55 Your PICO-8

My picks in the native PICO-8 lab (`~/Documents/ZyperOS/labs/pico8-native-lab.html`,
2026-11-19), from a player's ask on Discord: Lexaloffle's own PICO-8, for every
cart's full compatibility and for Splore, its online cart browser.

**Whose it is.** PICO-8 is Lexaloffle's to sell, and its license lets no one
pass it on. ZyperOS never ships it, never downloads it and keeps no copy of it:
the player brings the PICO-8 for Raspberry Pi zip they bought
(`pico-8_0.2.7_raspi.zip`). Two of its files run here, `pico8_64` (the 64-bit
ARM program) and `pico8.dat` beside it, in `Bios/PICO-8` (src/pico8.h). Its
words never call it ZyperOS's: **Your PICO-8**.

**Adding it.** ZyperOS Installer's **PICO-8** group, under Card: optional, the
zip chosen or dropped on the window. **Update** and **Fresh install** carry it,
and **What to do** gets **Add PICO-8**, for a card that has started: the two
files onto its ZYPEROS volume, no system written, no password asked. On a card
that never started, the files ride in the image's boot partition and its first
start moves them (installer/README.md). By hand, the zip as it comes or its two
files into `Bios/PICO-8`: the launcher takes the files out of a zip at its next
start or Rescan and says so in a one-line message (11.8): "Your PICO-8 0.2.7 is
ready.", or "… is not the PICO-8 for Raspberry Pi zip." for any other zip.

**The player.** `config/systems.cfg`'s PICO-8 row lists two cores, your PICO-8
first (`run:ZyperOS/pico8/PICO-8.sh`) and fake08 after it, and its `brings`
line names the two files: the core counts as on the card only with both there.
So once they are, carts open in it, and the shelf's **Core** row offers it
beside fake08; before, the row names fake08 alone, as today. *(2026-11-19:
adding it is the choice.)* It runs as OpenBOR does, a program of its own:
the loading screen (11.11) until it draws, **MENU** ZyperOS's menu over it
(Continue, Controls, Quit, **X** Quit from any row, 11.28), its Controls pages
the PICO-8 shelf's buttons, **START** PICO-8's own pause menu, **MENU+START**
held a second home even when it hangs, the volume keys and **MENU+VOL** over it,
**POWER** its end and the power-off. No save states: PICO-8 has none, so Save,
Load, Undo Load and Resume stay fake08's. Full clocks, as every program of its
own.

**The picture** follows the shelf's **Display** row (launch.sh hands the
row's name to the script): **Sharp Pixels** is whole multiples of 128, 3x on
the Pixel 2 (384 x 384); every other mode is the panel's full height
(480 x 480), its pixels 3 or 4 of the panel's. On the Brick 6x is its full
height (768 x 768).

**A cart's own saves** are one set for both players: fake08's `Saves/cdata`,
bound over PICO-8's `cdata` for the run. PICO-8's settings, Splore's downloads
and its favourites stay in `Saves/PICO-8`. *(Favourites stay in Splore.)*

**Splore** is the PICO-8 shelf's first entry, pinned whatever the sort, as
PortMaster's is on the Ports shelf (11.33): its own art, ZyperOS's, a lens over
a row of carts on a desk in PICO-8's colors, the band of PICO-8's color across
the top, the sun in Beard and the moon in Salt & Pepper (`res/pico8`,
`tools/genpico8-art.py`). It is never a game: not counted in the shelf's
**Games**, never a Random Game, and A says **Open**, not Play. Before your
PICO-8 is on the card it is there all the same (*my pick: the entry says how,
where a player looks*), its line **Not installed** and A **How to Add**, which
opens a page in System Update's form (11.17): heading **Your PICO-8**, "PICO-8"
where the versions go, "By Lexaloffle · not on this card" under it, three
points (what it adds, whose it is, how to add it), and **Back**. With no way
out to the internet and a radio to turn on, A asks first: **Splore needs
Wi-Fi**, "Wi-Fi is off. Offline, Splore has only the carts it downloaded
before.", **Turn On Wi-Fi** (on, and the saved network joined, up to 15 s) or
**Open Offline**; B leaves it.

**Splore's downloads.** PICO-8 for Raspberry Pi has no HTTP of its own: it
runs `wget` for every list and cart. The system's wget is busybox's, which has
no HTTPS, and every cart is HTTPS, so the lists came (plain HTTP) and no cart
did: "could not connect to bbs" (device test, 2026-11-19). The script puts
ZyperOS's own `wget` first on PICO-8's PATH (`ZyperOS/pico8/bin/wget`,
`sd/pixel2/PICO-8-wget.sh`), which hands Splore's calls to the launcher's curl,
the certificates checked against ZyperOS's bundle, a file there whole or not at
all. No change to the system image.

**The Brick** gets your PICO-8 with its ports (the parity plan's item 8):
until then its script is not in the Brick's payload, so the Brick's PICO-8
shelf has no Splore and plays on fake08.

Host shots: `make pico8-shots` (the shelf before and after, the Core row, the
page in both themes). The rules: `make check-pico8`, `check-config`,
`check-controls`.

### 11.56 Collections
*(NextUI's top ten, and my picks in the Library lab, 2026-11-19: "Add to
Collection" on a game, a group on the home after Favorites and Recents once
there is one collection, Home Systems can hide it, and Delete Collection
asks first.)*

**On the card.** A collection is a list of games of your own, from every
system: `Collections/<name>.txt` at the card's root, one game a line, its
path from the card's root ("/Roms/SNES/Chrono Trigger (USA).sfc"), the plain
format MinUI and NextUI write. A list written by hand, or on another
handheld, shows up too: a line is read as "/Roms/...", "Roms/...", "GBA/..."
(from Roms/ itself), with a card's mount before /Roms/ or \ for /; a line
starting with # and an empty line are kept and are no game. A line is kept
as it was written: a game off the card today keeps its line, and a list is
rewritten only when ZyperOS changes it (src/collect.h). The lists are read
at each scan, so one copied onto the card shows after a rescan as a game
does. 36 collections at most, A to Z by name; 2000 lines in one.

**Add to Collection**, a row of a game's details page (X on the shelf), right
after Favorite, on every game a favorite can be: its value how many
collections the game is in ("In 2"), none when it is in none. A opens its
list under the heading Add to Collection: a row for each collection, A to
Z, the ones the game is in ticked (11.3's tick); A puts it in, heard as the
**reward**, or takes it out, heard as **back**, written through at once.
Under a rule, **New Collection** opens the keyboard (11.5) for a name, the
game its first; the cursor lands on it, ticked. The keyboard opens with
Shift on for the name's first letter, as a phone's does, and goes back to
the small letters once one is typed (a Shift chosen with X stays, as
always; 2026-11-19: "Name input should have the keyboard start with shift on"). A name a card's file cannot
have, one too long or one another collection has (in any case) is said, and
the keyboard comes back on what was typed, as Rename's (11.41).

**Collections on the home**, a group like Favorites and Recents, after them
(first when there are neither), once a collection has a game on the
shelves: a collection whose games are all off the card is not there, as a
folder with no game is left out (11.39). A game of a system turned off the
home stays in its collections, as it stays on Favorites and Recents (11.48). Its card is a placeholder until a real design comes: a stack of
three cartridges, the top one's label lime, lifted off the stack and put
back in two poses of 400 ms each, as Favorites' heart beats
(`res/system-art/original/placeholders/collections-card.py`). Under its
name, its games, each once however many lists name it. Home Systems (11.48)
can hide it, its row there once the card has a collection; it is On as it
ships.

**Its shelf** is one folder deep. At its top, each collection is a folder
(11.39's card, in the group's lime, or Folder Color's, 3.5), its count on its
label, A to Z; under the row its name and "3 games". A opens one, on its
first game; the heading names the way down, "Collections / Couch Co-op",
and B goes back to the top, on the collection it left. In a collection its
games are of every system, each with its system under its name as on
Favorites, launched by its own system's core; the cover row, the Grid and
the List all show it (View is the shelf's, kept by its tag, COLL). X and Y
on a collection have nothing to do and play **deny**.

**Its menu** is headed by the collection's name: on the top, the one under
the cursor's; in one, its own. On the top: **Sort By**, **View**,
**Rename** and **Delete Collection**. In one: **Games** (its count),
**Random Game** (one of its games), Sort By, View, Rename, **Remove from
Collection** (the game under the cursor out of it; it stays on the card)
and Delete Collection. Sort By is the shelf's, one for every collection,
